docs/engineering/systems/rendering-streaming.md
Selected excerpts for the project portfolio.

# Rendering and streaming

## Terrain drawn near to far, November 14, 2026 (0.6.154-test)

The benchmark pond's extra GPU time ([results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-14/terrain-draw-order/results.md)). Render order only: the picture, rules and saves are unchanged.
- **The cause.** HDRP leaves front-to-back sorting out of its opaque order, and the game draws without a full depth prepass. The GBuffer therefore shades ground that nearer ground then hides, and how much depends on where the eye looks. From the benchmark's pond bank it looks west across the island: 645 of the 672 distant terrain blocks are in view (8.8 million triangles), against 131 on the open-snow walk. The pond itself is 9,018 triangles.
- **The change.** `PolarWorldStreamer` gives each terrain renderer a sorting order of 1 plus its metres from the player (`Rank`), when the chunk is made and again once the player has moved 8 m (`OrderTerrain`). Opaque draws sort by sorting order before material, so terrain is drawn near to far and after everything else, which keeps order 0: a hut's walls still hide the ground behind them.
- **Result.** On the release benchmark at 2560 by 1440, High: the pond's GPU time is 0.3 to 0.5 ms lower and the coast's about 0.35 ms lower; open snow, the hut and the blizzard are unchanged. The pond's P95 is 17.03 and 17.06 ms (17.13 and 17.42 before), still over 16.67 ms.
- **Tried and left out.** A full depth prepass costs more than it saves (+0.85 ms on open snow, +1.04 ms at the pond). Ordering every renderer by distance is slower than no order (+0.2 ms).
- **What is left.** Looking inland, about 2 ms of the GBuffer is vertex work on distant terrain (about 0.2 ms a million triangles in view). The blocks' resolution rings (`PolarStreamingPolicy.InitialResolution`: 256 within 6 km, 128 within 16 km) date from larger worlds; lowering them is a visible trade-off and was not changed.

## Intact ice from the benchmark bank (November 14, 2026)

Disabling only the intact caps' GBuffer pass on the same pond revealed the underlying water and removed a median 55 draws. The GBuffer pass saved 0.189 ms (GI Medium) and 0.191 ms (GI Off); the whole-GPU savings were 0.116 and 0.132 ms. These are fixed-bank paired measurements with slot 1 and the other passes active. [Cap experiment, per-frame data and captures](../logs/evidence/2026-11-14/pond-cap-cost/results.md).

Each close intact plate has a cap and underside submesh. A capture of plate 54 identified separate GBuffer draws with material parameter values 0 and 4. Suppressing only the underside GBuffer pass on the 55 plates of seed 777's benchmark pond saved 0.29 ms on the CPU render thread and 0.02 ms on the GPU in the median pair. The six GBuffer GPU differences straddled zero. The underwater material and its passes remain in use. [Frames, images and diagnostic](../logs/evidence/2026-11-14/pond-underside-cost/results.md).

## The handbook's first opening, November 13, 2026 (0.6.153-test)

At the user's word ([results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-13/handbook-warm-up/results.md)).
- **The warm-up.** `MvpExplorerHandbook.Warm`, called by `PolarMvpController.Preload` at the end of loading, still builds the canvas and reads `HandheldHandbook/Book`.
  - It then adds a `MvpHandbookBookView` of its own and runs `Create(Camera.main, …)` and `SetSpread(Spread)` on it, as `Show` does, with the handbook's canvas on.
  - `Dispose` and `Destroy` follow in the same call, and the canvas goes off again, so nothing is drawn: the world camera's mask and hidden renderers are back before the frame renders.
  - `MvpHandbookBookView.Quiet` mutes the view's AudioSource, so the opening sound plays silently and stops with the view.
- **What it covers.** Everything the first opening did for the first time: the book's foreground materials (`PolarHdrpBookMaterials.Dress`), the two cameras and HDRP's first render of the paper camera, the page materials, the open spread's pages with their photos (kept in the handbook's photo cache) and glyphs, the opening sound's set, the first layout of the handbook's controls (the pages' `Canvas.ForceUpdateCanvases`) and the compiled code.
- **The first opening now.** With the machine quiet it costs about what a later one does: 17.3 and 17.8 ms in the call, against 12.4 and 12.5 for the second. Left: about 5.8 ms of `Mono.JIT` in its frame, the opening's own controls (`OpenHandbook`, `Show`, `UpdatePresentation`, `LateUpdate`) and the timing code.
- **Timing it.** Time the opening only while nothing else fills memory. Runs while the ChatGPT desktop app's git diffs ran (up to 13 at once, free memory 0.1 to 3 GB) gave 42 to 198 ms for the same first opening.
- **Test code.**
  - The `-polarHandbookTiming` line counts the garbage collections in the call.
  - With `-polarProfilerLogs` the openings write their own profiler log (`handbook-<n>.raw`, started ten frames before the first), which `PolarFrameTimeReport` reads as the part "handbook".

## The pack's leftover assets, November 13, 2026 (0.6.152-test)

At the user's word ([results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-13/pack-leftovers/results.md)).
- **Pavement models.** `rock01`, `rock02` and `rock03` under `Resources/Polar/HDRP/Environment/SourceModels` map their material to `Polar/Derived/TundraLichenRock` (their `.fbx.meta`), as `Rock3` and `Rock5` have since 0.6.134-test. `PolarLandscapeScatter.Parts` draws them with the pavement material it is given and reads the model's own only to skip a missing one, so the mapped material must stay set. The pack's `Rocks` material (13.9 MB with its maps) no longer loads.
- **The pack's surface system.** `PolarPackManagers.Seed` also sets `DataDefinition<SurfaceDefinition>._definitions` to an empty array and an empty `SurfaceManager`, at Unity's first start-up stage, before the pack's loader. The pack's surface definitions (impact particles, decals, footstep and hit sounds; 12.0 MB in play, 25.1 MB on the front page) and the manager's asset (4.9 MB) are never read. Only the pack's own components ask for a surface (footsteps, impact handlers, firearm and melee impacts, sticky projectiles); one added to a game asset would find none.
- **Left as they are, at the user's word.** The sky's reflection at 256 px, the world drawn behind the front page, terrain tangents at half precision and the pack's share of the package on disk.

## The memory work's last items, November 13, 2026 (0.6.151-test)

Done at the user's word before sleeping: every item of list A of the [unmeasured findings](../logs/evidence/2026-11-13/unmeasured-findings/results.md), with decals off and the handbook's warm-up ([results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-13/memory-work/results.md)).
- **The sky's lighting.** `PolarHdrpPresentation.LateUpdate` sets the physically based sky to `EnvironmentUpdateMode.OnDemand` while an expedition is in play (`game.Active`), the eye is outdoors (`indoor<=0`) and the handbook is closed. Otherwise it is `OnChanged`, which `Attach` starts it at.
  - **Requests.** `skyUpdateForMain` asks HDRP to redraw (`RequestSkyEnvironmentUpdate`, from the world camera's begin-rendering) each game minute (`hour` a quarter of a degree), when the storm moves 0.005, the sun light's colour times intensity 0.005 stop (`lightStops`) or the sky's radiance 0.05 stop, again 0.2 s after each, and every 2 s.
  - **Why the light's term.** Exposure is fixed each frame from the light, and the sky's light goes as the sun light's colour times intensity. A stale sky lighting shows as the light's whole change since the last redraw: with requests from the hour alone the dawn screen stepped up to 29% a frame.
  - **Why each game minute.** After sunset the twilight sky dims with the hour while the night's light holds still. With requests every 2 game minutes, the snow in the lower quarter of the screen stepped by 3.5% after 22:00.
  - **Why not indoors.** The hut's reflection probe (`HutProbe`, realtime on demand) and the handbook's camera render their own sky contexts, which a request from the world camera never refreshes. A hut on demand drew white at dawn.
  - **What it costs in memory.** Nothing while the frame rate is held. Uncapped, the build holds about 95 MiB more private memory on average than the same build with the sky as before (`mo-03-changed`), outside Unity's counts and video memory, with equal peaks; it runs about 23% more frames a second there. The sky's own textures are not the cause, and the driver's share was not traced. Off is one line (`onDemand=false`).
- **The coast's lists.** `PolarCoast.Upload` drops a piece's finished build task (its vertex and index lists) once its meshes are made. `OnDestroy` skips a piece whose task is still running, as before.
- **The terrain window's margin.** `PolarWorldStreamer` moves the near window only once the focus is a whole route cell (9.86 m) past the edge of its centre chunk (`Outside`, in lattice cells, the ring's columns wrapped). Before, it moved as soon as the focus was in another chunk. Within the margin the player is still deep inside the window, which reaches two chunks (158 m) past the centre chunk each way.
- **Decals off.** `supportDecals` is 0 in `PolarPipeline.asset`: the game has no decal projectors (the pond's rings are a water decal, under `supportWaterDecals`), and HDRP no longer keeps the decal atlas and buffers: Unity's reserved memory is 73 to 89 MiB lower in play. A decal projector needs it on again.
- **The handbook's warm-up.** `PolarMvpController.Preload` (the last step of loading) calls `MvpExplorerHandbook.Warm`. It builds the handbook's canvas and reads the book's assets (`HandheldHandbook/Book`: the prefab with the arms, clips, sounds and page material), so the first opening no longer reads them from disk. `Preloaded` requires it, and `DropExpedition` lets the assets go (`Cool`).
- **DLSS's runtime, looked into and left.** HDRP 17.3 creates the NVIDIA device whenever its pipeline asset loads or its pipeline is built (`HDDynamicResolutionPlatformCapabilities.SetupFeatures`, `DLSSPass.SetupFeature`). It does so on any NVIDIA card with Unity's NVIDIA plugin, whatever the anti-aliasing. A trial build shut NGX down afterwards through the module's own `GraphicsDevice.Shutdown`, and that freed nothing: private memory went from 834 to 834 MiB and from 976 to 976 MiB at a start's two shutdowns. The 87 MB is NVAPI's and CUDA's, which stay loaded. Avoiding it would need HDRP's package changed, or the NVIDIA plugin left out of the build (no DLSS). Neither was done, and the trial was not kept.
- **Test options.**
  - `-polarSkySteps <folder>` (`PolarSkySteps`, Development builds with `-polarSaveRate`) runs five stretches: dawn and dusk outdoors, a clear noon, a blend into a blizzard, and a dawn inside the home hut. For each frame it writes the mean screen luminance, four screen bands, the exposure and HDRP's sky renders (`steps.csv`), and it takes a still of each stretch.
  - `PolarWindowShuttle` adds near legs, to and fro within 6 m of a chunk line.

## The sky's update, the coast's lists, the terrain window and untracked memory, November 13, 2026 (measured, no package)

Measured on `mf-01-dev` ([results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-13/unmeasured-findings/results.md)). Nothing changes in play.
- **The sky.** `PolarHdrpPresentation.Attach` sets the physically based sky to `EnvironmentUpdateMode.OnChanged`. HDRP then renders the sky cubemap, its convolution and the ambient probe whenever the sky's hash changes, which the moving sun and the storm blends make most frames: 0.43 to 1.11 ms of GPU a frame. The game's own requests (`skyUpdateForMain`: every 2 s, on the hour, storm or radiance thresholds, again 0.2 s after) are what `OnDemand` would use; `-polarSkyOnDemand` tries it.
- **The coast.** `PolarCoast` keeps each piece's finished build task with its vertex and index lists after upload; only the loose plates and shore are read later (2.81 MiB at the coast).
- **The terrain window.** The near window moves as soon as the player's cell is in another chunk, with no margin. A crossing builds and releases a row of 5 chunks and recuts 4 distant blocks.
- **Untracked memory.** On Direct3D 11 the video memory manager commits a backing for each GPU allocation into the game's private memory. Unity's own resources cost about 300 MB more there than Unity's graphics estimate. NVIDIA's driver heaps take 188 MB, and DLSS's runtime 87 MB: HDRP's check for DLSS at start loads NGX, NVAPI and CUDA on an NVIDIA card.
- **Test options.** `-polarSkyProbe <folder>` (`PolarFindingsProbe`: HDRP's sky samplers per frame in `sky.csv`, the coast's kept and needed bytes once a second in `coast.csv`); `-polarWindowShuttle <report.json>` with `-polarSaveRate` (`PolarWindowShuttle`).

## Saves and the garbage collector's lock, November 12 to 13, 2026 (measured, no package)

A save stalled all three of its threads together for 5.5 ms in 0.6.150-test's near walk day ([results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-12/save-gc-lock/results.md)). Nothing changes in play; this records how Unity's collector meets a save.
- **The collector.** The game's Players run Mono with incremental collection on.
  - **One lock.** On Windows, Unity's Mono collector (Boehm, bdwgc 7.7) has one lock for every managed allocation on any thread.
  - **What an allocation does under it.** An allocation over 2 KB, or a small one whose free list runs empty, calls into the collector under that lock. With no cycle running, that call only checks whether to start one. While a cycle runs, it marks.
  - **A cycle.** Its first stop of the world has a time limit. If marking does not finish in it, allocating threads and the frame-end `CollectIncremental` mark under the lock with the world running. An untimed stop then finishes the cycle, and the sweep is readied under the lock after it. Every 20th cycle also sweeps and clears the marks first.
  - **The profiler** shows stops as `GC.Collect`, and work inside an allocation only as a gap before that allocation's sample.
- **A save meets it.** A save allocates densely on three threads at once.
  - In these runs a cycle ends every 60 to 70 s, and its stops fall in its last one to four frames, so for nearly all the time no cycle runs.
  - A save's burst of about 0.8 MB starts over half of the cycles (26 of 45 in the profiles).
  - **At a cycle's end.** A save waits its turn at the lock, 1 to 3.4 ms at a time in the profiles.
  - **The big stalls.** These were 5 to 6 ms, in one or two saves a day, measured only with the profiler.
    - No cycle was running, but all three came in the last 8.1 s before one.
    - The leading explanation is a wait on the lock. What held it, and whether the profiler's allocation hook had a part, is not known.
  - **Frame time.** Neither has made a frame over 25 ms at a 60 fps limit. With no frame limit, the game's default, the frame carries the pause; that was not measured.
- **Test option.** `-polarGcProbe` (`polar_save_rate.py --gc-probe`) starts two threads:
  - `PolarSaveRateRun.Spin` never allocates and logs its pauses and `GC.CollectionCount(0)` (`gc-probe.csv`, with two pairs of its clock beside the profiler's). It pauses in stops of the world and not in waits on the lock.
  - `Hold` calls `GC.SuppressFinalize` on a plain object every 20 µs. That takes Mono's finalizer-table lock, then the allocation lock, without allocating. It logs calls of 0.3 ms or more and the frame of each autosave (`gc-lock.csv`).
    - Such a call waited for either lock, was suspended by a stop, or was descheduled.
    - The first thread separates the stops from the rest, but nothing separates the two locks, or a wait from descheduling.

  They take two cores, so a probe run is not a frame-time measurement.
- **Left as it is**, at the user's "Leave it" (November 13).
- **If it needs to go.**
  - **Allocate less in a save.** Keep each person's text while it is unchanged, and write the rest straight into the file. A save allocates about 0.8 MB, about half of the game's steady allocation, so cycles would also come less often. Fewer allocations meet fewer holds, but any allocation can still meet one.
  - **Not recommended: `GarbageCollector.GCMode` Disabled around the save.** The holds far from cycles were not marking. Sweeping and heap growth still take the lock, and so does each switch.
  - **No help: `GCMode.Manual` alone.** Any allocating thread still advances a running cycle.
  - **Turning incremental collection off** makes every cycle one full stop.

## Saves: the text shared by two workers and the main thread, November 12, 2026 (0.6.150-test)

With the player by an explorer's hut, a save's main-thread P90 was 2.25 to 3.05 ms, because the main thread waited for the one worker writing the text ([results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-12/save-pieces-shared/results.md)). The save file is byte for byte the same. No rule, save format or drawn pixel changes.

- **Pieces** (`PolarSaveService.Text`).
  - Each person is a piece: their four kept lists (`KeptList`), and `JsonUtility` over the rest of them (`PolarActorState.Bare`), into `persons`.
  - Each hut is a piece: its mark, and its text when the mark changed (`Kept`).
  - The last piece is the state without people or huts: `PolarGameplayState.Bare` now gives `actors` and `sites` both empty.
- **Who writes them.** `WriteInBackground` starts two workers (`Workers`), which take pieces in turn (`Interlocked.Increment`). The main thread validates, then takes what is left (`Wait`). It waits only for the pieces the workers are still writing: a `ManualResetEventSlim` is set by whoever finishes the last piece.
  - A worker that starts after the last piece is taken returns without reading the state.
  - The first failure in any piece is raised on the main thread (`Made`), and the save fails as before.
  - `Write`, `Payload` and `HutText` take every piece on their own thread.
- **Threads.** The kept arrays (`huts`, `marks`, `lists`, `listMarks`, `persons`) are sized on the main thread before any piece is taken (`Size`). Each piece writes only its own entries. As before, only the save's text writes them, after `Finish` has waited for the publishing task that reads them. `JsonUtility` runs on up to three threads at once, each call on its own objects.
- **Putting it together.** `Rest`, on the publishing task, finds `"actors":[]` and `"sites":[]` in the state's own short text. It puts each person there, with their four lists in place, separated by commas. Then it finds the cut for the huts, all in one `string.Create`.
- **Measured.** In a near walk day on Development builds:
  - a save's main-thread median went from 1.56 to 1.04 ms (minutes 2 to 6) and from 1.72 to 1.07 ms (minutes 10 to 15), with the P90 from 2.25 to 1.24 and 3.05 to 1.39 ms;
  - the wait after validating went from a median of 0.79 and 0.93 ms to 0.16 ms;
  - the two workers do about 0.3 ms more work in all than the one did. Allocation is unchanged.

## Managed allocation: a save's text beside its validation, November 12, 2026 (0.6.148-test)

This is the 2.3 ms a save still cost the main thread after 0.6.146-test ([results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-12/save-step-peaks/results.md)). The save file is byte for byte the same. No rule, save format or drawn pixel changes.

- **The text beside the validation.**
  - `WriteInBackground` starts `Bare` on a worker: the huts' and people's lists' kept text and their marks, and `JsonUtility` over the bare rest (`JsonUtility` is thread-safe).
  - The main thread then runs `Validate`. Both only read the state, and the main thread changes nothing until both are done.
  - A claim flag (`Interlocked.Exchange`) decides who writes the text. If the worker has claimed it, the main thread waits for it. If not, the main thread writes it itself, so a busy thread pool never holds a save up.
  - A worker that fails leaves the text to the main thread, which then throws as before.
- **Threads.** Only one thread at a time writes the kept text and marks: the main thread or the worker it waits for. As before, `Finish` first waits for the previous save's publishing task, which reads them.
- **The save's folder** is made by the publishing task, from the save's path.
- **Item marks.** `Mixed.Add(PolarItemInstance)` takes the item's 32-bit fields two to a 64-bit step (`Pair`, the low half unsigned): 7 steps an item where there were 12. Every field still changes the mark.
- **Validation.** `Validate` tests each carried, boxed and dropped item in one pass (`Item`: its tests, then a new id below `nextId`). Before, it took three passes. A hut's structure is tested against `PolarStructure`'s count (`StructureKinds`), where it used `Enum.IsDefined`, so `PolarStructure` must stay a plain list from 0. The outcome and message are the same in every case tested.
- **Measured.** In a free walk day on Development builds, a save's main-thread median went from 2.26 to 1.47 ms (minutes 2 to 6) and from 2.67 to 1.58 ms (minutes 10 to 15). The validation went from 1.09 to 0.71 ms. After validating, the main thread waits about 0.7 ms for the text.

## Managed allocation: a save's people lists kept as text, November 12, 2026 (0.6.146-test)

This is the last item the user left open: the 4 to 5 ms a save still cost the main thread after 0.6.138-test (the section below) ([results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-12/save-text-lists/results.md)). The save file is byte for byte the same. No rule, save format or drawn pixel changes.

- **Before.** The rest of the state, `JsonUtility.ToJson(state.WithoutSites())`, was 250,000 to 290,000 characters at every save, about 2 ms of the main thread. Three quarters of it was four lists each person holds: `route`, `inventory`, `locations` (with the boxes and items seen there) and `experiences`.
- **Each person's four lists are kept as text** (`PolarSaveService.lists`, `KeptList`). Each is kept with a number mixed from every saved field of its members. `Mark` now covers `List<PolarPosition>`, `List<PolarItemInstance>`, `List<PolarLocationRecord>` (with its `PolarContainerRecord`s and their items) and `List<PolarExperience>`. `JsonUtility` writes a list again only when its number changes. It writes it in a wrapper (`Wrapped<T>`), whose text is the list's own inside `{"list":` and `}`.
- **The rest** is `JsonUtility.ToJson(state.Bare())`. That is a copy of the state with no huts, whose people are copies (`PolarActorState.Bare`) with the four lists empty, so the state in play is never touched. `Bare` replaces `WithoutSites`.
- **The worker** (`Rest`) finds each person's `"route":[]`, `"inventory":[]`, `"locations":[]` and `"experiences":[]`, in the order `JsonUtility` writes them. No other saved class has fields with these names, and a name inside a string is escaped. It makes the text with the kept lists in it as one string (`string.Create`), then finds the huts' cut as before.
- **Threads.** Only the main thread writes the kept lists and marks. It does so after `Finish` has waited for the worker that reads them, as with the huts.
- **A new saved field must go into `Mark`.** This now covers `PolarLocationRecord`, `PolarContainerRecord` and `PolarExperience`, as well as the hut classes. The gameplay mode's check (`PolarMvpAcceptance.SaveTextFault`) also adds one member to each of the last person's four lists. It changes each saved field alone, and each part of a route point: 113 changes.
- **Also.** `Mixed.Add`'s steps are inlined; Mono inlined none of them. `Validate`'s box and item tests are plain loops, with the same tests, order and messages.
- **Measured.** In a walk day on Development builds, each save's own times were read from the profiler logs. A save's median on the main thread is 2.25 ms; it was 4.29 ms. The validation takes 1.07 ms and the text 0.98 ms. Managed allocation is as it was: 2.466 MB a second, 2.457 before.

## Managed allocation: a save's text in pieces, November 11, 2026 (0.6.138-test)

The user's "Yes, do it here" on the item 0.6.137-test left open, after its live day ([results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-11/save-payload-text/results.md)). The save file is byte for byte the same; no rule, save format or drawn pixel changes.

- **Before.** `JsonUtility.ToJson(state)` wrote the whole state on the main thread at every save: one string of about 2.8 MB, about 11 ms. 84% of that text is the huts, and 69% the items in their boxes.
- **Each hut's text is kept** (`PolarSaveService.huts`) with a number mixed from every saved field of the hut, its boxes, their items and its notes (`Mark`; 64 bits, floats and doubles by their bits). `Kept` has `JsonUtility` write a hut again only when its number has changed.
- **A new saved field must go into `Mark`.** A field added to `PolarSiteState`, `PolarContainerState`, `PolarItemInstance` or `PolarNote` and left out of `Mark` would, changed alone, leave the hut's old text in the save. The gameplay mode's check (`PolarMvpAcceptance.SaveTextFault`) changes every field of the four in turn, found by reflection, and fails on one that `Mark` misses.
- **The rest of the state** is `JsonUtility.ToJson(state.WithoutSites())` (since 0.6.146-test `Bare`, with each person's lists kept too; the section above): a copy of the state's fields with an empty hut list, so the state in play is never without its huts. `Rest` returns that text with `cut`, the place inside `"sites":[]` where the huts' texts belong (no other saved class has a field named `sites`).
- **The worker** takes the rest, the cut and the kept texts. `Hash` encodes them one after another into its kept buffer, and `PolarSaveFile.WriteEnvelope` escapes them one after another into the file, with commas between the huts. Every piece starts and ends at a JSON mark, so the bytes are the whole text's.
- **Threads.** Only the main thread writes `huts` and `marks`, and only after `Finish` has waited for the worker that reads them. `Payload` and `HutText`, the suite's two hooks, wait too.
- **Kept between saves:** the huts' texts, about 2.4 MB of strings for a world. The number is of a hut's content, not of an expedition, so a new or loaded expedition replaces the texts hut by hut.
- **Measured** (a walking game day on Development builds, `Tools/polar_save_rate.py --walk --profile`, 179 saves; markers `Polar.Save.Validate` and `Polar.Save.Text`): a save takes 5.1 ms of the main thread where it took 12.8 ms (the text 3.2 ms, the validation 1.6 ms); the text's strings at one save are 578 KB where they were 2,994 KB; "GC Allocated In Frame" is 9.5 KB a frame where it was 18.0.
- **Verified.** An Editor probe over 1,535 kept saves: the whole text from the pieces is `JsonUtility`'s in every one, and the file's bytes are those of `JsonUtility`'s own envelope in the 627 that pass today's validation; a negative control (one field left out of `Mark`) fails. The full suite passes on `pt-01` with gameplay 391 (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.138-test/pt-01`), and so do front page 173, startup 4 and screen-args 3 (`Logs/PolarFront/0.6.138-test/pt-01`).
- **In a live day** (November 11, [results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-11/live-day-0138/results.md)) a save takes 4.2 ms of the main thread where it took 11.1 ms on 0.6.137-test (the text 2.4 ms, the validation 1.5 ms), the text's strings at one save are 494 KB with up to 67 KB of huts written again where they were 2,773 KB, and no save is among the frames over 25 ms in the four timed minutes (two of six before).

## Memory: the front page lets an expedition go, and a save's envelope is written as it goes, November 11, 2026 (0.6.137-test)

Two of the known items the user asked to be solved after 0.6.136-test ([results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-11/known-items/results.md); the explorers' step is in [gameplay](polar-gameplay.md)). No rule, save format or drawn pixel changes.

- **Leaving an expedition** (`PolarMvpController.QuitToMenu`) drops its people, views and model client (`DropExpedition`, also run when a new expedition starts) and starts `PolarWorldController.Unload`. Unload waits for the world sound's 1 s fade (`PolarAudio.SetWorld`) and any streaming work, then: `PolarHdrpPresentation.Detach` destroys the presentation's own materials and far shadows, forgets the world's huts, stoves and sweep, turns its volumes off and the front page's camera on, and disables it (`Attach` turns it back on for the next world); the generated world and its plan textures are destroyed; every reference to the world, its player, camera, streamer, ice, atmosphere and scatter is dropped (`World` is null, `Ready` false); then `Resources.UnloadUnusedAssets` and a collection. A new expedition started during the wait stops it. The front page draws its opaque backdrop over everything, so nothing seen changes.
- **Measured** (release builds, the benchmark route and a second expedition): the front page between the expeditions holds 3.70 GiB of private memory where it held 4.85, and 1.04 GB of video memory where it held 2.00; after the second expedition 3.60 GiB where it held 5.05; the run's peak 5.21 GB where it was 5.63. What stays: the managed heap's size (Mono never shrinks it), Unity's allocator reserves, about 0.9 GB of HDRP's render targets and the driver's own memory, which the next expedition reuses.
- **A save's envelope** (`PolarSaveService`'s worker) is written straight into the temporary file by `PolarSaveFile.WriteEnvelope`: `{"version":1,"payload":"…","sha256":"…"}` with the strings escaped by `PolarSaveFile.Escape` as `JsonUtility` escapes them (`"` and `\`, the five short escapes, other control characters as `\u00XX` in upper-case hex, everything else as it is, a NUL ending the string, a null string written empty; probed on Unity 6000.3). The file is byte for byte what it was (a probe of every character to 0x2FFF and 191 kept saves); the worker no longer builds the envelope as one string (3.4 MB for the largest save). `Read` still parses the envelope with `JsonUtility`. What a save still allocates is the payload text, made on the main thread by `JsonUtility.ToJson` (about 3 MB). That followed in 0.6.138-test (the section above).
- **Verified.** Shared rules 2750 (two new envelope checks), explorer checks 208. The front-page validation checks after a return that the world is silent and let go (`World` and `SurveyCamera` null, `Ready` false); it passes 173 on `fp-02`, with startup 4 and screen-args 3 (`Logs/PolarFront/0.6.137-test/fp-02c`).

## Managed allocation: saves at most every 5 s, November 10, 2026 (0.6.135-test)

The user's "Both changes" after the save rate was measured (the section below): a hut's "last seen" stamp no longer marks the state changed, and a change is saved once the last save is 5 real seconds old ([gameplay](polar-gameplay.md), [results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-10/save-cadence/results.md)). No rule, save format or drawn pixel changes.

- **Measured** (one game day, Development builds, High 1600x900, the model withheld): 19 saves where there were 350 (1.3 a minute, was 23), the median gap 60 s (the 60 s autosave), 27 changes of the state where there were 3,303, and 56 MB a minute of managed allocation where there were 185.
- **Under constant change** (`-polarSaveRateChurn`, the state marked changed every frame) the game saves 11.7 times a minute, never under 5.01 s apart; a live day can therefore make at most 12 saves a minute where it made 75, at most 70 MB a minute of saves where there were about 470. A live day on the package (November 11, [results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-11/save-cadence-live/results.md)) saves 177 times where it saved 1,129 and allocates 243 MB a minute by day where it allocated 634; about 170 MB a minute of that is the explorers' own work. Frames over 25 ms fall only from 0.99% to 0.86% of frames, so most of a live day's slow frames are not save frames; their cause is not yet measured.
- **Verified.** Shared rules 2748, explorer checks 206. The full suite passes on `ag-01`: gameplay 390, physical 3959, physical-resume 18, continue 36, performance 79, buildings 395, fractured 31, handbook 353, weather 84, weather-resume 10, ui 463, handbook-revision 619 (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.135-test/ag-01`), and so do front page 173, startup 4 and screen-args 3 (`Logs/PolarFront/0.6.135-test/ag-01`).

## Managed allocation: how often a real expedition saves, November 10, 2026 (measured, no package)

The save cadence is as it was (a save half a second after any change of the state, and the 60 s autosave); this section records what that cadence does in a real expedition. [Results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-10/save-rate/results.md), measured with `-polarSaveRate` ([settings and benchmark](polar-settings.md)) over one game day on a Development build of the 0.6.134-test source.

- **With the explorers acting (the model live) the game saves 75 times a minute**: 81 by day, 50 at night, the median gap 0.52 s, which is the rule's own limit. The state changes in four of every five seconds.
- **With every explorer waiting it still saves 23 times a minute.** All but 7 of 3,303 changes are `PolarActions.ObserveExterior`: every person re-stamps the "last seen" time of each hut in sight every 5 game seconds (`PolarMvpController.DiscoverExterior`), and the stamp calls `Changed`.
- **In the live day** the stamps are 46% of 4,986 changes; boxes looked into are 12%, finished plan steps 10%, decisions taken up 9%, things picked up 8%.
- **Cost.** Saves are about three quarters of all managed allocation (635 MB a minute by day in the live run, 187 MB with the explorers waiting), and 1.0% of frames are over 25 ms in the live day against 0.02%.
- **Left to the user:** a hut's stamp without a save of its own, a shortest time between autosaves, or a cheaper save. None is made.

## Memory trims: unused maps, smaller atlases and 16-bit terrain indices, November 10, 2026 (0.6.134-test)

The items the [memory profile](../logs/evidence/2026-11-10/memory-profile/report.md) left unstarted were looked into, and the four savings that change nothing on screen are applied. The [results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-10/memory-trims/results.md) measure them and list what waits for a choice. No rule, save, world hash or drawn pixel changes.

- **Terrain indices.** `PolarWorldStreamer.Pack` writes a mesh's indices as 16-bit when the mesh has at most 65,535 vertices (`MeshBuffer.narrow`; `indices` holds them for a larger mesh), and the upload gives the mesh the matching index format. The gap walls' relief meshes keep 32-bit. `Digest` mixes 16-bit indices as the 32-bit values they replace, so `-polarMeshDigest` compares across the change.
- **HDRP atlases** (`PolarPipeline.asset`): the decal atlas is 512 px (was 4096; the game has no decal projector, and water decals have their own atlas), the reflection probe atlas 2048 px (was 4096; it holds the one 128 px hut probe, `PolarHdrpPresentation.HutProbe`), the light cookie atlas 1024 px (was 2048; it holds the 256 px moon surface). A decal projector or more probes or cookies would need them raised again. Every build re-saves this asset, so a change to it must be staged or committed before the re-saved assets are restored.
- **Outcrop models.** `Rock3.fbx` and `Rock5.fbx` under `Resources/Polar/HDRP/Environment/SourceModels` map their material to `Polar/Derived/TundraLichenRock` in their import settings. `PolarArtOutcrops` loads these models for their meshes and draws them in the game's own rock material, so the vendor pack's material and its three maps a model no longer load with them. The meshes and the `Sources` list are unchanged.
- **Handbook arms.** `Resources/HandheldHandbook/Book.prefab` names `PolarWeapons/FirstPersonWinterGloves` on both arms, the material `PolarHdrpBookMaterials.Dress` gives them before the book is drawn, in place of the old `Arm` material and its two maps. `MvpHandheldBookBuilder.Build` would write the old material back and must not be rerun.
- **Measured** (benchmark route, High 1920x1080): on release builds private memory is 281 to 356 MiB lower at every point of the route (4.70 to 4.35 GiB at its end) and the peak of a run with a second expedition falls from 5.62 to 5.24 GiB; in Development snapshots render textures fall by 164.0 MB, textures by 74.7 MB and meshes by 56.5 MB; the handbook's first opening takes 129 ms where it took 182 ms.
- **Verified.** 71 Editor views before and after (hut rooms with the probe, clear nights with the moon, cliffs with outcrops, the handbook, rifle and harpoon in hand) differ only in what moves with time: drifting cirrus, the rifle's shot, a drifting snow streak, a HUD key hint and a twinkling glint. The terrain mesh digest of a new seed-777 expedition is `BF7FD192D47A0654` before and after. The full suite passes on `mt-01`: gameplay 390, physical 3959, physical-resume 18, continue 36, performance 79, buildings 395, fractured 31, handbook 353, weather 84, weather-resume 10, ui 463, handbook-revision 619 (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.134-test/mt-01`), and so do front page 173, startup 4 and screen-args 3 (`Logs/PolarFront/0.6.134-test/mt-01`). Every mode's memory peak is lower than on `sv-01`, by 0.11 to 0.42 GB (gameplay 5.74 GB, was 6.15).

## Managed allocation: the save path, November 10, 2026 (0.6.133-test)

Set 3 of the [allocation report](../logs/evidence/2026-11-10/allocation-stacks/report.md), at the user's "Apply set 3". The [results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-10/save-path/results.md) measure it. The save file is byte for byte the same; no rule, save format or drawn pixel changes, and the save cadence (a save half a second after any change, the 60 s autosave) is as it was.

- **The file's bytes.** `PolarSaveFile.Write` encodes the text as it writes it, through a `StreamWriter` with `new UTF8Encoding(false)` (no byte-order mark, the bytes `Encoding.UTF8.GetBytes` gives). The whole text in one array was 1.5 MB at every save.
- **The checksum's bytes.** `PolarSaveService.Hash` encodes into one kept buffer (`hashed`). Only one hash runs at a time: the save worker's, or one on the world thread after `Finish` has waited for the worker. A caller that broke that rule would write a wrong checksum.
- **Validation** (`PolarSaveService.Validate`, on the world thread at every save and load): one kept set `ids` takes the boxes' ids and then the items', so an id it refuses belongs to a box or to an earlier item (the old `Distinct` and `Intersect` tests); `Slotted` checks a box's items in one pass with a 48-bit slot mask; a kind is tested against its enum's count (`BoxKinds`, `ItemKinds`: `PolarContainerKind` and `PolarItemKind` are plain lists from 0, and must stay so, or `Enum.IsDefined` must come back); `Repeats` compares pairs in short lists; the boxes' items come from the box array, not from each hut's `inventory` property.
- **Measured** (benchmark route, High 1920x1080, Development builds, 29 saves a run): one save 10.0 to 5.8 MB (main thread 4.3 to 2.9 MB, worker 5.7 to 2.9 MB); the save's frame about 8 ms longer than its neighbours, where it was about 17 ms; frames over 25 ms on the route 19 to 3 of about 9,500. What is left of a save is the state's JSON text and the envelope's text, 5.6 MB.
- **Verified.** Under Unity's Mono with the Player's class libraries the new writer and hash give the old bytes and checksums for 611 texts (nine real saves, block boundaries, split and lone surrogates, a 3,000,000-character text), and a writer with a byte-order mark is caught. Each rewritten validation test agrees with the expression it replaced on 1,000,248 cases (none accepted by one and refused by the other). Shared rules 2748. The full suite passes on `sv-01`, 6,437 checks (performance on its second run: the first had the camera pitched at the sky at one check, as mouse input does). The front-page mode stopped at the display confirm without the window in front (114 checks passed).

## Managed allocation: plain loops and a height cache that holds a world, November 10, 2026 (0.6.132-test)

The [allocation report](../logs/evidence/2026-11-10/allocation-stacks/report.md) named the code that allocates managed memory every frame; the user chose its fix sets 1 and 2. The [results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-10/allocation-fixes/results.md) measure them. No rule, save, world hash or drawn pixel changes.

- **Lookups on the game state.** `PolarGameplayState.Site()`, `Actor()`, `PolarActorState.Item()`, `Known()`, `Count()` and `PolarActions.KnownContainer()` are plain loops. Each was a LINQ search with a capturing lambda, three objects at every call, and they run hundreds of times a frame (146 hut doors each look their hut up): 47 to 53 KB a frame, now 0.1.
- **Lists walked by index.** `PolarWorldStreamer.Shore()` and `Separates()` and `PolarThinIce.HalfPlane()` take an `IList`; a `foreach` over it boxed an enumerator at each call, one for every terrain vertex built (10 KB a frame on a walk). Meshes and world hashes are bit for bit the same: all twenty v23 seeds keep their hashes.
- **Smaller per-frame sources.** Tests moved inside plain loops (`StepEnvironment`, `RefreshDrops`, `ObserveBodies`, `CheckLastSurvivor`, `UpdateViews`), `AdvanceAction` called only for a person with an action, the site view's removal pass only when the counts differ, kept delegates and constants (`ShelterAtHeading`, `Shelter`, `Mass`), task lambdas over copies (`PolarFarShadows.Update`), and no IMGUI layout pass for the world controller (`useGUILayout` false; its `OnGUI` draws nothing in the game and must stay free of `GUILayout`).
- **Renderer sweep.** `PolarHdrpPresentation.BeginSweep` keeps the `UnityEngine.Object[]` that `FindObjectsByType(typeof(Renderer), ...)` returns and casts each element. The typed call copied all of them into a second array: 188 KB every 2 seconds, now 94.
- **Height cache.** `PolarHeightCache` keeps `Blocks` decoded blocks, every block of one world (`PolarLayout.SectorCount*16` = 672, 21.3 MiB), where it kept 128 (4.1 MiB). One far-shadow sweep (`PolarFarShadows.Heights`, at every 1,000 m of eye travel and each load) reads most of a world's blocks on every pool thread at once; through 128 entries it decoded each block 12 to 16 times, 0.5 to 0.9 GB of arrays in about 3 seconds, and the main thread waited on the cache's lock in frames of 80 to 220 ms. Distant proxies read every block while loading, so the cache is full from the loading screen on and nothing is decoded in play. A new expedition decodes up to 336 district blocks once more as the reference world's blocks leave the cache.
- **Measured** (benchmark route, High 1920x1080, Development builds): allocation a frame 119 to 56 KB on the walk, 613 to 29 at the coast, 379 to 47 into a blizzard; the main thread between saves 53-63 KB to about 2 KB; allocations a frame about 1,100 to about 100; the longest frame at the coast 201 to 41 ms and into a blizzard 218 to 54 ms; collection steps on the main thread 80 frames and 225 ms to 19 frames and 55 ms. Loading on release packages, alternating: playable about 3 s sooner, refinement complete 5 to 9 s sooner.
- **Left.** The autosave allocates 10.2 MB a save and its frame is about 16 ms longer than its neighbours: every frame over 25 ms on the route but one is now a save frame. The save path (10.2 to 5.9 MB a save) and how often the game saves wait for the user. Terrain mesh output arrays are the mesh itself and stay.
- **Verified.** Eight review agents found every edit identical in behaviour; twenty seeds keep their hashes; shared rules 2748; the full suite passes on `ma-01`, 6,437 checks. The front-page mode stopped at the display confirm twice without the window in front (114 checks passed each time).

## Memory: the vendor pack's managers left empty, November 10, 2026 (0.6.131-test)

The [memory profile](../logs/evidence/2026-11-10/memory-profile/report.md) found about 1.2 GB of Survival Template Pro textures loaded since the front page. The [audit](../logs/evidence/2026-11-10/pack-asset-audit/audit.md) found the cause and the [results](../logs/evidence/2026-11-10/pack-asset-audit/results.md) measure the fix. Nothing drawn, no rule and no save changes.

- **Cause.** Two start-up hooks in the pack (`SaveableDatabase.Init`, `BuildingManager.Init`, both `BeforeSplashScreen`) load their manager assets with `Resources.Load`. `SaveableDatabase.asset` lists 74 pack prefabs and `BuildingManager.asset` reaches two more; their materials, 4096 px maps, sounds and clips come with them and stay for the life of the process, held by the static `Manager<T>.Instance`. The game never reads either manager: no game code names the pack, and the built scene holds only the polar controllers and a camera.
- **Fix.** `PolarPackManagers` (`Assets/Scripts/Polar/Gameplay/PolarPackManagers.cs`) runs at `SubsystemRegistration`, the first start-up stage, and sets empty instances of `SaveableDatabase`, `BuildingManager` and `PoolManager` through `Manager<T>.Instance`'s private setter. The pack's loader reads `Resources` only when `Instance` is null, so the assets are never read.
  - The database's prefab array is set to an empty array. A null array makes the pack throw before the splash screen, which both Player runners treat as a failed run.
  - `PoolManager` has no asset and would search the whole `Managers/` folder; seeded, it never does. Its instance is the bare one the pack would have made.
  - No check surrounds the reflection, on purpose: a pack copy that differs fails to compile or throws at start instead of bringing the memory back unnoticed.
  - No file under `Assets/PolymindGames` (untracked) changes. The game's code now names pack types, so a checkout needs that folder to compile, as it needs `Assets/Feel`.
- **What still comes from the pack.** Art the game shows loads through the game's own assets at first use, not at start: the handbook's book, clips and arm maps, the bodies and clothes of Frost, Moss and Shaw, beards, the cartridge, garment textures (about 140 MB in all). The pack's other hooks run as before (graphics options, audio, input, level and surface managers).
- **Measured** (benchmark route, High, 1920x1080, Development builds `mp-02` and `pm-02-dev`): private memory 1.32 to 1.36 GiB lower at every point in play (open snow 6.06 to 4.72 GiB), 1.46 GiB lower on the front page (2.38 to 0.92 GiB), the run's peak 6.40 to 5.08 GiB. Front-page textures 1,193 to 44 MB. A warm start reaches the front page in about 7.7 s instead of 9.8 s.
- **Suite** on `pm-02`: all 12 modes pass (6,437 checks) and every mode's peak private memory falls 1.3 to 1.6 GB; gameplay, the highest, peaks at 6.14 GB (was 7.50 to 7.70) against the 8 GiB (8.59 GB) gate. Front-page 173, startup 4 and screen-args 3 pass.
- **Not measured.** The one-off load at the first handbook opening and the first sight of a survivor-bodied explorer; Editor Play Mode with the new class.
- **Left.** The pack's `Rock3` and `Rock5` materials pulled in by the owned rock model copies (74.7 MB in play, not drawn with), the handbook's `Arm` maps (32 MB, replaced after loading), the pack's surface roots (16.7 MB), and the pack assets that stay in the package on disk (about 1.3 GB of 3.27 GB).

## Unity 6000.3.28f1 and HDRP 17.3; D3D12 still crashes on quit, November 7, 2026 (0.6.129-test)

The user said yes to upgrading Unity, then trying Direct3D 12 again. Unity 6000.2 is end of life, and its D3D12 start failures (087a0001) were fixed in 6000.3.11f1. The game moves from Unity 6000.2.15f1 / HDRP 17.2.0 to 6000.3.28f1 (8f082d2fe476) / HDRP 17.3.0 and stays on Direct3D 11. Render pipeline, rules, saves and world hashes are unchanged.
- **Upgrade.**
  - Unity's own upgrade of `Packages/manifest.json`: HDRP, core and Shader Graph 17.3.0; Input System 1.20.1; light-transport removed; newer patch versions elsewhere. `ProjectVersion.txt`, the pipeline asset (`m_Version` 26), the global settings, graphics and quality settings, and ModelImporter 24300 on the pack's FBX files were also rewritten by the import.
  - HDRP 17.3 writes every component of `DefaultSettingsVolumeProfile.asset` with its overrides on at 17.3's defaults. The values that differ from the old file (for example ambient occlusion's ray length, exposure limits, sky ambient mode, water's ambient probe dimmer) had their overrides off before, so HDRP already used the code defaults. Kept.
  - The import blanked `MvpNaturalVisualCatalog.asset`'s PolymindGames references; it was restored from git.
  - The first batch open stalled: HDRP 17.3's baked reflection tick looped on "The scene is not loaded" after Unity opened a vendor example scene mid-import. Killing it and opening again finished.
- **Code.**
  - `PolarSurface.shader`: the GBuffer, TransparentBackface and Forward passes take `Lit.hlsl` and `ShaderPassForward.hlsl` with `#include_with_pragmas`, as HDRP 17.3's Lit does (those files now carry `#pragma rendertarget_format_hint`). `PolarProperties.hlsl` declares `_BentNormalMapOS` in its layered branch, as 17.3's LitProperties does. `PolarGround.shader` regenerated (`PolarGroundTwinAuthor`).
  - `PolarHdrpBuild`, `PolarMvpBuild` and `Tools/polar_v7_player_run.py` require HDRP 17.3.
  - Main-thread time. Unity 6000.3's `FrameTiming.cpuMainThreadFrameTime` is the main thread's own work: it leaves out the waits for the render thread and for present, as Unity's counter reference says. On 6000.2 it included them, so with the GPU busy it read as the whole frame. The code subtracted `cpuMainThreadPresentWaitTime` on top, which on 6000.3 went negative (-2.1 ms at the pond on Extreme). The pond and coast checks, the benchmark and the performance overlay now read `cpuMainThreadFrameTime` alone (`PolarMvpAcceptance.PondSoak.cs`, `.SeaCost.cs`, `PolarBenchmarkRun`, `PolarPerformanceOverlay`). Read that way on a quiet machine, breaking through the ice costs main-thread time over the same pond quiet: +0.83 and +0.68 ms before 0.6.128-test was merged, +1.25, +1.77 and +0.95 ms after (`u63-02d`: intact 5.22, breaking 5.85, quiet 5.02 ms; `u63-04`: 5.04, 6.64 and 5.39 ms), while frames stay GPU-bound (11.2-12.7 ms P95). The bank view alone reads 5.04-6.77 ms between runs, so the check is noisy. On 6000.2 the waits hid it (+0.21). 0.6.128-test's terrain also raised the pond's GPU P95 by 1.3 ms here (1.7 ms on 6000.2, `sr2-02` against `sr3-01-pc2`). The pond check allows 1.5 ms instead of 0.5: the user chose 1 ms, then 1.5 ms after the +1.25. Like 6000.2's, it can still need a rerun. The coast's check (0.25 ms, median of 16 pairs) reads +0.11 ms and keeps its limit.
- **Build.** The first build compiled every shader afresh (2348 s); the next took 149 s. 3.60 GB (6000.2: 3.60 GB). One error, the inherited CTI `LOD_FADE_CROSSFADE` message.
- **Look.** The gameplay mode's screenshots against `sr2-01` (6000.2, same source) differ by timing, not rendering: the 6000.3 run's clock is 3-4 game minutes later, so breath clouds, the stove's flicker and positions differ. Six pairs in the evidence.
- **Package build** `u63-05` (0.6.128-test merged in): gameplay 390 on `u63-04`, which differs only in the pond limit (1 ms; its performance run stopped there at +1.25 ms), and performance 79 on `u63-05` at the second run (`u63-05b`; the first, `u63-05`, stopped on the pond check at +1.77 ms main and +1.17 ms GPU, with the bank view alone 6.77 ms of main thread against 5.04-5.67 in other runs) (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.129-test`).
- **D3D11 results** (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.127-test/u63-01`, `u63-01b`, `u63-01c`, `u63-02d`, `u63-03`): gameplay 390 (peak 7.70 GB, 6000.2 7.45), physical 3959, physical-resume 18, continue 36, buildings 395, fractured 31, handbook 353, handbook-revision 619, ui 463, weather 84 and weather-resume 10 (all eight weather P95s 16.67-16.73 ms); performance 79 on `u63-05` (`u63-05b`, above).
  - Weather first failed at 22.3 ms indoors in grade 2 and three performance runs failed, each while another session's Editor art capture or Player run went on; ui first failed on a missing test save in the worktree (`Logs/CloudNpc/CaptureSave`).
  - On a quiet machine (`u63-02d`) every frame-paced view's P95 was 16.68-16.75 ms, as on 6000.2 (`sr2-01-pc2`: 16.71-16.75). `u63-03`'s first views (medium snow 23.05 ms) overlapped another session's performance run.
- **Benchmark** against `sr2-02`, alternating, two runs each, 1600x900, TAA, on AC (`Logs/PolarBenchmark/u63-vs-62`): average fps Low 175.3 (176.7), Medium 137.7 (138.4), High 113.9 (113.7), Ultra 99.1 (92.7, one slow 6000.2 run), Extreme 88.3 (88.8). 1% lows vary ±15% between runs on both. The player's data folder hashed the same before and after.
- **D3D12 trial** (`u63-d12-01`, D3D12 first; scripts in `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-07/d3d12-trial`; results `Logs/PolarD3D12/u63`, `Logs/PolarV7/0.6.127-test/u63-d12-01`).
  - Starts are fixed: 17 of 17, ten visible, one hidden and three pairs started at once (on 6000.2 two of three pairs failed, and after the morning's Windows update every start failed).
  - Twenty loading-profile starts and quits were clean, as on 6000.2.
  - The quit crash remains: 4 of the suite's 10 game modes ended with an access violation after every check had passed. It is now in the Agility SDK copy of `D3D12Core.dll` 1.618 that 6000.3 ships (offsets 0x26488e and 0xa1f5). Unity's player symbols (`UnityPlayer_Win64_player_mono_x64.pdb` in the Editor's non-development player folder) place it on the main thread in `DoQuit` → `DestroyGfxDevice` → `~GfxDeviceD3D12`: Unity's own device teardown, after the game's code has finished.
  - Private memory is higher: gameplay 8.50 GB against 7.70 on D3D11, 0.09 GB under the suite's 8 GiB budget.
  - The D3D12 benchmark and loading comparisons were not run, and ray-traced hut light does not start.
- **Ways back to D3D12.** A later Unity release that fixes the device teardown; or ending the process after the game has saved and shut down, before Unity destroys the device (not done: it skips Unity's own shutdown, so every save and log write must be finished first).

## Crest strips, cliff feet and the near crest's edge, November 7, 2026 (0.6.128-test)

The user asked for a plan for the three things 0.6.127-test left. Render only: rules, colliders, saves, world hashes and navigation are unchanged. The drawn snow depth (`PolarSnowDepth.Mask`) and the moss scatter (`PolarSnowDepth.Bare`) follow the shader, as before.
- **What was wrong.**
  - **Crest strips.** A wind-bare crest's strip read as one thin grey line along its ridge for hundreds of metres. The crest cover (vertex A, `PolarLandscape.GroundCover`) is a narrow strip near 1 along a whole crest. There the bare test's base (.40 at cover 1) sits above the bare edge (.27) by more than the crest field's noise ever falls, so the strip never closed.
  - **Cliff-foot teeth.** Distant cliffs showed a row of small teeth along their foot.
    - Distant blocks split every cell along one fixed diagonal, which crossed cliffs one or two cells wide obliquely.
    - The normals and the cliff band their triangles interpolate then put a notch at every cell.
    - Drawing the slope cover and the band alone showed the teeth in both.
  - **The near crest's edge.** A faint straight edge lay in the snow beside the near crest.
- **What changes.**
  - **Crest strips** (`PolarCoverShift` in `PolarData.hlsl`; `PolarSnowDepth.CoverShift`). The crest's bare test moves by up to -.30 through a gate of drift tongues along the wind (167 m along it, 25 m across) and 63 and 22 m octaves. Where the gate is low, drifts snow the strip over across its full width. Elsewhere the strip is as before.
    - **Modelled on ridges of every heading.** The share of 300 m stretches of a crest top that stay bare without a break falls from 17-21% to 1-2%. Bare runs along a crest are mostly about 15 m long, and the longest tenth from about 80-90 m (was 170-190 m). Crest ground draws 23% bare instead of 42%.
    - **It only ever closes.** Any widening moves the strip's edge out onto the cover's ramp. There the vertex cover outweighs the noise, so the edge follows the triangles:
      - at +.1, sawteeth along the b3-04/b3-05 crest;
      - at up to +.6 down the ramp, stairs, and a wide straight band where the strip filled the cover's whole footprint.
    - **Lee snow, tried and dropped.** Snow on lee flanks by the vertex normal was tried too.
  - **Cliff-foot teeth.** Distant blocks split each cell along the diagonal joining its closer heights (`PolarStreamingPolicy.NearFlip`, `PolarWorldStreamer.ComputeMeshBody`), as near cells have since October 26. The morph toward the coarser level (`AddMorph`, with its fan key) and the off-grid normals (`OffGrid`) follow the same split.
  - **Moss.** The moss scatter's bare test takes the world's wind axis.
- **The near crest's edge** (diagnosed, left).
  - Toggling each term in turn (one capture run, not shipped) showed that the straight line goes only with the ground twin's sastrugi relief off. It is a long, thin drift trough of the relief, lit low, not a triangle edge.
  - A meandering hairline beside it is a narrow crack.
  - The depth-bank tilt, the lip rounding, the map normals, the drift swell and the snow lift were each ruled out.
- **Result.** 23 views in b3-ground, b4-intact-ice and b3-snow-rock against a genuine before (evidence `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-07/crest-foot-edges`):
  - in the user's views from pond 33 (ii-distant, ii-distant-far), the grey line along the left ridge and the wide grey sheets to the right are snowed over in stretches;
  - the b3-04/b3-05 tundra crest keeps its soft edge, with a little more snow;
  - the two distant cliff feet (sr-foot-far, sr-foot-far-b) lose their teeth;
  - the panoramas, cliffs and peaks are unchanged or carry a little more snow.
- **Checks.** See current-status.
- **Left.**
  - Some crest stretches stay open. The capture's own crest (sr-crest-mid, sr-crest-near) lies in an open stretch for the 150 m in view and still reads as a strip there.
  - The near crest's faint drift trough.
  - Cliff lines keep their long, gently bending courses (world generation).

## Snow and rock on faces and crests, November 7, 2026 (0.6.127-test)

The user's "continue" on 0.6.125-test's natural snow and rock edges: a further pass over what still drew lines no real hill draws. Render only: rules, colliders, saves, world hashes and navigation are unchanged; the drawn snow depth (`PolarSnowDepth`) follows the shader, as before.
- **What was wrong.**
  - Cliff faces 100-200 m away showed white rectangles with straight vertical and diagonal sides, and a row of white sawteeth along a near face's foot. On steep triangles whose corners' drawn snow depth differs, the gap-lip rule (October 28, 2026) moves the texture coordinates onto the face's own plane, by up to the scene height (tens of metres). Since 0.6.125-test the rock cover follows noise at those coordinates, so whole triangles turned to snow.
  - Snow ledges on sheer faces (0.6.125-test) were blocks of a thresholded noise, which read as stacked white bricks from 30-200 m.
  - Beside wind-bare crests, close up, the snow showed a field of bright triangular facets. The crest cover rises within a few metres, so the drawn depth thins to bare ground steeply there, and the depth-bank tilt (`dTilt`, meant for hut discs, tent pits and pond and gap banks) lit each triangle as its own slope. The depth also followed the crest field's 14 and 3.4 m octaves, which near vertices a metre or two apart stepped from full to none.
  - Close up, a crest's snow edge over gravel was a soft wash a metre or two wide.
- **What changes** (`PolarData.hlsl`, mirrored by `PolarSnowDepth.Mask`).
  - Section 1's rock (its cover, the distant cliff band, the rock map and its perturbation) reads the coordinates before the lip faces move them (`qr`, `dqxr`, `dqyr`); snow textures on lip faces keep their own plane.
  - Couloirs no longer depend on |n.y|, which vertex normals carry from a cliff's foot and top across its few triangles (it cut straight-sided wedges). They are narrower, with ragged sides that come and go over 5 m along and 9 m down.
  - Ledges on faces are gone near the eye. From 300 to 650 m they fade in again (section 1 only, by pixel size), so a far face stays mottled rock and snow; nearer, the rock map's dust on upward facets stands for them. Thin strata contour lines were tried in their place and dropped: they drew long straight white bars across the faces.
  - `dTilt` is off on ground with crest cover (vertex A above .05).
  - The drawn depth thins over the crest field's 90 and 37 m octaves only, from .12 below the painted edge (`PolarSnowDepth.Bare` stays the moss scatter's test, with all four octaves).
  - The painted bare edge narrows near the eye (to .03 either side of .27, from .07 as a pixel grows past .16 m) and a 1.1 m octave rags it, gone by .13 m pixels, so far views are as before.
- **The ii-distant-far seam** (left by 0.6.125-test). A notes-only probe in b3-snow-rock projects the near window's edges, its chunk lines, the distant blocks' edges and the near stretch bands into that view, and marches the seam's own pixels to the ground: the seam crosses the chunk lines diagonally and lies on none of them. A transect across it drops 8 m within 3.5 m. It is a cliff line (`PolarLandforms.Cliffs`) seen from above its top with its face turned away, so only the lip and the shadow under it show; the new view sr-seam-near looks up at its rock face and snow cornice. World geometry, left as it is.
- **Result.** 19 views in b3-ground, b4-intact-ice and b3-snow-rock against a genuine before (evidence `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-07/snow-rock-faces`):
  - the cliff 180 m off loses its white rectangles, and a soft-edged snowed-over stretch remains where a couloir crosses it;
  - the near steep face loses its sawteeth and is rock with snow on its upward facets;
  - the snow beside the near crest loses its bright facets;
  - far escarpments keep their mottling, and the user's view and the panoramas barely change.
- **Checks.** See current-status.
- **Left.**
  - Crest bare strips seen along a ridge from 90 m still read as one thin grey line: the ground cover (`PolarLandscape.GroundCover`) is a narrow strip there.
  - On distant blocks a cliff's foot still shows small teeth where the slope cover crosses their large triangles.
  - A faint triangle edge remains in the snow beside the near crest.
  - Cliff lines keep their long, gently bending courses (world generation).

## D3D12 trial, November 7, 2026 (0.6.126-test)

HDRP traces rays only on Direct3D 12, so the user's ray-traced hut light needed D3D12 first. The trial built the same source as `d11-01` (D3D11) and `d12-01` (D3D12 first, D3D11 second) and ran starts, the Player suite and a diagnosis pass on `d12-01`. It failed, and the game stays on D3D11 (package build `d11-02`); ray-traced hut light was not started. Results and scripts: `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-07/d3d12-trial`.
- **What worked.**
  - Ten visible starts and one hidden start, the September failure's conditions on Built-in, all ran on D3D12.
  - Gameplay 390, physical 3959, physical-resume 18, performance 79, buildings 395, fractured 31 and handbook-revision 619 passed, and in a rerun continue 36, weather 84, weather-resume 10 and ui's 463 checks.
  - `d12-01` built in 119 s and is 18 MB larger than `d11-01`: D3D12 runs D3D11's shader bytecode, so no long shader compile.
- **What failed.**
  - **On quitting.** After every check had passed and the log reached the input system's shutdown, the process ended with an access violation in Windows' `D3D12Core.dll` (10.0.26100.9836, offset 0x24e85). Unity wrote no crash report; Windows' Application log names the module. It hit 8 of 20 completed D3D12 game-mode runs (handbook in 4 of 7, continue, weather and ui too) and none of 8 loading-profile runs, which only load and quit. No D3D11 mode result since 0.6.10 ended that way.
  - **A GPU hang.** One of two ui runs lost the device while pages switched mid-motion (removed, reason DEVICE_HUNG).
  - **Starts close together.** Two copies started at once failed in 2 of 3 pairs, and a start a few seconds after the previous Player quit failed every time in the diagnosis runs, each with "Device failed error (87a0001)" at the first frame. 0x087A0001 is DXGI_STATUS_OCCLUDED, a status rather than an error; Unity 6000.2 treats it as a device failure. The upgrade assessment found the fix in Unity 6000.3.11f1's release notes (UUM-135201). 20 s apart (both then running) and 60 s after a quit they start. D3D11 pairs pass.
  - **Memory.** Private memory about 1 GB higher: gameplay peaked at 8.37 GB against 7.41 GB on D3D11, 0.2 GB under the suite's 8 GiB gate.
- **Diagnosis.**
  - Neither graphics jobs off (threaded rendering) nor moving the DLSS and FSR plugins out stopped the quitting crash.
  - NVIDIA Overlay's capture module (`nvspcap64.dll`) was loaded in every D3D12 Player; turning the overlay off is the user's setting and was not tried.
  - Retest, morning of November 7. The user switched NVIDIA Overlay off, but `nvspcap64.dll` still loaded. After that morning's Windows update (system DLLs 10.0.26100.9836 to .9871; `D3D12Core.dll` unchanged), `d12-01` failed at the first frame on 6 of 6 starts made one at a time, each with 087a0001, as were 3 starts with the window brought to the front, and `d11-02` started 2 of 2. The exit crash could not be retested (`results.md` in the trial's evidence).
  - The D3D12 debug layer is not installed, so `-force-d3d12-debug` adds nothing. The driver is 32.0.16.1074.
  - Unity 6000.2 is end of life on Unity's issue tracker: it gets no more fixes. A Unity upgrade with a fresh trial is the way back to ray tracing.
- **Tooling kept** (default D3D11, so builds are unchanged).
  - `PolarHdrpBuild` takes `-polarGraphicsApi d3d11|d3d12` and restores the project's API list after building. A D3D12 build writes "Start in DirectX 11 (safe mode).cmd" beside the exe, and the build record lists the APIs; `PolarMvpBuild`'s contract gate accepts exactly the list asked for.
  - `PolarHdrpBenchmark` accepts D3D12, and the benchmark's `result.json` records `api`.
  - `Tools/polar_v7_player_run.py` fails a mode whose Player ran on another API than the build's first and records `graphicsApi` per mode.

## Natural snow and rock edges, November 6, 2026 (0.6.125-test)

The user's view from 170 m over pond 33 showed rock and snow edges no real hill draws. Render only: rules, colliders, saves, world hashes, navigation and `SnowAt`'s callers are unchanged; the drawn snow depth (`PolarSnowDepth`) and the moss scatter follow the shader's new coverage, as before.
- **What was wrong.**
  - The distant cliff band was a dark ribbon of one width snaking over the hills: its core was always rock.
  - Wind-bare crests were long, evenly grey sheets with straight edges: the crest cover (`PolarLandscape.GroundCover`) rises from 0 to 1 within a few metres, so the old two-octave edge noise moved the edge by about a metre and opened no holes.
  - Slope rock followed one contour of the slope, so mountainsides showed rock edges as long smooth lines.
  - The rock map's 19 m repeat drew a cobble of snowy cracks over whole faces from a kilometre.
  - The gravel and tundra maps' repeats drew fine stripes over bare ground seen from afar.
- **Slope rock** (`PolarRockCover` in `PolarData.hlsl`, mirrored by `PolarSnowDepth.Mask`; takes the normal and world height now).
  - The threshold follows ribs and gullies down the fall line (side projections, 33 and 11 m across, 17-33 m down), snow ledges along the contours (50 m along, 6 m up) and an 80 m wander (`PolarRockFall`).
  - The deepest gullies hold snow on rock less steep than about 70 degrees.
  - Ledges and bedding hold snow even on sheer faces (20 m along, 4.5 m up, where their noise is in its top fifth or so), so an escarpment of even height reads as rock with snow on its ledges, not one ribbon.
- **The distant cliff band** (`PolarBandCover`). Its edge wanders over 60, 17 and 5 m, from the core alone to the whole fade. It also thins out over 110 and 32 m into snowed-over stretches where that noise is low.
- **Bare ground** (`PolarCover`, mirrored by `PolarSnowDepth.Bare`, which the snow depth and the moss scatter share).
  - Four octaves (90, 37, 14 and 3.4 m) on a lower base (cover × .65 − .25) break crests into gravel patches with snow lying between them and into their edges.
  - The painted edge is .20-.34 (was .15-.40).
  - The gravel and tundra maps fade to their mean as a tile shrinks from 64 to 16 px, and the bare ground's tone wanders over 30 and 9 m (.72-1.15).
- **Rock map at distance.** From 300 m the 19 m repeat gives way to one 8.3 times the first (58 m), alone beyond 600 m, for both the map and its perturbation.
- **Result.** 19 views in the b3-ground, b4-intact-ice and new b3-snow-rock sections against a genuine before (evidence `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-06/snow-rock-natural`):
  - The user's view: the ribbons on the mountain become ribs, couloirs and snowed ledges, and the grey crest sheets become patches. The white dotted line the user saw in a grey gully lay on bare ground that is now snow (the probe found no river or crack there).
  - Cliff lines from 180-600 m break into segments with snowed ledges. The far escarpment is mottled rock and snow instead of a flat dark sheet.
  - Rock and bare ground are rarer overall: the hills are snowier.
- **Checks.** Gameplay 390 and performance 79 pass on `sr-01` (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.125-test/sr-01` and `sr-01-pc2`); the first performance run stopped on the pond check at +0.74 ms main thread, and the rerun measured -0.21 ms GPU and +0.22 ms main thread. On the CPU the cover terms run only when a near chunk is built (its snow depth) and when the moss is scattered, not per frame.
- **Capture section b3-snow-rock** (`PolarArtCapture.SnowRock.cs`, capture only), on the 14:00 clear fixture:
  - from 40 m over the home front on four headings;
  - the nearest wind-bare crest (cover .45 or more) from 250, 90 and 30 m;
  - the nearest sheer cliff line from 600 and 180 m and along it;
  - the highest ground within 2.5 km from 1 km and 350 m;
  - the nearest steep slope from 30 m.

  Its notes also list the rivers and cracks the ii-distant eye sees.
- **Left as they were.**
  - Close up, a crest's snow edge over gravel is a soft wash over a metre or two, since the cover now changes slowly there.
  - The near cliff faces keep their even height (world geometry).
  - The far line in ii-distant-far is a straight seam with a lit edge and was not diagnosed.
  - Rocky-tile patches on flat ground keep their crisp outline and cobble map.

## Anti-aliasing and upscaling, November 6, 2026 (0.6.122-test)

M1c of the graphics core plan's M1 (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-graphics-core.md`). Render only: the rules, colliders, saves and world hashes are unchanged. The settings are in [player settings](polar-settings.md#anti-aliasing-render-scale-and-sharpness-november-6-2026-graphics-core-m1c-06122-test).
- **Methods** (`PolarHdrpPresentation.Upscale`, run on every graphics change and on each new world camera).
  - **TAA** is the camera's TAA as before. Dynamic resolution stays off, so it renders at full size, with sharpening .5 at Medium. Sharpness sets TAA's sharpening to 0, .25, .5 or .8.
  - **SMAA** is HDRP's SMAA at High.
  - **The upscalers** turn on the camera's `allowDynamicResolution` and set HDRP's software dynamic resolution to the chosen percentage (`DynamicResolutionHandler.SetDynamicResScaler`):
    - TAA upscale uses HDRP's TAAU filter.
    - FSR 1 uses HDRP's edge-adaptive upscale, with RCAS sharpness 0, .5, .92 or 1.
    - DLSS uses custom quality from the scale (MaximumQuality, Balanced or MaximumPerformance). It follows the game's percentage, not DLSS's optimal one, with sharpening 0, .3, .5 or .8.
    - FSR 2 uses Quality, Balanced or Performance, with the same sharpening.
- **HDRP asset.** Dynamic resolution is enabled: software, 50-100%, with mip bias, and DLSS then FSR 2 as the advanced upscalers. Nothing changes while TAA or SMAA is chosen, because the camera's `allowDynamicResolution` is off then.
  - DLSS and FSR 2 come from Unity's built-in modules `com.unity.modules.nvidia` and `com.unity.modules.amd`. They ship with the Editor, so nothing was downloaded.
  - The package carries `nvngx_dlss.dll`, `NVUnityPlugin.dll` and `AMDUnityPlugin.dll`.
- **Results.**
  - Gameplay 390 under TAA, DLSS, TAA upscale and FSR 1 on `aa-05` (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.122-test/aa-05`, `aa-05-dlss`, `aa-05-taaupscale`, `aa-05-fsr1`). Under FSR 2, and ui 463 under TAA, they ran on `aa-01`.
- **Not yet.**
  - The front page's per-method screenshots and GPU times in the Player, which follow the display confirm and need the window in front (DXGI 887a0022 without it, as on a `po-03` control).
  - M1d: the benchmark and per-preset performance.

## Graphics presets, November 6, 2026 (0.6.120-test)

M1a of the graphics core plan's M1 (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-graphics-core.md`; the user asked for settings like Forza Horizon 6's). Render only: rules, colliders, saves, world hashes, the near window, `SnowAt`, the visibility table and fog density are the same at every level. The model, file fields and option table are in [player settings](polar-settings.md#graphics-tab-november-6-2026-graphics-core-m1a-06120-test).
- **Presets.** Low, Medium, High, Ultra, Extreme and Custom, over twelve options (`PolarGraphicsProfile`, pure). High is the game as it drew before: HDRP's volume tiers at their default (Medium) and every scale 1. Unity's quality level stays the top one (Ultra), which Players and captures always used; `PolarGraphics.Set` re-applies it.
- **Owners.** `PolarHdrpPresentation.Graphics` (on `PolarGraphics.Changed`): cloud steps, SSAO, contact shadows, SSGI (off, or Low at half resolution), SSR, fog tier (custom above High), motion blur intensity (a `MotionBlur` override on the global volume, .5 as HDRP's default profile at High), sun shadow size. Every frame: `PolarSnowTracks` (relief reach, and `PolarWorldStreamer.ReliefDetail` for the twins' segment length), `PolarSnowLayers` (rate and cap together; buffers sized for Extreme), `PolarArtWater.Weather` (sea tessellation). `PolarGraphics` itself: mip limit, anisotropic filtering, and the shadow size of the stove lamp and tent burner (`PolarGraphics.Lamp`). `PolarSnowTracks` no longer reads Unity's quality level.
- **HDRP asset.** `maxDirectionalShadowMapResolution` 4096 (was 2048), for Ultra's sun shadows.
- **High is today.** All 143 art captures (b5-snow, b4-intact-ice, b6-habitat, weather) after the change against a genuine before: median 0.10% of pixels over 8 levels, the same as two after runs of the same code against each other (0.08%). The live-blizzard frames move 4-9% either way (particle timing), and the damaged-roof interior 37-75% (eye adaptation and haze). One trap: b6 run alone and b6 run after three other sections differ by 5-13% on exteriors (TAA and motion blur history), so before and after were both run alone (0.2-0.4%).
- **Performance at High.** Every paced view equals `ht-01` (P95 16.68-16.73 ms). Uncapped, the pond and sea views on `gp-01` are +0.05 to +0.73 ms GPU P95 over a control run of `ht-01` an hour later, while `ht-01` itself moved +0.37 to +0.84 ms between its two runs. Not paired; nothing High draws differs (each value checked, and `SetShadowResolution` writes only on a change).
- **The other presets.** The same 143 views at Low, Medium, Ultra and Extreme (`-polarGraphicsPreset`; contact sheets in the evidence). Thin-ice cracks, open water, crevasse and roof edges and the lit window at night read at Low. Low has no contact shadows and draws flat snow, fewer flakes and streaks and no ice reflections. Ultra and Extreme reach further with relief and draw more streaks. Day interiors are 25% brighter at Low (.191 against High's .153 mean) and 9% darker at Ultra (.139): with SSGI off, indirect light falls back to the sky probe; at HDRP's High SSGI tier, more of the dim room is found. Night interiors stay within 5%.
- **Not yet.** The readout and video memory meter (M1b), anti-aliasing and upscaling choices and flag motion vectors (M1c), the benchmark (M1d), and per-preset performance gates (Ultra at most about 1.5 times High, Low and Medium about .6 and .8): measured in M1d with the paired pattern.

## Light, reflections and hut shadows, November 6, 2026 (0.6.118-test)

M6 of the graphics core plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-graphics-core.md`), which completes the plan's four milestones. Render only: the rules, colliders, saves, world hashes, the huts' collision boxes and their LOD1 and LOD2 are unchanged.
- **A probe in the hut you are in** (`PolarHdrpPresentation.HutProbe`). One HDRP reflection probe, made on first use as "Hut reflection", stands over the inside of the hut the eye is in (the `IndoorWeight` box, hut-local x ±2.95, y .25-4.435, z -.14-8.14), once the eye has adapted to the inside (indoor weight 1). Its box is 6.2 x 4.4 x 8.6 m round hut-local (0, 2.34, 4) with a 10 cm blend, so walls, floor and ceiling take it whole, and box-projected (HDRP's proxy is the influence box). It is realtime on demand with time slicing (a face a frame) and culls the world camera's layers, never the hands or the interface. It renders again on coming in, when the stove lights or goes out, when the door opens or shuts, each game hour, and when the exposure has moved a quarter stop; outdoors it is off. Inside, SSR's missed rays and rough reflections take the room instead of the sky: the heater reads as dark metal and the cold sky wash on the wood is gone.
- **Why it waits for the eye.** HDRP stores a cube probe unexposed (its frame settings leave probes out of exposure control), but the stove lamps (`2^min(ev,9)`) and the sky's indoor balance follow the eye's exposure. A probe rendered from outside, or before the eye had adapted, lit the room up to 250 times too bright at night (a mean of .043 became .64 in the first trial).
- **SSGI's missed rays.** While the probe is in use SSGI's missed rays take the sky alone (`rayMiss` Sky). The accepted daylight inside comes from them, and taking the probe's dim room made day interiors four times darker. Outdoors they stay on HDRP's default (reflection probes and sky); Sky everywhere made exteriors about 4% darker. Before the probe is on, a missed ray already took the sky, so the switch at indoor weight 1 shows no step.
- **Hut shadows** (`PolarHabitatAuthor.Hut`, the LOD0 `Shadow` mesh `<hut>-shadow0`). The shadow-only proxy now also carries the furniture as boxes (wall shelves, bunk posts, bench, storage shelf, and the table's legs as well as its top; canvas and ladder rungs left out) and the fallen boards' own meshes as they lie, so the stove and the sun cast from them near. LOD0 still draws every part without shadows, so each shadow face is still one mesh and one draw (Hut1 2,700 indices to 3,852).
- **Results.** Day interiors are darker and warmer: the captures' mean brightness falls 16-45% (the heater by day, unlit, .465 to .257; the room .190 to .142; Pole Station .271 to .227), the wood is warmer and the heater reads as dark metal. Night interiors with the stove lit are within 4%; fallen boards and shelf brackets cast the stove's shadows (variant 1 at night). Exteriors and pond ice are unchanged.
- **Checked, not changed.**
  - SSGI ray thickness .05 instead of HDRP's .1: no visible change, so it stays at the default.
