  - The stove's room fill (`PolarSiteView.FillShare` .7): the probe adds little at night, so it stays.
  - The stove light's shape radius: the shadow filtering quality is Medium, which ignores it.
  - SSR's PBR accumulation against Approximation on the pond ice and in the hut (the capture-only switch `-polarArtSsr`): the ice is nearly identical, its reflections slightly darker (`ii-standing` mean .503 to .493). No gain, so Approximation stays.
  - Hut timber (G04.01): followed in 0.6.119-test ([huts](polar-huts.md), top section).
- **Capture section** `b6-hut-light` (`PolarArtCapture.HutLight.cs`): the home hut at midnight with the stove lit and the door shut, three views with SSGI on and off, and the probes in use in the notes.

## Weather that has places in it, November 5, 2026 (0.6.117-test)

M5 of the graphics core plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-graphics-core.md`). Render only: the rules, the weather's timing, visibility table, wind and gusts, the cirrus pass and the clouds are unchanged.
- **The lee behind shelter** (`PolarSnowLayers.LateApply`, `LeeAt`). Huts and pitched tents within 60 m of the player each cast a wedge downwind (from the displayed heading): 24 m from a hut's centre, 4 m wide there and widening .15 m a metre; 9 m and 1.6 m for a tent; full strength to their top and fading over 2 m above it, falling off as the square root of the distance along it. In the wedge ground drift is drawn up to 95% fainter and held back by up to 80% of its travel each frame, and blowing streaks up to 90% fainter (their alpha drawn again from each particle's seed, as the layer's .35-.7). The existing loops over the drift and the sheltered layers carry it; no particle is added.
- **Snow off the roof** (`PolarSnowLayers`, layer "Snow roof powder", up to 700 particles). In gusts over .45 the ridge of the nearest hut within 60 m (hut-local `(0, 4.43, .2)` to `(0, 4.43, 7.8)`) sheds powder at up to 220 a second, scaled by the drift's strength for the weather, riding the blowing snow's wind in the drift's wisp material. Indoors it is clipped like the other layers.
- **Storm curtains** (`PolarStormFog`). From heavy snow up, one Multiply local volumetric fog round the eye (352 x 240 x 384 m, past the 150 m voxel range on every side) multiplies the volumetric storm fog by 2 x a periodic 32³ noise of mean .5 (`Alpha8`, mean free path .5), with a contrast rising to .9 in a blizzard, rewritten only in 1/16 steps. The mean, the visibility table, the fog past 150 m and the sky's fog are unchanged. The volume is turned to the base wind heading (`WindDirection`) and set on its 16 x 40 x 48 m tiles (across, up, downwind) round an anchor moving downwind at 3-13 m/s with the gust, so the curtains stay in the world and travel with the wind. Fog over snow looks the same at any density, so the curtains show against near dark things (huts, rock, people), which come and go as they pass; a test at ten times the density hid the hut 14 m away.
- **Frost smoke** (`PolarStormFog.Smoke`). In clear to medium weather (none above storm .5), up to eight Additive volumes 30 x 4 x 30 m stand from 1 m under to 3 m over open water: the nearest wet cells of a 24 m lattice within 150 m (`PolarWorldController.WaterLevel`, four cells a frame) and broken pond plates. They rise at .3 m/s and drift downwind through a wispy mask, mean free path 4 m divided by `6/(6+wind)`, thinning with height. The first placement 1.9 m up put the volumes over the eye of a swimmer and showed nothing.
- **The window at night.** In medium snow the window lamp lights the snow but draws no visible beam in the air; left unchanged, as the plan allows.

## Feet on the bent ice, November 5, 2026 (0.6.116-test)

M3 of the graphics core plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-graphics-core.md`). Render only: the rules, colliders, saves, world hashes and the player's eye are unchanged.
- **Feet on the drawn ice** (`PolarThinIceRuntime.DrawnBelow`, `PolarFootPlanting.Target`). Since 0.6.106-test a loaded plate's collider sags whole (`.03 f`) while the drawn sheet bends round the bowls (`PolarMeshModification`). Feet planted on the collider stood off the drawn ice:
  - a walker's own feet up to about 1 cm over the bowl;
  - a second walker on the same plate up to 3 cm into the ice, since the drawn sheet stays up away from the bowl;
  - someone on the plate beside it up to 3.2 cm over the ice, since the bend crosses the seam and that plate does not sag.

  `DrawnBelow` repeats the bend on the CPU from the same four bowls (last frame's, as drawn): the sum of `D e^-r (cos r + sin r)` times the shore hold (`PolarArtIceCaps.Bend`), at most 3.2 cm. To that it adds the world height of the collider's transform, which is its cap's sag (a hole's rim collider is the cap's child), because the drawn sheet keeps its mesh's own height. Where the foot's ray meets a collider with `PolarIceSupport` on a loaded pond in HDRP, foot planting lowers the foot by it, and the body with the deeper foot; elsewhere it is 0. At pond 33's halfway frame the drawn sheet lies 0.77 cm under the collider at the player's feet.
- **No motion vectors for the bend.** At walking pace (about 1.4 m/s) a weight moves its bowl about 2.3 cm a frame at 60 fps. The bowl's steepest slope (2.9%, at `r = π/4` with `D = .045`) then moves a point of the sheet up or down by at most .7 mm a frame. A plate loading at pond 33's rate (`.045 / loadSeconds` a second, hold 1.95 s) moves it .4 mm a frame. From a standing eye 2 m away that is under .4 px a frame, so the caps stay on Camera motion and `ThinIce.mat` keeps no motion-vector pass.
- **Bubble ovals: diagnosed, left as they are.** The bright ovals at a swimmer's eye (open since 0.6.114-test) are not on the hole faces (`PolarIceCut`). They are the intact ice's large bubble layer (`PolarIceBubbles`' 12 cm cells, bubbles 1.5-10 cm across with bright rims) on floating pieces' tops, which draw with the cap material, seen 1-2 m off at about 20 degrees.
  - A Schlick transmission on the bubbles (ice's F0 .018) was tried. It dims them by about 10% there and makes no visible change in the other views, so it was left out.
  - Softening or shrinking that layer near the eye is a look choice still open.

## Snow you walk in, November 5, 2026 (0.6.115-test)

M2 of the graphics core plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-graphics-core.md`). Render only: the rules, colliders, saves, world hashes, walking effort and footstep kinds (`SnowAt`, `PolarSurface`) are unchanged.
- **Rims** (`PolarSnowTracks`, `PolarSnowTracks.compute`). A second 1024² `R32_SFloat` field of the same 4 cm texels, `_PolarTrackRim`, holds a rim weight (0-1). `Stamp` writes a ridge 1-10 cm out from the sole, highest 5.5 cm out, and presses an older rim flat under the sole. `Refill` lowers it by `_Rise/.3`, so a rim settles as a 0.3 m print fills, and clears it with the window as it clears the ceiling. The twin's domain stage (`PolarMeshModification.hlsl`) raises the drawn snow by `rim × PolarRimRise (.2) × min(E, .3)` where the ceiling stands above the snow (not inside a print), fading with the prints from 6 to 10 m; flattened ground (pond and gap edges, people lying, items) keeps no rims, as it keeps no prints. The pixel stage (`PolarData.hlsl`) adds the rims' slope from four more taps where a rim stands, out to the prints' 20 m.
- **Powder** (`PolarSnowTracks.Powder`). Each boot accepted by the footprint setting where the drawn snow is 12 cm deep or more (`SnowAt`, `PolarSurface`'s snow line) kicks 6-12 grains off its toe along the foot's heading, more in fresh snow: one pooled particle system (384) on the drift's sky-lit card, clipped by the huts' and tents' volumes, .5-.9 s, a little gravity and drag.
- **Fresh and old snow** (`PolarHdrpPresentation.snowFresh`, global `_PolarSnowNew` = fresh less .5, unset = the look before). Falling snow freshens it over about an hour in medium snow; without snow it ages back to the old look (.5) in six hours and to old snow in twelve. Not saved: a load or a jump of more than an hour starts it at 1 in falling snow and .5 otherwise. Fresh snow lies as powder over the wind-packed patches and narrows the crust (the `packed` and `crust` thresholds shift), is up to 3% brighter, rougher, and glints more; old snow widens packed snow and crust and is glossier.
- **The pond-bank lip** (open since 0.6.108-test). `PolarArtIceLip.DepthAt` reads the drawn snow over the ice's top from the nearest column's profile as last written (the shore height `f·h0`, its reach onto the ice and `1 - s³`), and `PolarThinIceRuntime.LipAt` returns it on a loaded pond, cleared under tents (`PolarSnowShelter.TentClear`). Items put down on pond ice (`PolarMvpController.Seat`) and people lying (`PolarCharacterPresentation`) rest on it; writing a lip bumps `PolarSnowShelter.ClearRevision` (`Reseat`) so both are seated again.
- **Left as they were.** Back-lit crests (optional in the plan) were not added. The capture's low-sun trail shots show no trail in either phase (`SetHour` jumps the clock, which fills the prints), so rims at a low sun are seen only in the day shots.

## Bolder pond hole rims, November 4, 2026 (0.6.114-test)

0.6.112-test's rims made bolder at the user's request after its before/after shots ("make it bolder"): a broken, chunky crust instead of a smooth bead, chips that read at standing distance, a stronger waterline, and floating pieces' sides chipped and shaded like the rim. Render only: the rules, colliders, saves, world hashes, `PolarThinIce.Hole` and the pieces' rest poses are unchanged.
- **Runs** (`PolarArtIcePondView.Column`). The lip and the chip no longer vary column by column. They hold over runs of the line, about 38 cm on average (20-60 cm), and change at a run's end. The runs come from a warped periodic index round the loop, `floor(u*count + 1.2*Fbm(...)) mod count`, so the closing column is the first. Per run:
  - the lip stands 1-6 cm back from the line (was .3-2 cm);
  - in six runs in ten the chip, .8-2.7 cm down, cuts 1-3.5 cm under the lip; in the rest it steps out up to 2.6 cm;
  - a notch .6-2 cm deeper still sits at the waterline.

  The foot and bottom are as before. Every offset scales with the rim's width and keeps 1.2 cm inside it, so a merged hole's straight cut is unchanged.
- **The crust** (`Section.crust`, `Join`'s `Facet`).
  - It lies on the strip in runs of its own, about 34 cm each. Half carry it and the rest are bare.
  - It stands .5-3.6 cm proud and 2-10 cm wide, in lumps whose height, width and crest change from column to column. A crack (a share of .3) breaks it one column in five.
  - Its front reaches up to 1.3 cm over the lip, never past the line.
  - Each of its three bands (back slope, crest and front) is a flat facet with its own vertices and normal, so it reads as broken snow and ice rather than a rounded bead. The front faces the hole even where it overhangs the lip.
  - Seven runs in ten are snow and the rest are ice. Colour b is 2 + the run's snow, which `PolarThinIceSurface` takes as a floor under the tongues' snow. The grain is coarser (.82-1.05) and a crumb tints the normal.
- **Waterline** (`PolarIceCut`). The wet band is near black (.025, .038, .048) and 8-16 mm high (was 6-12 mm), with a brighter glaze line over it (.8 of a pale ice colour).
- **Floating pieces' sides** (`PolarArtIcePondView.Fracture`, `Ragged`, `Buffer.Piece`).
  - Each piece's outline is cut about every 9 cm. Each cut except the corners is pulled up to 2.5 cm (less on a small piece) toward the piece's middle, so the edge is toothed and the outline stays star-shaped for the top's fan.
  - Each side keeps its own face normal, and its uv0.x runs along the whole outline.
  - The sides are drawn as hole faces: colour b 10 + their rest height over the water, a .8 under the top. So they carry the same milky-to-clear ice, bubbles, wet band, glaze and turquoise below the water. Before they had one uniform band.
- **Cost.**
  - FXC counts 3,422 instruction slots in `PolarThinIceSurface` across all branches (3,421 on 0.6.112-test).
  - Geometry grows by the crust's own facet vertices (up to 12 a sample, in half the runs) and the pieces' toothed outlines (about 9 cm a side quad instead of one per Voronoi edge).
  - Nothing runs per frame; a rebuild happens only when the broken set changes.
  - In the performance mode on `rb-01`, pond 33's GPU P95 is 8.50 ms intact from the bank, 11.23 ms breaking through and 10.95 ms swimming in the hole, against `rm-02`'s 7.63, 10.44 and 10.29 ms (not paired). The whole run was slower: the sea view, which has no hole, is 7.29 ms against 6.28 ms, and the intact view draws no rim either. Over the same run's intact view, breaking through and swimming cost 2.73 and 2.45 ms, against 2.81 and 2.66 ms on `rm-02`, so the rims are no slower.
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-04/bolder-hole-rims`. It uses `PolarArtCapture` section `b4-deep-water`: before is `bold-before` on 0.6.113-test's source, and after is `bold-after2` after one tuning pass (the first pass's crust was a wide continuous band and its wet band heavier). There are 9 pairs:
  - the rim standing, also at night;
  - a near crop;
  - the second rim view (E02);
  - the waterline and oblique views of a piece;
  - from under the water;
  - the merged rim.

## Gap walls: crevasses, bay ice fronts and lead rock, November 4, 2026 (0.6.113-test)

M4 of the ice-edge plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-ice-edge-upgrade.md`, concepts E05, E06, E09), which completes the plan: near chunks draw the walls of dry cracks, coastal bays and interior leads in relief instead of as flat quads. Render only: colliders, navigation, rules, saves and world hashes do not change.
- **Flat walls kept for collision.** In near chunks with HDRP gap art (`gapArt`, fine meshes) the flat walls' triangles (one double-sided quad per 2 m of outline, `BuildGapWalls`) go to their own list, are culled as the rest (`Cull`) and are appended last as submesh 1 (`MeshBuffer.wallStart`): the MeshCollider, built from the chunk mesh, still cooks them, and the renderer, with one material, draws submesh 0 only. A distant step-4 block over the near window (`holed`) leaves out the segments whose middle lies under it. The memory counters (`PolarMvpAcceptance`, `PolarStreamingValidation`, `PolarCentralMemoryCapture`, `PolarExperienceCapture.World`, `PolarTerrainRoundTripCapture`) count every submesh.
- **Walls in relief** (`PolarWorldStreamer.GapRelief.cs`): a child "Gap walls in relief" of each near chunk in the chunk's material, casting two-sided shadows (the flat walls no longer cast, and a wall on the sun's side faces away from it). Columns every 0.8 m along the outline (`Knot`); rows at set depths under the lip (`Depths`, 0 to 160 m), rows round the water (1.5 and .9 m over it, .9 m under it) and over a dry crack's floor (1.8 and .3 m), and the foot. Each point is set back into the bank by a function of the gap's shape, the knot and the depth only, so neighbouring chunks meet bit for bit; a segment is drawn by the near chunk holding its middle (`Holder`), and the relief eases back to the collider's plane over two knots where that chunk changes (`ends`), as a distant block's flat wall may meet it there.
  - Set back: dry cracks up to .15 m in the top 1.5 m and .4 m deeper; bays .1-.6 m in facets of about 3 m; leads .25 m. Nothing at the lip, within .9 m of the water (climbing out) or within .3 m of a dry crack's floor.
  - A dry crack at least 1.5 m wide hangs a cornice of packed snow .1-.25 m out over the crack, its nose .12 m under the lip and gone by .42 m: the one place the relief stands proud of the collider. The width is the world's crack record (`PolarCrack.widths`), mapped onto the outline as `PolarCompactWorld.CrackOutline` builds it.
  - Vertices carry the flat walls' colours (r 1 + metres under the lip, a 1000 + metres over the water) with g .9 and b .02 on bays, and the segment's direction along the wall in the tangent.
- **Shading** (`PolarGapSurface` in `PolarData.hlsl`). Relief walls read the distance along the wall from their tangent, flat walls from their normal.
  - Dry cracks: firn over the top 1.1-1.9 m, bluer and denser ice below it that darkens over about 6 m, snow on faces turned up.
  - Bays (colour b .02, flat and relief alike): a glacier ice front. White firn over the top 1.5-2.5 m; white-blue strata 1.6 m deep with a dirty band now and then; calving columns about 2.7 m across that wander, lean and shade apart, with broken seams; mottled and matte (smoothness .32-.55); bluer and darker toward the water, turquoise under it, wet at the water and rimed above.
  - Leads keep the rock, the wet band and the rime.
- **Cost.** At the capture's bay stand on seed 777, 19,086 relief vertices in 8 near chunks; at its lead stand, 11,571 in 6. The performance mode has no gap-wall view, so their cost is not measured apart; its hut-to-hut walk, which streams near chunks with gaps, holds P95 16.67 ms (16.69 ms on 0.6.110-test).
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-04/gap-walls`: `PolarArtCapture` section `b8-ice-edge` before (`ie-m4-before3`, taken with M4 out of the tree) and after (`ie-m4-after5`). The pairs show the crack from its lip, down into it and along it; the bay's wall from the water, along it and at its foot; and the lead's wall from the water and along it.
  - The section's bay and lead shots gained `-near` and `-near-along` views of the wall under the bank stand, since the `-foot` views look at a far wall that distant blocks draw flat. The section also logs the relief's vertices.
  - The first after capture drew the bay as a pale, flat wall with faint bands. The next drew straight calving seams, then saturated blue ones on a smooth, plastic face. Hence the wandering, broken seams, the mottling, the rougher normals and the matte front.
  - The capture showed the bay wall at the stand 155 m tall. There the rows ended at 74 m, and the relief stood up to about .1 m back at the water. Hence the rows to 160 m and round the water.
  - A review found the relief casting shadows only from its front: under a low sun, light reached the far wall and floor through a bank near the window's edge. It also found dry cracks' relief still set back at the floor, a slot up to about .2 m. Hence the two-sided shadows and the floor rows.
- **Results**: gameplay 390, physical 3959, performance 79 and weather 80 pass on `ie-09` (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.113-test/ie-09`); world hash BF321ACDC2E7B04D unchanged; the hut-to-hut walk P95 16.67 ms (0.6.110-test's `ie-05`: 16.69 ms), the coast's sea view P95 7.05 ms uncapped.

## Pond hole rims, November 4, 2026 (0.6.112-test)

A broken plate's hole now ends in a chipped section of ice with a crust ridge along the strip, after concepts E01 and E02 (`docs/art/ice-edge-concepts`). This is the pond-holes line of the ice-edge plan, done by the thin-ice session. Render only: the rules, colliders, saves, world hashes and `PolarThinIce.Hole` are unchanged.
- **The section** (`PolarArtIcePondView.Column`, `Join` in `PolarArtIcePond.cs`; replaces `Face`). It has one column at each sample of the hole's line (every 12 cm or less), on the ray from the hole's centre. Each column has five rows, measured over the still water:
  - the lip, at the strip's top, 0.3-2 cm back from the line, with a 6 mm step in some runs;
  - a chip 0.6-1.8 cm down that cuts under or steps out;
  - a notch at the waterline;
  - a foot 0.8-1.6 cm under the water, up to 2 cm into the hole in shelf runs;
  - the bottom, the underside's edge 6 cm under the top.

  Nothing above the water lies past the line. Every offset scales with the rim's width, so a merged hole's rim, narrowing to nothing within 0.6 m of the open edge, ends in a straight cut.
- **Facets.** Each band between two columns is one flat facet with its own vertices, facing into the hole.
  - Colour b is 10 + the row's rest height over the water, and a is the row's contact shade. uv0 holds the metres along the line and below the face's top.
  - A corner column is built by both its edges from the same inputs (the next edge's top, colour and hashes), so the facets meet bit for bit.
  - Every vertex carries the sheet's (Bend, 1) in uv2 and bends with a loaded neighbour (thin ice M4).
  - A facet whose normal Unity would round to zero (under 1e-5 across, on edges under a millimetre long) is left out, so no facet draws a NaN normal.
- **The crust ridge.** It lies on the strip, buried 4 mm at its back, up to 1.6 cm proud and 3-11 cm wide. Its front reaches up to 1 cm over the lip, never past the line.
  - Its share (colour b 2 + share, g its height over the strip) follows two Fbm runs about 1.2 m long and the shore drift, so it breaks into runs.
  - Its vertices bend at their own place.
- **Shading.**
  - **Hole faces (`PolarIceCut`, `_PolarIcePart` 1 with colour b over 5).** Above the water they are milky to clear blue-white ice in conchoidal shells, with columns of bubbles, milkiest in the top centimetre and on upward chips. A dark wet band runs 6-12 mm over the waterline under a glaze line, so it shows over pieces jammed against the face; below the water the ice is dark turquoise.
    - The waterline follows the bend's drop (uv2.x). Smoothness stays at .89 or less, under HDRP SSR's .9.
    - The thin faces glow with a share of the sky's ambient light (`PolarIceLit`, `PolarIceFaceGlow`). Undersides keep `PolarIceGlow` unchanged.
  - **The ridge** (`PolarThinIceSurface`, colour b over 1.5). It is the tongues' snow where a tongue reaches it or ends within a few centimetres. Elsewhere it is the ice's own, with rime in patches 10-40 cm long, grained at about 6 mm, and it stands proud of the flood.
    - The first draft took the snow's colour wherever drifted snow was likely nearby, which drew a white tube round the hole on bare ice.
  - **Unchanged.** Floating pieces' sides (colour b 1), part 2 (lead and river shore ice) and caps.
- **Cost.**
  - Geometry: about 24 vertices a sample instead of 8; a 5 m hole's rim is about 4k vertices.
  - Main thread: nothing per frame; a rebuild happens only when the broken set changes.
  - GPU: the faces are 3-6 cm slivers. FXC counts about 570 more instruction slots (3,421 against 2,853) in `PolarThinIceSurface` across all branches. In the performance mode on `rm-02`, pond 33's GPU P95 is 7.63 ms intact from the bank, 10.44 ms breaking through and 10.29 ms swimming in the hole, against `hr-01`'s 7.93, 10.89 and 10.82 ms (not paired): no slower.
- **Known limits.**
  - Pieces' sides kept their old uniform band, since their origin is no waterline reference (shaded as hole faces from 0.6.114-test).
  - uv0's run jumps once a hole at its first sample (a noise phase only).
  - A shore edge's strip stands 2 mm over a plate edge's, and a corner takes the next edge's height.
  - Worlds before v22 (0.65 m water) would fold the section; built games delete them.
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-04/pond-hole-rims`: `PolarArtCapture` section `b4-deep-water`, before (`rim-before`) and after (`rim-after4`, after three tuning passes). The new shots are:
  - the rim standing, as E01 (also at night, overcast and in a storm), at a swimmer's eye, along the waterline both ways (E02), from under the water, at the shore and from 25 m;
  - the merged hole's rim and waterline.

## River, lake and lead shore ice, November 4, 2026 (0.6.110-test)

M3 of the ice-edge plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-ice-edge-upgrade.md`, concepts E03, E04): shore ice along open rivers, lakes and interior leads is drawn as broken plates with thickness instead of an even ribbon of 2 m quads with a gap after about every other one. Render only: no colliders, rules, saves or world hashes change, and the river, lake and lead water is drawn as before.
- **Plates** (`PolarIceEdge.Run` with the `River` and `Lead` profiles, in the edge-ice material, kind 9): skewed cuts every 2.5-8 m (leads 4-10 m); 6-16 cm thick and 3-7 cm out of the water, tucked .3 m under the bank; stations every 1.5 m (leads 3 m), so the water edge is jagged; neighbours differ in width by up to 40% (leads 20%); some cuts open a crack of water and some plates are rafted onto the next; snow lies on them by the bank and thins to frosted and clear ice at the water. A river plate's bank edge sinks to the water (`Profile.sink`), so a bank that slopes under it or a lake end's level floor never shows its open side, and a rafted plate rises only toward the water; a lead's wall hides the edge. Plates do not drift off the bank: open plates have no bank face or underside, so a drifted one showed both and passed through its neighbours. About a third of a river bank is bare and a fifth of a lead's wall; a thin plate floats loose off a river bank now and then, no farther out than half way to the axis.
- **Lake ends.** At each lake end whose land holds the water (`PolarLeads.Held`) the ice runs round the end (`PolarArtLeads.End`): the waterline is found in metre steps out from the end and bisected (within Held's 5 cm), inside the drawn water's end square, easing in from the banks' half. River ends still taper over 3 m: a river's water slopes to its ends, and a level end row added to the river water for them (so their ice would float) laid a level sheet over lower land at an upstream end on seed 777, so it was dropped.
- **Built on loading workers**, a river each (`PolarLoadingParallel.For` in `PolarArtLeads.RiverPieces`), started at the top of `PolarLeads.Build` (`StartRiverShoreIce`) so they run while the leads and floes are built; `RiverShoreIce` takes their pieces after them. Pieces are about 64 m; each piece is drawn to .16 of the screen's height (about 350 m), and a second renderer sharing its mesh casts shadows to .9 of it (about 60 m).
- **Budget.** Seed 777: 324,529 shore-ice vertices in 986 pieces (rivers and lakes about 254,000, leads about 70,000), against 250,829 in 928 before, 1.29 times (the plan's 1.3 times; its 311,000 was the v19 world's, before v20 halved the area).
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-04/shore-ice`: `PolarArtCapture` section `b8-ice-edge` before (`ie-m3-before`) and after (`ie-m3-after5`): a river bend close, a bank from above, a river's end, a lake's end from above, a lead's wall foot and a river from its bank; the section's fixture now logs the shore-ice count. The first after capture drew flat glassy slabs with straight water edges (stations every 3 m) and the level end rows; the third showed a lead's foot nearly bare along one wall, a 14 m bare plate of the 6-18 m lead plates, so they are 4-10 m now. A review (`PolarIceEdge.Run` and the river pieces rerun on seed 777 outside Unity) found drifted plates' open sides out in the water (813 of 1,052 river plates more than 5 cm, 234 of 246 lead plates), rafted river plates' bank edges up to .28 m out of sloping banks, normal plates' bank edges up to 8.5 cm over open water at 941 of 5,276 bank points and most lake ends, and a single worker awaited at once: in the Player it took 1.67 s, mostly tracing 74 lake ends, and the load waited 1.45 s for it (build `ie-07`, which added the loading rows `proxies.river_ice`, `proxies.river_ice_wait`, `proxies.river_ice_keep` and `proxies.lead_ice`). Hence no drift, the sinking bank edge, and a river to each loading worker, started before the leads. Rerun outside Unity on seed 777 after the fixes: no river or lake plate's open side stands more than 2 cm out of the bank or the water (before: 3,351 bank-edge vertices of normal plates over 5 cm, 652 rafted plates and 813 drifted ones), one lead plate 5.8 cm at a corner where the wall turns 128 degrees, one loose plate inside another plate by more than 5 cm (42 before), and the vertex count unchanged.
- **Results**: gameplay 390 and weather 80 on `ie-08` (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.110-test/ie-08b`; a first `ie-08` run stopped once at the hut door, which did not swing open within its 5 s of real time, the first time that check has failed in any run there, and passed on `ie-05`, `ie-06` and the rerun); performance 79 on `ie-05` (`ie-05p`), which differs from `ie-08` only in the review's fixes (plate placement, with the same 324,529 vertices, and the loading workers): the coast's sea view +0.58 ms GPU P95 and -0.26 ms main (the median of 16 pairs), breaking through the ice +0.10 and +0.06 ms, the hut-to-hut walk P95 16.69 ms; world hash BF321ACDC2E7B04D unchanged. New-game loading (seed 777, a 1600x900 window, `Logs/PolarLoadingProfile/2026-11-04/cmp-m3-*`): on `ie-08` the river workers take 0.30 s off the main thread, the wait for them 0.03-0.05 s, keeping the pieces 0.03 s and the leads' ice 0.04 s; `load.proxies` 9.39 and 9.72 s against `hh-01`'s 9.02-9.11 s, in runs where world generation, which M3 does not touch, also ran 3-6 s slower (`ie-06`, the single worker: 10.60 and 10.88 s).

## Huts from Polar Habitat, November 4, 2026 (0.6.109-test)

M4 of the habitat plan: Hut0-4, HutFar0-4, HutDoor and Pole Station are written from the pack by `PolarHabitatAuthor.BuildHuts` ([huts](polar-huts.md#huts-and-pole-station-from-polar-habitat-november-4-2026-06109-test)). Render detail:
- **LODs** at the old screen heights (.13 / .035 / .001). LOD0 draws the pack's meshes as they are (67,188 triangles in Hut0, 52,308-64,752 in the others and 93,808 in Pole Station); LOD1 the outside (walls, roof, snow, framing, joinery, windows, marking), with the floor and lining in the holed and ruined huts (37,736-48,908 triangles; Pole Station 64,224); LOD2 and the far huts boxes in the pack's timber and the roof's pitch (816-1,128 triangles).
- **Shadows.** LOD0 and LOD1 cast none; a shadows-only mesh of boxes (walls, roof boards, base, door frame, and in LOD0 the berths and table top) casts them, since the stove lamp's soft shadows draw every caster six times.
- **Seams.** The pack's roof boards and floorboards leave seams that showed the sky and the snow under the hut; a box of half their thickness inside each roof run and inside the floor (`Seams`, dark timber) closes them.
- **Roof snow** is the pack's snow cap in `Wind-packed-snow`, baked into the hut's frame, since `PolarSurface` kind 8 shades in its object's frame.
- **Retired.** `PolarArtB6HutUv` and `PolarArtB6HutMaterials` (board UVs and pinned materials for the old shell) and `PolarHabitatAuthor.StripHutStoves` are gone; the old hut meshes (`Hut?-*.asset`) are deleted. The B6 materials stay for the dressing.
- **Frame time and loading.** Hut-to-hut P95 16.70 ms (paced), as `ss-01`'s; loading alternating with `ss-01` (seed 777, windowed 1600x900, two runs each): playable in 60.3-60.8 s (60.5-61.6 s), distant refinement complete in 177.8-178.5 s (177.6-178.1 s), P95 12.5 ms in all four (`Logs/PolarLoadingProfile/2026-11-04/cmp-hh-*`); the report's own flag fails on both builds, since `PolarLoadingProfile` still expects 21 people (20 since world v21), and its background check passes.
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-04/habitat-huts`: `PolarArtCapture` section `b6-habitat` (20 pairs) and an open door seen from inside, before and after.

## Snow on the pond ice and its shore, November 4, 2026 (0.6.108-test)

M5 of the thin-ice plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-thin-ice-upgrade.md`), the shore. Render only: the rules, colliders and saves are unchanged.
- **The lip.** Each loaded pond, except a river's window, carries the bank's snow onto its ice (`PolarArtIceLip`, in `PolarArtIcePond.cs`). It is a mesh drawn in the ground's own plain material (`PolarWorldStreamer.GroundMaterial`; the tessellated twin zeroes uv2), so it shades, gains relief and takes prints as the bank's snow does. The 16-sided outline's hard line is gone.
  - Columns every 0.25 m run radially from the pond's centre, so rows never cross. Each starts 2.4 m up the bank, 12 cm under the ground as sampled (`PolarSurfaceCracks.DrawnGround`); the near ground cut at the outline can lie up to about 7 cm lower. Each ends in a front 0.4-2 m onto the ice, set by noise and wider on the bank the wind comes over, tucked 1 cm under the ice's top.
  - The bank's snow depth 2.4 m up (`PolarWorldStreamer.SnowUncleared`, which leaves tents' clearing to the vertex stage) is held to the shore and then falls as 1 - t³ onto the ice. It is written as the ground's tangent.w, so the ground's own lift and bank shading draw it. A bank without snow keeps the whole lip under the ground.
  - On the ice, uv2 is (Bend, 2), so the lip bends with a loaded sheet (`PolarIceSheet`).
  - The front stops 15 cm short of every hole (`PolarThinIce.SupportAt`); a break refits only the columns near the new hole.
  - The ground is sampled about once a metre within the main-thread budget (`ice_lip`).
- **The ice.** `PolarThinIceSurface` loses the foggy shore drift (colour a) and gains:
  - **Tongues:** drift streaks about 6 by 0.8 m along the ground's wind. `_PolarIceWind` is set once per world in `PolarWorldStreamer.ConfigureGroundArt`: the sastrugi axis and the wrapped Datum. The tongues are long and many off the upwind bank, short off the downwind one, and gathered out on the pond into long streaks 4-10 m wide. Their edges are antialiased over a pixel and fade to their mean coverage far off. Their last few centimetres are thin, bluer snow.
  - **Snow in the cracks:** snow lies along about half of each pond's primary and secondary refrozen cracks, read in the one crack loop (`PolarIceCracks`' `rime`).
  - **Hoarfrost:** an apron 1-4 m along the bank and 5 cm round those cracks, a bloom of crystals mottled at 4 and 11 cm that is matte where it is thickest. A few crystals, and the tongues, glint on the ground snow's sparkle grid. Single-cell specks (the first draft) read as square pixels.
  - **Load cracks through the snow:** where snow covers a loaded plate, its stress cracks (`PolarStressFracture`) show as wet grey lines.
  - **Far off,** loaded ice eases from .95 to .9 smoothness and, past a 0.6 m pixel (about 80 m), to .62; distant ponds to .62. At grazing angles a .84-.9 sheet mirrors the bright sky as brightly as the snow, so the streaks vanished and the pond read as a pale-blue disc.
- **Distant ponds.** These have no ice map. Their uv0 carries the metres to the shore here and 4 m upwind (`PolarThinIceRuntime.BuildDistantPond`), and colour a is 1 except on a river's window. The same tongues, streaks and frost draw from them, and the lip is painted to the loaded lip's width.
- **Known differences.** The painted distant lip and the loaded lip mesh differ in shape at the switch, under a pixel or two at that range. Things put down within 2 m of the shore, and feet there, can sink into the lip by up to its height (render only: `SnowAt`, which seats them, does not know the lip). The lip mesh hides a loaded plate's cracks under it, as snow would.
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-04/pond-shore-snow`: `PolarArtCapture` section `b4-intact-ice` before (`ss-before`, 0.6.107-test's code with the new shots) and after (`ss-after6`). The new shots are the lip low, close and from 5 m overhead, and the pond from 170 m (loaded) and 320 m (distant) back on the hills.
- **Results.** Gameplay 390, continue 36 and performance 79 pass on `ss-01` (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.108-test/ss-01`). Pond 33's GPU P95 is 8.14 ms intact from the bank, 11.15 ms breaking through and 10.97 ms quiet in the pond, against `ie-04b`'s 8.69, 11.30 and 11.07 ms (not paired); breaking through costs +0.18 ms GPU and +0.19 ms main over quiet. After 30 visits each, both soak ponds hold what they held on the third visit (meshes, materials, textures and objects +0), so the lip mesh is released. The offline FXC build of the ice code grew from about 2,560 to 2,810 instruction slots (the streaks' and the frost's noise; the frost's runs only on its apron).

## Floes and offshore icebergs, November 3, 2026 (0.6.107-test)

M2 of the ice-edge plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-ice-edge-upgrade.md`, concepts E07, E08): floes read as thick, snow-topped ice with a body under the water instead of flat glassy pans, and tabular icebergs stand off the coast. Render only: the floes keep their footprints, freeboards (0.45-1.6 m) and non-supporting colliders; rules, saves and world hashes are unchanged.
- **Floes** (`PolarIceEdge.Floe`, built at load from each of the six owned silhouettes, in the ice-edge material, kind 9): the outline is the silhouette's farthest vertex in each of 48 bearings; rows run down from a rounded snow lip through a snow cap and a fractured face that leans in or out by bearing, to a waterline notch, then a body widening under the sea to three times the freeboard and a closed underside; a level snow top just under the freeboard, rising a little to the middle; five to nine chips of brash at the waterline. The old floe top tint and side material are gone. Sea, bay and lead floes all use them.
- **Snow on edge ice** (`PolarEdgeSnow`): tops and snow caps read the ground's wind-packed albedo and normal maps on their 5 m tile (`PolarArtLeads` binds them to the edge material), fading to the map's mean as a tile shrinks, so floes, bergs and M1's coast plates show the land's grain instead of a flat white.
- **Riding the swell.** Sea and bay floes within 400 m take the HDRP ocean's height and tilt from the same search as the coast's loose plates (`PolarCoast.Floats`), tilting less the larger they are; farther ones rest; lead floes keep their sway.
- **Offshore icebergs** (`PolarIcebergs`, built on a worker at load): 20-28 tabular bergs round the island, about one every 3 km of coast, 150-400 m long and 15-40 m out of the sea, 0.4-2.2 km past `SeaEdge` (as far as a swimmer may go), placed from the seed: a nearly rectangular outline of calving facets 4-10 m wide whose corners lean and are bitten back here and there, a rounded snow lip, a waterline notch and a body reaching 4 m farther out 12 m under the sea, flat-shaded; a snow top; a closed underside. No colliders or shadows, stationary. Seed 777 has 28 bergs of 94,171 vertices; from the capture's coast stand two stand in the view out to sea, 1.9 and 3.1 km off.
- **Sea view in the performance mode** (`PolarMvpAcceptance.SeaCost.cs`): at the open coast nearest the pond soak's last pond (`PolarCoast.Waterline`, shared with the capture), uncapped throughout, the coast's ice, plates, foam, floes and icebergs against the same view with them hidden (`PolarCoast.Shown`, which also turns the ocean's script interactions off at once), in 120-frame stretches taken A B B A sixteen times; the gate takes the median over the rounds of each pair's GPU P95 and main-thread difference. This laptop's GPU changes clock state every few seconds (a stretch's GPU P95 is about 7.2 or 9.6-10.8 ms in either view), so one shown/hidden pair read +1.4 to +4.2 ms; with both in the fast state the coast adds 0-0.4 ms. Left out part by part (foam, shadows, icebergs, the swell's search, shore-ice runs, loose plates), no part stood clear of that noise, so the breakdown was dropped. on `ie-04` in the full mode (a coast of 10 runs of 22,630 vertices, 261 loose plates with 93 riding, and 28 icebergs in the world), the median of the pairs is +0.05 ms GPU P95 and -0.11 ms main (pooled +0.54 and -0.14 ms), shown frame P95 9.03 ms; from home, on a coast of 26,720 vertices and 317 plates with 144 riding, two interleaved diagnostic runs read +0.89 and +1.12 ms pooled GPU P95 (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.107-test/sea-03`, `sea-04`). `POLAR_SEA_ONLY=1` runs only the sea view, from home, for diagnosis. The pond gate's timing helpers are shared members now (`PolarMvpAcceptance.PondSoak.cs`).
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-03/floes-and-bergs`: `PolarArtCapture` section `b8-ice-edge` before (`ie-m2-before`) and after (`ie-m2-after-4`): a sea floe close, at 20 m and against the sun, the horizon, and the coast stand, storm and night (the coast plates' snow grain). Against the sun the eye now stands .5 m over the floe's drawn top where that is above 1.5 m (2.33 m here), since the solid floe's level top otherwise holds the eye. The fixture notes list the bergs within 8 km of the stand. The first after run caught an `else` bound to the wrong `if`, which logged a null exception whenever the bergs were shown; the second showed flat white tops, the third too few bergs (10 round a coast about 70 km long, the nearest 56 degrees off the view).
- **Results**: gameplay 390 and weather 80 on `ie-02`, performance 79 on `ie-04` (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.107-test/ie-04b`), which differs from `ie-02` only in the sea view's measurement and in `PolarCoast.Shown` turning the ocean's script interactions off and re-picking the foam at once, which only the performance mode switches; on `ie-02` the performance mode failed only the first sea-view gate (one shown/hidden pair, +1.44 ms), and a first `ie-04` run stopped on the pond gate at +0.55 ms main in a noisy run (intact bank frame P95 12.66 ms against 8.31 on `ie-02`), which `ie-04b` passes at +0.16 ms.

## Thin ice bends and floods, November 3, 2026 (0.6.106-test)

M4 of the thin-ice plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-thin-ice-upgrade.md`; its star cracks went to the deep-water plan's M3): a loaded plate is drawn as a bending sheet, and water comes up over it before it gives way. Render only: the rules, the colliders' sag and saves are unchanged.
- **The bend.** Each stressed weak plate of a loaded pond puts a bowl in `_PolarIceLoads` each frame (`PolarThinIceRuntime.LateUpdate`, the four deepest): D e^-r (cos r + sin r), r the metres from its centre. That is a floating plate's deflection with a flexural length of 1 m: flat under the weight, through 0 at 2.36 m, a 4% swell beyond; the plan's exp(-r/L)cos(r/L) with L 1.5 m has its first zero at the same place. D is .045 times the plate's stress over its hold, 1.5 times the rules' sag: the drawn top stands 3.5 cm over the water and the sag stays under 3 cm until the break, so a bowl only as deep as the sag would never reach the water. The bowl stands under the plate's heaviest weight (an explorer's drawn body, `PolarMvpController.NpcBody`; its crack still starts at the rules' feet) and stays there while the plate recovers, or at the crack's start when no weight was seen (after Continue).
- **One sheet.** `PolarMeshModification` drops every sheet vertex (caps, their undersides, rim strips and cut faces; uv2.y 1) by the sum of the bowls and tilts its normal, and draws it at its own height without its cap's rigid sag (the transform still carries the collider's), so plates bent by the same function of position meet bit for bit and no seam opens. uv2.x is the shore's hold (easing to 0 within 1 m of the bank, where the ice is frozen to it), then the drop for the pixel stage. Plate tops are drawn in rows about 0.3 m apart (`PolarArtIceCaps.Append`), each edge split by its own length from the end with the lesser key, so neighbours share their edge points: about 470 vertices for a 5 m plate. Caps draw camera motion vectors only (their sag moves the collider, not the drawn sheet). The CPU dish of 0.6.100-test (`PolarThinIceRuntime.Dish`) is gone.
- **The flood.** Where the bowl takes the top within about a centimetre of the water, a dark, mirror-smooth film (.97) with a ragged edge spreads over the ice and its snow, first along the cracks: from about half the plate's hold round the weight, covering the ice under it by about .7. The sheet stops 3 mm over the water. HDRP's pond water drawn over the bent sheet (the plan's flood line) showed the rows as a pale faceted polygon from a low eye, so the film draws the flood. It replaces the seep drawn round the break point.
- **Not done.** No motion vectors for the bend itself; the boot is not planted in the bowl (it stands up to 1.5 cm over the drawn ice near a break).
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-03/ice-bending-flood`: `PolarArtCapture` section `b4-intact-ice` before (`ib-before`, 0.6.105-test's code with the new shots) and after (`ib-after2`): a weight at half and 95% of the weak plate's hold (close, low, bank, overhead) and at 95% just inside its seam with the stable plate (close, low), and two unloaded shots, unchanged to the eye (under 2.5% of pixels move more than 8 levels, in soft reflections).
- **Checks.** Gameplay 390, continue 36 and performance 72 pass on `ib-01` (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.106-test/ib-01`). Pond 33's GPU P95 is 8.91 ms intact from the bank, 11.38 ms breaking through and 11.00 ms quiet in the pond, against `ii-02`'s 9.23, 13.17 and 12.86 ms (not paired; the machine varies by more), and its 30 visits hold at +0. A review before the build caught three faults in the drafts: a comment that ended the far ponds' mesh line, caps' object motion vectors carrying the whole bend (HDRP builds the previous position from the raw attributes), and explorers' cracks moved to the drawn body.

## The island's coast and the sea, November 3, 2026 (0.6.105-test)

M1 of the ice-edge plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-ice-edge-upgrade.md`, concept E10): the coast reads as snow, rock and ice meeting a moving sea instead of a clean snow line with bright snow under shallow water. Render only (HDRP): colliders, rules, saves and world hashes are unchanged, except that the drawn snow thins out at the sea, which `SnowAt` reads too (see below).
- **Ground at the sea** (`PolarIceEdge.hlsl`, called from the kind-0 ground and kind-7 rock paths in `PolarData.hlsl`). A coast gate from `PolarGeometry.Project` at the shelf's first row (the last 70 lattice rows, where the sea lies over the land), its angle inverted in four fixed-point steps, so inland hollows below the sea's datum stay dry; `PolarCoast` sets `_PolarCoast` and `_PolarCoastDrive` once a frame (centre, row radius, wobble phase; the sea's height, storm, weather clock, radial weight). Under the sea: dark rock and stones, bluer and darker deeper; on stretches of rocky shore the bare band reaches up to .25 m above the sea (`PolarCoastRock`; the CPU's `RockTop` keeps the drawn snow off it). At the waterline: a wet band (darker, glossy) whose reach follows a surge every 9 s or so, higher in a storm, and above it an icy glaze of frozen spray. Noise fades with the pixel footprint.
- **Drawn snow at the sea** (`PolarCoast.Snow`, read by `PolarWorldStreamer.SnowDepth`, so `SnowAt`, footsteps and things put down follow it): past the shelf line the depth falls from the full .25 m above the rock band (or the sea) to none at it, ramped in over the shelf's first four rows, so the drawn waterline is the collider's and the ice meets it.
- **Shore ice** (`PolarCoast`, `PolarIceEdge.Coast`; material kind 9 `IceEdge` from `PolarArtLeads.Edge`): broken ice crowding the waterline, 15-40 cm thick and floating 3-12 cm out of the sea, cut every 1.5-5 m along slanted cuts into plates 0.8-3.2 m out, each up to half narrower than its neighbours, with up to 1.4 m of open water at most cuts and a fifth of the line bare. Nearly half the plates are broken off, drifted 0.3-2 m out, turned up to 7 degrees and tipped up to 2; some raft onto the next. Each plate has a middle row, so its cuts bend and its snowy rims stand over a greyer middle. Young pancake plates (7-11 corners, up to 1.5 times as long as wide, a grey middle under a raised snowy rim) lie loose 1-25 m out, drawn instanced in eight shapes. Coastal bays take the `Bay` profile (0.4-1.8 m out, fewer drifted) along their walls. Runs of about 250 m (32 lattice columns of coast, or 250 m of a bay's rim) are built on a worker within 700 m of the camera and released past 950 m, casting shadows within 150 m; the waterline is traced from the heights (the first sea crossing out from the shelf row, tracked within 1.5 rows while the ground crosses the sea only once). `PolarIceEdge.Plate` also serves the river and lead profiles for M3.
- **Riding the swell.** Within 400 m the 512 nearest loose plates take the HDRP ocean's height and tilt from HDRP's CPU search over its read-back displacement (`WaterSimulationSearchJob`), the ocean's script interactions on while any ride (off 5 s after the last; the first 4 frames skipped, as HDRP keeps the last read-back until a new one lands); the 48 nearest within 90 m cast shadows.
- **Shore foam.** Eight HDRP shore-wave water decals (`SeaShore.mat`, HDRP's Sample Water Decal graph, foam only) on the ocean at the nearest stations within 110 m, every 40 m of waterline, the waves breaking from about 13 m out to the ice.
- **Edge ice shading** (`PolarEdgeIce`, kind 9): snow tops over frosted or clear ice; faces with the snow cap, milky white-blue fractured ice with streaks and bubbles, denser and bluer as colour g rises, a dark wet band at the piece's own water and a muted blue-green below; pale grey-blue undersides; a faint glow of the sky's light through thin young ice (`PolarEdgeGlowShare`).
- **Counts** (seed 777): at the capture's coast stand, 10 runs within 700 m hold 26,720 vertices and 317 loose plates, 144 of them riding the swell (`ie-m2-before` fixture notes).
- **Results**: gameplay 390, physical 3959, performance 72 and weather 80 pass on `ie-01` (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.105-test/ie-01`; gameplay's peak 6.83 GB) and the world hash is unchanged (seed 777 BF321ACDC2E7B04D); frame P95 stays 16.7 ms at 60 fps pacing as on 0.6.104-test, and the uncapped pond views are 1.1-1.4 ms faster on the GPU than 0.6.104-test's run (not paired). The performance mode has no view at the coast, so the coast's own GPU cost is not yet measured; M2 adds a sea view.

## Intact ice: looking into it, November 3, 2026 (0.6.104-test)

M3 of the thin-ice plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-thin-ice-upgrade.md`, concepts 03, 09 and 10): intact pond ice reads as clear, cracked ice over deep water instead of a painted floor.
- **The pond's map.** `PolarIceMap` (pure C#, built on a worker thread when a pond loads, deterministic from the pond's seed, render only) grows a refrozen crack network: 4-10 primary cracks from the shore across the pond in straight steps with kinks, crossing an older crack half the time; secondaries branching at 30-70 degrees; short tertiaries ending on others. Each 1 m cell lists the segments within reach by level, at most 16 (`MaxPerCell`), in an RGBAFloat texture 256 texels wide (`_PolarIceCracks`, point-filtered); a crack ends where a cell it would join is full. A second texture (`_PolarIceShore`, 2 texels a metre) holds the metres inside the shore. Caps read both at m = uv0 + `_PolarIceFrame.xy` through their property block; rim strips and floating pieces use the pond's own copies of the ice and underside materials with uv0 in the map's metres, so a piece keeps the cracks it had in the sheet. Distant ponds have no map (`_PolarIceFrame.w` 0); their uv0.x is the metres to the shore. Seed 777 passes 1104 world checks, the new one that each of its 422 maps is the same built twice, keeps to 16 a cell and lies inside its shore.
- **Cracks.** Each crack is a sheet through the ice's 6 cm, measured exactly against the view's refracted path (n 1.31) and antialiased over the pixel's footprint: a hairline looking down, a wider pale band at a slant, dimmer with depth. Tertiaries fade out by about 7 m from a standing eye, primaries and secondaries by about 20 m. The undersides show the same cracks from below (refracted from the water, n 1.33). They replace the Voronoi cell lines, the plate seams and the cracks drawn round a hole's neighbours (`_PolarDamage` and `PolarArtIceCaps.Damage`, removed).
- **Shore and depth.** White ice (frozen slush, .5 smooth, with some of the snow's grain) runs 0.3-1.4 m wide along the bank with a ragged inner line; clear ice darkens to near black about 6 m out, over the 20 m of water. There is no pond floor to see.
- **Bubbles.** Three layers 1, 3 and 5 cm down offset by the refracted view (2-25 mm), and a sparse fourth of larger ones (1.5-10 cm) that still shows a few metres off, gathered in patches 1-3 m across and thicker by the bank, with a faint haze where they are densest. Patches, haze and the snow dust mix value noise on two grids 37 degrees apart; thresholded on one grid they showed its square cells as blocky light patches.
- **Surface.** Clear ice is .95 smooth (.91 under haze and bubbles), over HDRP's .9 screen-space reflection minimum, so it mirrors sky, shore and people; snow on the ice is .25. A faint frozen ripple, fading with distance, replaces the bump map. There is no clear coat: the project's hand-ported Lit compiles the coat only for ray tracing.
- **Shader.** The crack loop runs once in `PolarThinIceSurface`, before the pieces' branch, for tops and undersides: FXC's optimiser overflowed its stack (0xC00000FD) with a second copy of the loop inside that branch. From below only primaries and secondaries are drawn, and a crack further from the pixel than its half-width, the footprint and the view's path through the ice is skipped before the exact test, which leaves the image unchanged.
- **Not changed.** The snow drifted onto the pond (`snowMask`) and the pond's straight outline against the bank belong to M5 (the shore).
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-03/intact-ice-look`: `PolarArtCapture` section `b4-intact-ice` (the deep-water fixture's pond 33, seed 777), before (0.6.102-test's code, `ii-before`) and after (`ii-after3`), eleven pairs: bank, bank looking along it, feet, standing, close, 25 m and 8 m overhead, 60 m, night, overcast and blizzard.
- **Checks.** Seed 777 passes 1104 world checks, one new: every pond's map is deterministic, keeps to 16 a cell and lies inside its shore (`Logs/PolarCompactWorld/ii-map2-777`). Gameplay 390 and performance 72 pass on `ii-02`, continue 36 and weather 80 on `ii-01`, which differs only in the crack loop (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.104-test`). `ii-01`'s performance mode failed the pond gate: breaking through cost +2.54 ms of GPU P95 over the same pond quiet (13.91 against 11.37 ms), from the crack loop on the undersides filling the view in the dip; `ii-02` passes at +0.32 ms (13.17 against 12.86 ms). Against 0.6.102-test's run (`dm-06`, not paired) pond 33 is slower on the GPU: 9.23 against 7.91 ms P95 intact from the bank, 13.17 against 11.15 ms breaking through and 12.86 against 10.90 ms quiet in the pond. Part of that may be the machine; a paired run has not been made.

## The tent from the pack, November 3, 2026 (0.6.103-test)

M3 of the habitat plan ([gameplay](polar-gameplay.md) top section):
- **Tent.** The tent uses the pack's materials: canvas, CanvasDark seams, hems, skirt and stove jack, TimberWarm poles, TimberDark stakes, Rope guys and ties, and Brass eyelets. The canvas sheets are single faces, so the tent draws them with `CanvasTent`, a double-sided copy of `Canvas`; the sack and the bunk keep `Canvas`.
- **Removed.** The woven canvas, its weathering (0.6.22-0.6.23) and the STP sleeping bag are gone, with their assets in `Resources/Polar/Camping` (`TentCanvas*`, `TentWeathering`, `SleepingBag`). `PolarTentAssetsAuthor` keeps only the camp props.
- **Rigging.** The heater's chimney passes through the pack's stove jack, so the iron collar is gone. The two front ridge guys and their stakes are stripped from the rigging (`PolarHabitatAuthor.StripFront`): they crossed the entrance at head height. Ropes and brass cast no shadows, since the burner's soft shadows draw every caster.
- **Flaps** (`PolarHabitatAuthor.BuildTent`). The pack's closed shape (`build_habitat.py`) flattens each flap's hem, ties and eyelets into the canvas and stretches the ties across it, its gathered cloth stands up to 12 cm above the gable's slope outside the posts, and its folds sweep through the door posts as they close. The author rewrites the eight flap meshes in the tent's frame (saved as `Assets/PolarHabitat/Meshes/TwoPersonTent-*`):
  - every part moves 3 cm out, which clears the posts;
  - the open cloth stays under the gable line (2.18 - 0.75|x| m);
  - the hems of both shapes reach 0.035 m, on the groundsheet;
  - the hem, ties and eyelets travel rigidly to the closed leading edge, 1.5 cm short of the middle, and stretch up as the canvas does there.
  The flaps' bounds cover both shapes.
- **Front hem.** The pack's front has no groundsheet upstand, and its wings and flaps stopped 4 cm above the groundsheet, so snow showed through a slot from inside. The wings' hem now reaches 0.035 m, and the groundsheet runs on under the front wall and the flaps to z 1.49 m (1.40 m in the pack).
- **M2 faults fixed.** The mica pane darkens as the burner door opens: its emission is scaled by (1 - `Opening`), in huts and tents. Each flipbook frame reads the middle 90% of its cell (ST .5/.45, offset +.025), so neither the pane's edges nor the mouth's top row picks up the next cell's flame. This removes the bright line along the open door's edge and the dashed line above the tent's mouth.
- **Captures.** 43 five-areas cases, before and after: the `world` tent cases, `stove`, `anim-door` flaps, `anim-pitch`, the tent cases of `anim-rest`, `anim-fire` tent and hut cases, and `tracks` tent cases. A review of all 43 pairs, each fault read again by a second reviewer, found the front-hem slot, the flattened and stretched ties, the gathered cloth above the gable and the folds through the posts; the author's flap and hem rewrite fixes them, and a recheck of the final set confirmed it. Left: the rope ties dip about 1 cm behind the closed canvas at their bend (a few pixels from the entrance); an explorer getting up at the back-right bed stands with his head in the roof, as before; and the `world` group's tent cases are staged with the talk camera still on Adler, before and after alike, so they show no tent.

## Pond cost and soak, November 3, 2026 (0.6.102-test)

M4 of the deep-water plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-thin-ice-deep-water.md`). Test code only: the performance mode measures a pond and soaks two (`PolarMvpAcceptance.PondPerformance`, `PolarMvpAcceptance.PondSoak.cs`).
- **The scene.** The nearest pond over the sea with a weak plate at least 0.9 m from centre to edge (pond 33 on seed 777). It runs 600 frames intact from the bank, then places the player on the weak plate and measures from that moment until 6 s after the plate breaks (cracks, fold, dip, rings, spray and bubbles). It then measures 360 frames swimming in the hole from 9 s after the break.
- **Uncapped.** Each stretch runs with vsync off and no frame cap:
  - At the game's 60 fps pacing, `FrameTimingManager`'s GPU time includes the GPU's idle gaps within a frame. Measured that way, the breaking ice read as +6.6 ms of GPU P95 in `dm-04`.
  - Uncapped, the same effects cost about 0.2 ms of GPU and 0.15 ms of frame time (diagnostic builds `dm-04p` to `dm-04t`). Paced, the gap closes only with every effect off at once.
  - Main-thread time is `cpuMainThreadFrameTime`, the main thread's own work: from Unity 6000.3 it leaves out the waits for the render thread and for present. On 6000.2 it included them (about the whole frame when GPU-bound), and the present wait was subtracted.
- **Gates.**
  - Breaking frame P95 at most 20 ms.
  - Breaking against the same pond quiet: at most +2 ms GPU P95 and +1.5 ms main thread (+0.5 ms until Unity 6000.3, whose own-work reading shows the breakthrough at +0.68 to +1.77 ms, hidden on 6000.2 by the waits; the user's choice).
  - A broken pond against the intact bank: at most +64 MiB private and +128 MiB of texture and render-texture memory.
  - Result: Pond 33: breaking through 11.46 ms frame P95 uncapped, GPU P95 +0.25 ms and main thread +0.21 ms over the same pond quiet (11.15 against 10.90 ms; 10.45 against 10.24 ms); the broken pond +40 MiB private and +28 MiB of render textures against the intact bank.
- **Where the frame goes.** Swimming in the hole costs about 3.2 ms more than standing on the bank (paced). Ice near the eye takes about 1.7 ms and the pond's water about 0.5 ms; the rest is the view itself.
- **Soak.** The test visits that pond and one at least 250 m away, outside its three-block load window, 30 times each by teleport, with both plates broken.
  - Each visit waits for the hole to load, then records what thin ice holds loaded, the pool's meshes, and the counts of meshes, materials, textures, render textures, objects and particle systems, with memory.
  - From the third visit on, nothing may grow more than 16 (objects 64), the pool's meshes must stay equal, and native memory and the floor of private memory over the last ten visits may grow at most 64 MiB each (the last five until November 7, 2026: a passing rise of 250-300 MiB from cycle 25-28, in every build, could cover all five).
  - The floor, not the last sample: in `dm-05`, private memory rose about 300 MiB for three cycles and fell back, while native memory and every count stayed flat. Judged on the last sample, that passing peak read as growth.
  - Result: after 30 visits each, meshes, materials, textures, render textures, objects and native memory +0 at both ponds, the private floor +4 and +21 MiB (passing peaks +288 and +251 MiB).
- **Inspected.** The performance shots (`pond-intact`, `pond-swim`, and the soak's first and last visit to each pond) show the same hole and pieces on the first and thirtieth visit, with no TAA trails on the water, the rim or the moving pieces and no reflection seam at the screen edges. The hands are not in these views, and the storm sequence is M3's (`dw-m3d`).
- **Not done as planned.**
  - No paired runs against 0.6.95-test: that build has neither the deep water nor the breakthrough, so the scene cannot run there. The gate compares within one run instead.
  - The soak runs inside the performance mode instead of under its own Player argument.
  - The window is the runner's 1600x900, not 1920x1080.
- **Checks.** Gameplay 390 on `dm-05` and performance 72 on `dm-06`, which differs from `dm-05` only in the soak's memory check (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.102-test`). Both differ from `dm-03` only in the performance mode, so physical 3959, continue 36, weather 80, ui 463, physical-resume 18 and weather-resume 10 on `dm-03` stand for the game.

## The breakthrough, November 3, 2026 (0.6.101-test)

M3 of the deep-water plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-thin-ice-deep-water.md`, concepts C08-C10): the ice cracks from under the feet, its pieces fold into the water from there, and the water answers with rings, spray, bubbles and foam.
- **Events.** `PolarThinIceRuntime.Raise` sends `ThinIceVisualEvent`s (LoadStart, Break, Entry, Resurface, FragmentImpact, Stroke) from real changes: the runtime for load and break, `PolarSurveyPlayer` for the player going in, `PolarBodyFeedback` for coming up, `PolarNpcView` for explorers going in (with their drawn speed) and coming up, when they gasp. They are never saved and change no rule.
- **Where it broke.** `SetLoad` takes the feet. The first weight on a plate fixes where its cracks start; only feet on the plate itself count (feet on a hole's rim load the plate across), and not once cracks already show; the break keeps that point (`PolarIcePlateData.struck`, `hitX`, `hitZ`), and saves keep it (`PolarIceSnapshot`), so a reloaded hole shows the same pieces. Older saves and plates stressed after a load use the plate's hashed point.
- **Cracks.** The plate's 12-30 Voronoi seeds, crowded toward that point (`PolarArtIcePondView.Network`, shared with the pieces), go to the cap as a 16 px float texture, two to a texel (`_PolarCrackMap`; `_PolarStressOrigin.w` their count). `PolarStressFracture` finds each pixel's two nearest seeds and draws their bisector, the network's edge itself, revealed out from the point as stress grows, each edge to its own reach (0.6-2.4 m times the stress, by a hash of the pair), thinning outward: straight cracks with kinks that end at their junctions, along the lines the pieces later break on. This replaces the curls and rings. The drafted 128 px map of each texel's nearest edge as a line, made on a worker thread, broke cracks into dashes near junctions, and reading the texels round each pixel ran them past the junctions (found in the Unity pass, `dw-walk-load-half-close`).
- **The fold.** On a break seen live, each piece leaves its place in the sheet and folds to its resting pose over 0.18-0.25 s plus its size, starting later the farther it is from the point (up to 0.25 s); sinking pieces go down and vanish. A hole loaded or streamed back is built settled.
- **Rings.** One HDRP water decal in texture mode (HDRP's Sample Water Decal graph, `Resources/Polar/HDRP/PondRings.mat`, foam off) deforms the pond surface over a 16 m square. `PolarIceRipples.shader` writes up to 16 damped rings into a 256 px height map, faded by a 48 px open-water map (sampled over the broken plates only) so nothing rises under the ice. The pond's mesh is far coarser than a 30 cm ring, so the rings show in the water's normals only. The square follows the first ring of a quiet pond and moves with a swimmer past its middle third. `supportWaterDecals` is on in `PolarPipeline.asset`.
- **Particles.** Three pooled systems replace the per-break splash: spray (falls back and dies at the water), bubbles (rise, stop under the ice and creep to open water; drawn before refraction) and foam (floats and slows). Nothing runs while no particle lives.
- **Pieces in the water.** Pieces bob and rock at rest, and the swimming player shoves them aside; a piece landing in the water raises a ring and, if large, a swirl. The plan's 24-piece rigid-body simulation was not built: with the rings in the water's normals only there is no height for pieces to ride, and the shove reads the same at a fraction of the cost.
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-02/continuous-breakthrough`: `PolarArtCapture` section `b4-deep-water`, before (0.6.95-test, `dw-before-b`) and after (`dw-m3d`), ten pairs: the plate in the sheet with dark cracks at 0-0.15 s, pieces folding in, rings on the water, spray over it and bubbles under it, a storm break and two holes side by side. The walk's load frames change nothing at the eye, since the cracks start under the feet; `dw-walk-load-half-close` (after only) shows them from 2.4 m up..
- **Checks.** gameplay 390, physical 3959, continue 36, performance 61, weather 80 and ui 463 pass on `dm-03` (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.101-test/dm-03`).

## Under the ice, November 3, 2026 (0.6.100-test)

M2 of the deep-water plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-thin-ice-deep-water.md`, concepts C02-C04, C09): the ice is seen from every side, the view goes under the water for a moment on going in, and the hands go into the water with it.
- **The ice from below.** Caps, rim strips and pieces have an underside 6 cm down (`PolarArtIceCaps.Thickness`), drawn as ice part 4 in `PolarData.hlsl` (`PolarIceUnder`): pale grey-blue, whiter in patches, with a few bright crack lines, and the sky's light through the sheet added as emission from the ambient probe (`PolarIceGlow`: about half through clear ice, a sixth where snow lies). Cut faces reach the full 6 cm. Caps, strips and pieces cast sun shadows, so the volumetric light comes down through the opening only. Looking straight up from under a hole, the water's surface beyond the critical angle shows the dark depths (Snell's window), as it should.
- **Under the water.** HDRP's `underWater` is on for "Pond water (HDRP water)" only while the eye is inside the active pond's outline and below its water (`PolarArtWater.Under`). While the eye is under that water, the sea, now on layer 20 ("Sea", `PolarArtWater.SeaLayer`), leaves the eye camera's mask (`SeaSeen`; HDRP draws a surface only on a layer the camera culls in), a WaterRendering override lowers the ambient probe by how much of the ice within 6 m of the eye is open (`PolarArtWater.UnderLight`, reset when the world goes), and over a pond at the sea the dry land's exclusion is turned off (`DryShown`), since it would hide the water seen from below. Swimming in the sea or a bay changes none of this.
- **The lens.** Under the water the view wavers and softens; coming up, a film runs down the lens over 1.5 s and a few drops stay until 4 s (`PolarLens.shader` `_Under`, `_Film`, `_Drops`; `PolarHdrpPresentation`). Game time, so pause holds it.
- **The body.** The drawn body goes under with the eye (`PolarPlayerBodyView.Place` adds `PolarBodyFeedback.Dipped` while in the water); without it the eye sank to the knees of the treading body, which filled the view.
- **The hands.** While the player swims or climbs out in a pond, from when the dip has the eye under, the world camera draws the arms (layer 10) and the foreground pass leaves them (`PolarWeaponView`, `PolarHdrpForegroundPass`), so they go through the water's refraction and absorption and behind the ice's edge; they cast no shadow meanwhile. Leads and the sea keep them in the foreground.
- **Slush and seams** (handed over by the thin-ice session after 0.6.95-test). The slush was one white sheet: every flake fully frosted at one height. Now each flake has its own frost (colour r .5-1 at its middle, .55 less at its edge, so it fades to clear ice), sits 1.5-4 mm over the water tilted up to 10 degrees, and lies packed toward the hole's line, in patches, or in clots 3-9 cm apart (`PolarArtIcePondView.Slush`). A loaded plate's collider still sags whole by up to 3 cm, but its drawn cap is a dish with its rim at rest (`PolarThinIceRuntime.Dish`; 0.6.106-test bends the whole sheet instead), so no step or slit opens at a seam; the hole's strips no longer follow a neighbour's sag.
- **Not done.** Thin-edge transmission (HDRP's transmission feature with an owned diffusion profile) and a darker, smoother wet band on piece tops by the water were left out: the cut faces and pieces already read as ice in the captures, and both would add a material variant and a profile to the HDRP global settings for little change. A dark line where a hole's rim strip laps over the shore's snow (the 5 cm lip, `CapOutline`) shows from a swimmer's eye at the hole's line when the hole touches the shore; it is older than M2 (the swimmer now stops at the hole's visible line, which brings it into view) and belongs to the shore work queued after this plan.
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-02/underwater-entry`: `PolarArtCapture` section `b4-deep-water`, before (0.6.95-test, `dw-before-b`) and after (`dw-m2c`), ten pairs: going in, the view goes under the water at 0.5-0.8 s with the hands dim in it; from below, the hole, the ice's undersides and the floating pieces show over navy depths with no floor. In the same capture the slush is sparse and grey and a half-loaded plate shows no seam (`dw-walk-load-half`).

## Kerosene heaters, November 2, 2026 (0.6.99-test)

M2 of the habitat plan ([gameplay](polar-gameplay.md) top section):
- **Heaters.** `HutHeater` and `TentHeater` are the pack's models with owned HDRP/Lit materials. Only the body, panels, reservoir, bands, chimney and rain cap cast shadows (the stove lamp's soft cube shadows draw every caster six times). The mica pane is an owned copy with UVs 0..1 across its face (`Assets/PolarHabitat/Meshes/<heater>-mica`; the pack's metre-scale box UVs showed about 5% of one flipbook cell), and the burner mouth is an owned quad (`<heater>-mouth`).
- **Hut.** `PolarArtHutDressing.PlaceHeater` adds the heater to the dressing's LOD (to the LOD1->LOD2 switch) on layer 14; the code-built cast-iron shell, flue, cap and stove glass are gone. A roofed hut keeps a ceiling collar and roof flashing round the heater's chimney at (2, 4.175) (`Collars`). Chimney smoke leaves the rain cap (2, 4.72, 4.175); the teapot stands on the heater's top and the pot that stood on the stove is gone; the broken-stove debris stays hidden inside the heater's body.
- **Old stove stripped from the huts.** The stove the hut builder merged into Hut0-4's LOD0 and LOD1 meshes (five iron boxes, a latch and seven panes, 156 triangles a LOD) would have enclosed the heater, so `PolarHabitatAuthor.StripHutStoves` removes every triangle with all corners in the old stove's body or flue box, 312 a hut, refusing any hut whose count differs. LOD2 and the far huts keep their flue. Collision meshes are separate and unchanged. Retired in 0.6.109-test with the old hut meshes.
- **Tent.** The heater replaces the code-built stove, door, glass, vents and mouth; a collar of the old iron stays where its chimney passes the canvas (`Collar`, until the tent comes from the pack in M3); the burner lamp is 4 cm lower, 6 cm out from the mica; the kettle stands on the heater's top behind its filler.
- **Captures.** Five-areas `stove` and `anim-fire` (a fresh v23 camp fixture, group `fixture`, `PolarFiveAreasCapture.Fixture.cs`) and `PolarArtCapture` `b6-habitat`, before and after. Frame time was not measured.

## Deep pond water, November 2, 2026 (0.6.98-test)

M1 of the deep-water plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-thin-ice-deep-water.md`, concepts `docs/art/thin-ice-deep-water` C01-C03): a hole in thin ice now looks down into deep, clear navy water with no floor in sight.
- **The basin.** World v23 makes every pond 20 m deep in the rules ([polar-world](polar-world.md)). The floor and sidewalls are drawn at that depth in near-black submerged ground (`silt`, now released on teardown). The walls are one inward face per outline edge with their own normals (`PolarThinIceRuntime.Wall`); until v23 each edge's quads shared vertices in both windings, so their normals summed to nothing. The "Drawn pool bottom" at sea + 0.6 m is gone.
- **Water over the sea.** HDRP hides every water surface wherever the dry land's exclusion mesh (sea + 0.5 m) lies in front of the opaque scene; that is why 0.6.95-test drew pond floors no lower than sea + 0.6 m. `PolarArtWater.Dry` now cuts the exclusion round each pond whose water lies over 0.6 m above the sea plane (`OverSea`; lower water would be hidden wherever a view ray crosses the exclusion before it): each cell it touches becomes the cell minus the pond's convex outline (`PolarThinIce.Subtract`, both windings), built once a world. The pond's own water is nearer than the sea plane, so it hides the sea in its basin.
- **Ponds at the sea (`low`).** Ponds with water up to 0.6 m over the sea lie under or at the exclusion, so their water would be hidden. They keep the owned water while the eye is above the exclusion and switch to HDRP's below it (`PolarThinIceRuntime.Low`). On twenty v23 seeds 1190 of 8566 ponds are low (13.9%; seed 777: 51 of 422), most of them in inland hollows below the sea.
- **One pool surface, re-anchored.** HDRP reads a pool's height (the camera's and the waterline's) and its volume bounds from the surface itself, though it draws each listed mesh at its own height. So "Pond water (HDRP water)" stands at the active pond's water level (`PolarArtWater.Anchor`): the pond holding the player, else the nearest loaded pond with an open hole within 60 m, kept until 80 m. A trigger box sized to that basin is its `volumeBounds`. Nothing is anchored until the surface exists (a low pond's comes only below the exclusion).
- **Optics (the pond surface only).** Absorption over 4 m, underwater multiplier 1, refraction at most 0.5 m; restrained navy refraction (.04, .12, .22) and scattering (.03, .09, .17); ripples fade from 15 m over 60 m; smoothness falls from .95 to .82 between 20 and 140 m; caustics stay off. The sea and inland water are unchanged.
- **Capture fix.** `PolarFiveAreasCapture`'s water timing picks the inland surface by name; it had taken the first non-sea surface, which can now be the pond.
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-02/deep-pond-water`: `PolarArtCapture` section `b4-deep-water`, before (0.6.95-test, `dw-before-b`) and after (`dw-m1`), eight pairs: the hole's water is a deeper navy and the pale floor is gone. Views from under the water still show the basin's dark walls; HDRP's underwater view comes in M2.
- **Checks.** Package `dm-01`: gameplay 387, physical 3959, continue 36, performance 61, weather 80 and ui 463 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.98-test/dm-01`). Twenty v23 seeds pass (`Logs/PolarCompactWorld/v23-twenty`).
- **Not done.** The underwater view, ice undersides, rim support, the dip and the hands are M2; the break's motion, rings and particles are M3.

## Polar Habitat v1.1 import and materials, November 2, 2026 (0.6.96-test)

M0 and M1 of the habitat plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-habitat-models.md`); containers and supplies in [gameplay](polar-gameplay.md) top section.
- **Files.** The pack's 27 FBX models, 16 tiled 1024 px PBR sets (BaseColor, OpenGL normal, HDRP mask), four printed labels and the survey map are in `Assets/PolarHabitat/` (LFS by `.gitattributes`); the Blender file, its `source/*.py`, the three manifests, README and `SHA256SUMS.txt` are in `ArtSource/PolarHabitat-v1.1/`, outside Assets. The GLB copies are not kept.
- **Import** (`PolarHabitatImport`, an `AssetPostprocessor` for that folder): scale 1 with Bake Axis Conversion, so Blender's (x, y, z) reads as the game's (x, z, y) and the hut's heater lands on `StovePoint` and its entry hinge at x -0.6 with no turn (`PolarHabitatAuthor.CheckAxes`); blend shapes on, legacy clips, no compression; `_Normal` maps as normal maps and `_Normal`, `_HDRPMask` and `_ORM` linear.
- **Materials** (`PolarHabitatAuthor.WriteMaterials`, 28 HDRP/Lit in `Assets/PolarHabitat/Materials`): each set at the pack's normal strength 0.65 with its HDRP mask (R metal, G occlusion, A smoothness); the sack's dark lining double-sided; the labels and survey map plain colour at smoothness .14; the pack's plain colours (ink, glass, black, enamel, ivory, ember) from its glTF factors; `PaperDamp` for a prepared ration. An imported material maps by name onto its owned copy, and a Blender duplicate (`Iron.001`) onto its original. Roof snow and the fire glass waited for M2-M4 (the fire glass came in 0.6.99-test, roof snow in 0.6.109-test).

## Thin ice floats: a ragged hole with the sheet's own pieces, November 2, 2026 (0.6.95-test)

The user said thin ice "looks really unnatural" and approved a five-milestone plan (`docs/engineering/plans/polar-thin-ice-upgrade.md`). M1 and M2 ship here, because the worst fault was the hole: a pit with water 0.65 m down, vertical walls and glassy tiles hovering in it.
- **Water at the ice (M1, rules).** World v22 puts pond water 3 cm under the ice's top instead of 0.65 m ([polar-world](polar-world.md#world-v22-pond-water-3-cm-under-the-ice-november-2-2026-0695-test)). Real 6 cm ice stands about 5 mm proud; the sheet's edge is drawn 3 cm proud so it reads at walking distance. Explorers swim at `WaterLevel(pond) - 1.15` as the player does.
- **The hole (M2, render only; `PolarArtIcePondView`, the collider stays the plate).**
  - Outline: the broken plate opened by a radius of 0.5-1.2 m (inset, then grown back, so corners become arcs), found by bisection on rays from the centroid, then pushed in by periodic 1D value-noise fBm (three octaves, about 0.25 m either way).
  - A strip of the surviving ice runs from each plate edge to that line, 1 cm under a neighbour's cap or 5 cm over the shore's snow lip, and fades out within 0.6 m of an edge shared with another hole. A cut face goes down from its inner line to 5 cm under the water. Each edge's points sit out along its own normal, so one triangle at each corner closes the sliver two strips would leave.
  - Pieces: a 2D Voronoi fracture by half-plane clipping, 12-30 seeds crowded toward a break point within 30% of the plate's reach, each cell shrunk by 3-12 cm so water shows between them.
