Pieces 6 cm thick float 5 mm proud with the sheet's own ice on top (cap channels, so bubbles and cracks match). Near the break, 30% are pushed under and the rest tip 15-35 degrees about their far edge; a few raft 4-8 degrees; the rest lie within 2 degrees. All bob 2-5 mm and rock 0.4-1.2 degrees (`Animate`), each its own object, shadows on. Deterministic from the pond seed and plate id.
  - Slush: 60-260 flat white flakes 2.5-9 cm across at the water, 70% within 0.5 m of the hole's line and the rest in patches about 0.8 m across.
  - The old shards (`PolarArtIceCaps.Shards`, 2.5 cm over the water with frosted rims) and the pebble debris are gone under HDRP.
- **HDRP pond water (`PolarArtWater.Pond`).** Each loaded pond's water mesh goes into one HDRP pool surface, "Pond water (HDRP water)" (custom mesh, no tessellation, calm ripples on the weather's wind), and leaves it on release. It gives Fresnel reflection, refraction and absorption, and its ripples follow the weather as the other surfaces' do.
  - HDRP hides water wherever the dry land's exclusion mesh (sea + 0.5 m, `PolarArtWater.Dry`) lies in front of the opaque scene. So the pool's floor is drawn no lower than sea + 0.6 m, in a silt colour, while its collider stays at the rules' 3 m. A pond whose water would then be under 0.3 m deep keeps the owned water.
- **Tried and left out.** A wet stain spreading from a fresh hole (`_PolarDamage.z` above 1) darkened whole neighbouring plates and lifted their shore snow, because the damage block covers a whole plate. The plate grid showed through, so M4's flooding will draw wet ice instead. Skirts under the plate caps, a first guess at the slivers, were tried and removed.
- **Evidence.** `docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-11-02/thin-ice-floating`: `PolarArtCapture` section `b4-thin-ice` (new; seed 777, pond 8, weak plate 26 broken by the real load from its stable neighbour 27). Twelve shots: from the shore by day, at night and in a snowstorm, 60 m back, 25 m overhead, 1.2 m inside the stable plate before and after the break, 8 m over the hole, from the bank, and a swimmer's eye in the hole.
  - Broken views change 0.08-4.3% of pixels, and the swimmer's 83%: the swimmer now looks across the water at a 3 cm edge instead of up a 0.65 m shaft.
  - The intact shore view changes 0.08% (snow and particles); intact ice is M3.
- **Checks.** Package `ti-01`: gameplay 387, physical 3959, continue 36, performance 61, weather 80 and ui 463 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.95-test/ti-01`); shared rules 2748; twenty v22 seeds pass.
- **Pond water's GPU cost** (measured later on November 2, on 0.6.98-test's code with its re-anchored surface). The `waterperf` group of `PolarFiveAreasCapture` gained three pond views: the nearest pond over the sea with a weak plate at least 0.9 m from centre to edge (seed 777: pond 33, plate 14). The views are from 25 m above while intact, from 3.5 m over the broken plate so the hole fills the screen, and a swimmer's eye in it. Each alternates the pond's HDRP water, none and its owned water, three rounds. Editor, uncapped, HDRP's pass timers (`Logs/ProjectUpdateCapture/five-areas/pw-1/waterperf`):
  - **Intact, from above:** +0.06 ms, only the pool's update and simulation. HDRP simulates an enabled pool even with all its water under ice.
  - **Hole filling the screen:** its own passes +0.28 ms (deferred lighting 0.15, GBuffer 0.07, update and simulation 0.06). The frame rises 0.25-0.41 ms a round against no pond water and 0.07-0.47 ms against the owned water.
  - **Swimmer's eye:** its own passes +0.22 ms. The frame's change is within its noise (-0.08 to +0.25 ms).

  The plan's target was +0.3 ms. The worst view, an unusual close look straight down a hole, meets it for the water's own passes and is at its edge for the whole frame.
- **Not done.** Intact ice, load and the shore are M3-M5, now in the deep-water plan. The slush's look (bright, even) and the step a loaded plate's 3 cm sag makes at its seams were diagnosed on November 2 and handed to that plan's M2, whose rework of the plate and hole meshes covers both files.

## Clear-day cirrus drawn only in the view, November 2, 2026 (0.6.93-test)

The user asked to try the cirrus again as a layer that does not light the snow. On HDRP's cloud layer they had raised the snow's sky light and lowered the sun's share (0.6.92-test, below). This version ships.
- **How.** `PolarCirrus.shader` is a full-screen custom pass at `AfterOpaqueAndSky`. It runs on the survey camera from `PolarHdrpPresentation` (volume "Polar HDRP cirrus").
  - It runs after HDRP has drawn the sky and before its fog, cloud layer and volumetric clouds. HDRP renders its ambient probe and sky reflections from the sky and cloud renderers alone, so neither they nor anything lit sees the cirrus.
  - It changes only far-plane pixels above the horizon. It first copies the camera colour (`PolarHdrpLensPass`, shared with the lens), since reading the target in place is undefined on D3D11.
  - The map is `Resources/Polar/HDRP/Sky/Cirrus.png`, written by `Tools/polar_cirrus_map.py`: the cirrus (blue) channel of HDRP's `DefaultCloudMap.png`, one linear 2048x1024 channel.
    - It is stored uncompressed, because BC4's 4x4 blocks showed overhead.
    - It is sampled in HDRP's upper-hemisphere latitude-longitude layout at full detail, because a mip chosen from the derivative would draw a seam where the azimuth wraps.
    - It turns with the climate clock at the cloud layer's rate.
  - Colour: the sky behind the pixel, 85% whitened to the sun's colour at the same luminance, times 2.2. Opacity: the map times 3.5, faded out below about 14 degrees of elevation. The cirrus therefore follow dawn, dusk and the exposure without a light scale of their own, and HDRP's fog then veils them as it veils the sky.
  - Weight: `look` x `ClearVisibility(storm)`. The cirrus are gone by medium snow and at night, and the pass is off whenever the weight is 0.
  - Placed after the fog instead (`BeforePreRefraction`), the cirrus read harsher and lay over the volumetric clouds, so that placement was not used.
- **Measured.** In `b2-hold`, shots alternated pass on, off, on within one session; the measure is the mean linear luminance of the lower 40% (open snow):
  - 07:00: .3924 and .3916 on, .3918 off;
  - 14:00: .5704 and .5698 on, .5700 off.
  - Both are within shot-to-shot noise; the cloud layer added 26-93% of sky light at these hours. The sun's share of the snow's light is therefore unchanged. The sky in the same views was 12-15% brighter.
  - Evidence pairs (`cirrus-before`, `cirrus-after`): 10 clear-day views (07:00, 14:00, 18:00 and 20:00, toward and away from the sun, plus 14:00 up and overhead) change 6-40% of pixels. Medium snow and a clear night change no pixel by more than 8/255.
- **Cost.** By clear day: a full-screen copy of the camera colour and a full-screen pass that discards everything but the sky, plus a second 16-bit colour buffer (about 29 MB at 2560x1440). Not measured separately.
- **Checks.** Package `ci-01`: gameplay 387, weather 80 and ui 463 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.93-test/ci-01`). Gameplay peaked at 6.90 GB, against 6.64 GB on `vp-01`.

## Visual pass: faces as skin and no grid on distant snow, November 2, 2026 (0.6.92-test)

The user asked for improvements that could start at once and be confirmed. Three were proposed and approved: faces in the talk close-up, a grid pattern in mid-distance snow, and thin cirrus on empty clear-day skies that keeps the sun's share of the snow's light (October 24). The first two ship. The cirrus could not keep that share and was left out (below).
- **Faces.** The 18 Winter-bodied faces render as skin. The survivors (Frost, Moss and Shaw) keep their body texture under the period overlay.
  - `Tools/polar_face_maps.py` writes beside each `Resources/PolarCharacters/Faces/FaceNN.png`:
    - `FaceNN_NRM`: a normal map from the painting's fine detail (luminance high-passed at 5 px, strength 3; the eye pictures and the mouth strip stay flat);
    - `FaceNN_Mask`: smoothness .40 on skin, .48 on the lips, .22 on hair and beard, .88 on the eye pictures and .30 on the mouth strip;
    - `FaceNN_Skin`, for the nine bearded people: the painted beard under the hair cards darkened to 45%, fading in below the cards' roots, so the gaps between cards read as depth instead of bright paint;
    - the shared `FaceSubsurface`: 1 on skin, .35 on hair and beard, 0 on the eyes.
  - `PolarFaceSkinAuthor` (Tools > Polar Art > People > Author face skin) binds them on `PersonNFace.mat` in place: HDRP Lit subsurface scattering with the HDRP package's Skin diffusion profile, occlusion remapped to 1 (the painting carries its own shading) and metal 0.
  - The faces and their maps import at 512 px with high-quality compression.
  - `PolarArtCapture` has a new opt-in section, `faces`: each person seen from 0.7 m in front of the eye, as in the talk close-up.
- **Distant snow.** Open snow from about 20 m showed a grid of converging lines and dashes, plainest at night.
  - Cause: as a tile of a tiled ground map shrinks to tens of pixels, the map's own variation repeats as a lattice. The FreshPowder albedo and normal also carry a regular grain of 12 cycles a tile, which the broad rotated sample magnified into a 1.7 m dimple grid.
  - Fix, in `PolarData.hlsl` `PolarGroundSurface` (the tessellated `PolarGround` twin includes it, so no rewrite of the twin):
    - each tiled albedo's variation and the packed normal fade to the map's mean (level 12) as a tile shrinks from 64 to 16 px of the footprint's geometric mean;
    - the FreshPowder normal, and the gloss read from its albedo, fade out from 5 to 2 px a grain cycle along the view;
    - the broad sample reads no finer than level 8.5.
  - Snow within about 20 m keeps all its detail.
- **Measured.** Over the grid's area (`dist` shots), the strongest periodic peak against the median of its band (periods 6-60 px), where plain snow gives about 3:
  - at night (`dist-11`), 4.2 before and 2.9 after;
  - in medium snow by day (`dist-06`, `dist-07`), 5.7 and 5.4 before, 3.4 and 3.3 after.
  - The `b5-land` frames, the `b3` hut shots and every other `dist` frame change by at most 0.66% of pixels.
- **Clear-day cirrus (tried, not shipped).** HDRP's cloud layer was given a map with the overcast coverage in red and HDRP's own cirrus and wispy channels in blue and alpha, weighted onto clear days. Nights and snowfall were bit-for-bit unchanged.
  - Measured in `b2-hold`, linear, as the sun's share of open snow's light (direct sun alone over the total). At full weight: at 14:00, 41.0% to 39.6%; at 07:00, 9.3% to 5.1%, where the snow's sky light nearly doubled.
  - With the weight cut to (sin elevation / sin 32)^2 and the indirect light cut by 6% on clear days, averaged over alternating shots: 14:00 41.4% against 41.0%, 07:00 9.7% against 10.4%, 20:00 13.3% against 15.8%. A (sin elevation / sin 32)^4 weight still added 26% of sky light at 18:00.
  - Cause: HDRP bakes the layer's light with a Henyey-Greenstein forward lobe (g .7, `BakeCloudTexture.compute`), so cirrus near the sun are about 70 times brighter than cirrus 90 degrees away. The layer also turns slowly with the climate clock, so the sky light it adds depends on where its cirrus lie relative to the sun, not only on the sun's height.
  - Routes left open: a cirrus layer drawn only in the view, kept out of HDRP's sky probes (a far dome with its own fog), or the layer's lighting turned off with its brightness set by hand (the flag is shared with the weather deck, so it would need a second layer).
  - The cirrus measurements are in `Logs/ProjectUpdateCapture/polar-art/vp-share` and `vp-share2`.
- **Baseline.** The same `b2-hold` view now measures the sun's share at 14:00 as 41.0% (45% on October 24). The cause was not investigated here.
- **Cost.** The ground reads its two tiled maps' level-12 means as two more texture reads a ground pixel. HDRP's subsurface pass now runs while a face is on screen. Neither was measured separately.
- **Checks.**
  - Face evidence: 8 genuine close-up pairs (old face assets in `vp-face-before`, new in `vp-face2`; 0.9-2.3% of pixels changed, on the faces and beards) and 8 portrait pairs (`vp-before`, `vp-final2`).
  - Snow evidence: 6 frame pairs and 2 cropped grid pairs (`vp-before`, `vp-final2`).
  - Package `vp-01`: gameplay 387, weather 80 and ui 463 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.92-test/vp-01`); gameplay peaked at 6.64 GB.

## The view in hard walking, October 30, 2026 (0.6.87-test)

The player's view shows the hard-walking rules (`docs/engineering/systems/polar-gameplay.md`, top section) in `PolarBodyFeedback`.
- **Down.** While the player is down (`fallenAt + fallSeconds`), the view turns up toward the sky (`LookUp`, -65 degrees) over the 1 s going down, holds while lying, and comes back down over the 2.75 s getting up, eased at both ends. The eye follows the body's lying head as for rest (`PolarPlayerBodyView.Rest`, which now also plays the fall's clips).
- **Landing.** A landing from 2 m (`Stumble`) jolts the view 8 degrees down, 3 degrees of roll and 0.15 m lower, fading over 0.35 s. Under 3.5 m (`Down`) the jolt is half that.
- **Gusts.** A gust's shove leans the view downwind by up to 2.5 degrees of roll at its peak in full wind (`PolarWalkEffort.Shove` / 1.2, by the downwind side), but not while down.

Presentation only; the rules decide when.

## World v20's distant blocks keep v19's resolution, October 30, 2026 (0.6.86-test)

World v20 is 0.707 of v19 across (`docs/engineering/systems/polar-world.md`, top section).
- **Resolution.** `PolarWorldStreamer.SpacingMaximum` stops a block's resolution where its vertices around the ring would come closer than a limit. v16's limit is 0.82 of v15's 5 m, so v16's blocks kept v15's resolutions. v20's is 0.707 of v16's (2.9 m), so its blocks keep v19's resolutions and vertex counts, with cells 0.707 of v19's metres.
- **Render only.** Collision is the near chunks'.
- **Seed 777, by the home hut.** A lattice unit is 4.6 m around the ring and 20.6 m along the radius. The near window is about 185 by 825 m.

**The "slab" in the 900 m firelight capture (wv-03, wv-04) was drawn snow, not distant terrain.** An Editor probe at that viewpoint narrowed it down:
- Hiding the distant blocks, the other near chunks and both HDRP water surfaces changed nothing.
- Drawing the eye's own chunk with an unlit material removed it.
- Raising the eye 0.5 m moved it about 60 px, so it lay about 5 m away.

What the image showed was the snow drawn up to 0.45 m above the collider (`PolarSnowDepth`), plus the tessellated twin's crests. It lay on the lee of a bare crest 3-5 m ahead, seen almost edge-on. The test's sight line cleared the collider there by only 0.4 m. v19's capture shows the same pale band beside its sight line. The firelight views now keep 0.8 m over the collider (`PolarActsRuntimeValidation.TerrainLineClear`).

## Breath paced by its sounds, October 29, 2026 (0.6.80-test)

The user asked for the visible breath to match the pace of the breathing heard. Until now each person's clouds kept their own cycle, so a cloud and a breath out in the recording met only by chance. Presentation only (`PolarBreath`, `PolarActionSounds`, `PolarAudio.PlayAt` now returns its source).
- **Marks.** Breaths out in each breathing recording, read from where the take is loudest with low or voiced sound (a breath in is quieter and airier). Times are in seconds into the take:

  | Take | Breaths out |
  |---|---|
  | `Pant1` | .25, 2.3, 3.85, 5.45 |
  | `Pant2` | .08, .88, 1.73 |
  | `ColdBreath1` (12.5 s loop) | 0, 1.45, 3.4, 5.35, 7.05, 8.95, 10.75 |
  | `SleepBreath1` | 1.72, 4.38, 6.66, 8.62 |
  | `SleepBreath2` | 2.39, 6.04, 9.81, 13.52, 17.54 |
  | `Dying1` | 0, 1.25, 2.98 |

- **Following** (`PolarBreath.Follow`). While a person's breathing is heard, each breath out starts as the source passes a mark (`AudioSource.time`, so pitch does not matter), and the cycle waits at its end for the next mark instead of starting another breath. A take played once leads until it ends: the player's panting after a run, an explorer's or the player's weak breaths under 15 health. A loop leads while it is handed over every frame and no other take is heard: the player's cold breathing (wet, or under rest temperature plus 1), and the sleeping breath of the nearest sleeper within 8 m. Once the take ends or is no longer handed over, the person's own cycle resumes from where it stopped. Takes without marks are not followed.
- **Evidence:** [breath-sound-pace](../logs/evidence/2026-10-29/breath-sound-pace/), three cases from a new chapter of the `breath` reel (`PolarFiveAreasCapture.Breath.cs`, `reel:breath`, `Tools/polar_reel.py --tour breath-sound`), each a sheet of eight frames over 3.5 s. Still frames cannot show timing, so the reel's log does: for every breath out of the player it writes where the cold breathing or panting take was. Before, clouds left at 2.53, 6.77 and 10.65 s into `ColdBreath1` and 1.34, 3.41 and 6.23 s into `Pant1`, none near a mark. After, they leave at 0.04, 1.51, 3.45, 5.41, 7.10, 9.02 and 10.81 s into `ColdBreath1`, 0.13, 0.91 and 1.76 s into `Pant2`, and 0.31, 2.35, 3.89 and 5.50 s into `Pant1`: each within 0.03-0.07 s after its mark (one or two frames at 30 fps). The sleeper case is drawn in a hut at night with its stove out and shows almost nothing, so it has no evidence pair.
- **Checks:** package `bs-01`: gameplay 382 (peak 6.53 GB) and weather 80 pass with no exception (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.80-test/bs-01`); weather's eight P95s are 16.67-16.75 ms (0.6.78-test: 16.68-16.77 ms).

## Breath from the player's mouth, October 29, 2026 (0.6.78-test)

The user's playtest of 0.6.76-test: the player's own breath appeared in mid-air in front of the view instead of from the mouth. 0.6.76-test formed it 25 cm before the eye, inside the view, so each cloud began as a puff floating in space. Presentation only (`PolarBreath`).
- **Mouth.** The player's breath now leaves the mouth: 11 cm below and 9 cm before the eye, under the view's lower edge at the default 60-degree field of view. It rises into the lower view as it moves out, entering at about 19 cm before the eye.
- **Motion.** Breath leaves at 0.6-1.2 m/s (was 0.35-0.8), so the cloud reaches 15-30 cm before the mouth before the drag hands it to the wind. Each puff now also leaves with the mouth's own motion, so a walking person's breath starts with them; a jump over 10 m/s (placing, a first frame) counts as none. This applies to explorers too.
- **Near fade** on both breath materials is 0.15 m (was 0.3), so the cloud shows as it rises into view rather than once it is well clear of the eye.
- **Evidence:** [breath-from-mouth](../logs/evidence/2026-10-29/breath-from-mouth/), the same seven `breath` cases as 0.6.76-test (a sheet of nine frames over 4.5 s each). In `fp-open-clear` the cloud now rises from the bottom edge and drifts off with the crosswind; before, it hung mid-view. Explorers still breathe from their own mouths.
- **Checks:** package `bm-01`: gameplay 382 (peak 6.53 GB) and weather 80 pass with no exception (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.78-test/bm-01`); weather's eight P95s are 16.68-16.77 ms (0.6.76-test: 16.67-16.72 ms; the mode paces at 60 fps).

## First-person sleeves and gloves, October 28, 2026 (0.6.77-test)

Part of the first-person arm rework ([gameplay](polar-gameplay.md#first-person-arms-october-28-2026-0677-test)). The held rig is drawn by the foreground camera on the ForegroundVariants keyword tuple, which has no normal or mask maps, so the sleeve recipe's quilted normal and mask never drew and the sleeve was one flat colour lit the same everywhere.
- **Cloth** (`PolarPeriodClothingAuthor.Cloth`, `Tools/polar_kit_maps.py`). The sleeve wears `KitSleeve`, a colour-only wool twill with felting and broad wear repeating every 12 cm. The gloves, which had no recipe, wear `KitGrain`, colour-only pebbled leather repeating every 6 cm. Both maps tile exactly, using noise filtered in frequency. They lie over the arm mesh's own uvs, which hold about 2.0 uv units a metre on the sleeve and 2.8 on the hand (within a fifth either way). Each is dyed by the matched colour over the map's linear mean, so the sleeve keeps the player's coat colour and the gloves their brown. The handbook's arms share these materials (`PolarHdrpBookMaterials`), so they match. The overlay has 139 recipes (`Gloves` 1 added).
- **Cuff** (`PolarCharacterArtBuilder.Split`, rerun in place by `ResplitFirstPersonSleeves`). A triangle is glove when all three corners lean on the hand's bones by more than .45, so the cuff follows a ring of vertices. Before, the mean of the corners cut each quad of the wrist diagonally, which showed as a row of teeth once the sleeve had a texture. 34 and 25 triangles move from the gloves to the sleeves; vertices, uvs and weights are unchanged.
- **Evidence:** the 38 first-person pairs of [first-person-arms](../logs/evidence/2026-10-28/first-person-arms/) show the wool sleeves and grained gloves wherever the arms are in view.

## Breath in the cold, October 28, 2026 (0.6.76-test)

Each breath out of the player and of every explorer within 25 m leaves a small cloud that grows and goes downwind (`PolarBreath`, on the game controller). Presentation only: rules, saves and worlds are unchanged.
- **Air.** How much shows follows the air the survival rules give the person, not their temperature. `PolarActions.ShelterFactor` is a hut's factor (intact 1, damaged .5) or a pitched tent's .5 for whoever is inside it and not in a hut; the survival step now calls it instead of keeping its own copy. Opacity is `1-.3·shelter`, eased over 1 s, times `max(0,1-1.4·shelter)` at a lit fire (`AtLitFire`), eased over 4 s. So: outdoors 100%, a tent or damaged hut 85% (25% by its fire), an intact hut 70% (none by its lit stove). Nothing shows while swimming with the mouth under water.
- **Cycle.** 4 s at rest, 1.5 s running, 5.5 s asleep (gain .4), each varied ±10% by a slow sine and started at a phase from the seed and the person. Effort rises toward running (1) or walking (.35) at .5 a second and falls at 1/8 a second, so breathing calms over about 8 s after a run. The cycle runs on `Time.deltaTime`, so a pause holds it and sleep's 10x (game time only) does not hurry it. Eight particles leave over the breath-out window (.55 s at rest, .3 s running) at opacity .35, times up to 1.4 with effort and halved for the player. Speech beats give a faint breath out (`PolarNpcView`, .5), and calls and waking a stronger one (the gesture bus, 1.5). Both restart the same cycle, so they never double.
- **Mouths.**
  - Explorers: 11 cm before the head bone along the body's forward, measured in each prefab's upright pose (`PolarCharacterPresentation.Awake`). It follows turning, looking down and lying, on both body types. They count as running above 2.5 m/s (the explorers' Run rule).
  - The player: 10 cm below and 25 cm before the eye along the view, so the cloud forms low in the view, past the particle shader's 0.3 m near fade. At 8 cm before the eye it never showed.
  - Captures that stand the camera somewhere else draw the player's cloud at the camera.
- **Motion.** A puff leaves the mouth at 0.35-0.8 m/s and is dragged (4 a second) to the wind's speed within about .15 s. Wind speed (0.4-7 m/s) and life (1.1-0.35 s) come from the route flags' strength (`.2+.8·wind/.7+.3·gust`, so gusts count once), along `EffectiveWindHeading`. Each puff also rises .12 m/s, starts at 3-5 cm, grows six times and carries slow noise. Under shelter there is only the rise, and life is 1.3 s.
- **Drawing.** Two shared world-space particle systems (256 each) under the atmosphere.
  - The outdoor system's material is clipped by the huts' and tents' volumes (`PolarAtmosphere.ClipIndoors`, `PolarSnowShelter`), so a cloud from outside never shows within.
  - The indoor system draws people under shelter, tinted .5, since it is lit as the snow outdoors.
  - Both are `PolarParticle` in the drift's sky-lit card mode on Smoke28's puff (`PolarSnowLayers.VendorMaterial`, now static): lit and fogged as the snow, faded near surfaces and the eye.
  - `PolarSnowLayers.Apply` now sets the sky-lit inputs (`_PolarSnowGain`, `_PolarSnowSun`) in clear weather too; before, they were set only while it snowed.
  - Past 25 m only the phase moves; from 12 m to 25 m the clouds fade out.
- **Evidence:** [breath-cold-air](../logs/evidence/2026-10-28/breath-cold-air/), 7 cases from `PolarFiveAreasCapture` group `breath` on the camp save. Each case runs 4.5 s at 1/30 s, and its nine frames at 0.5 s form one sheet. The cases:
  - the player outdoors in clear weather, and facing into a blizzard;
  - Adler across the wind, and in the hut with the stove out and lit;
  - Frost in the tent by its lit burner, and on a clear night.

  The phase starts from loading time, so which frames show a cloud differs between runs.
- **Checks:** package `br-02`: gameplay 382 (peak 6.51 GB) and weather 80 pass with no exception (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.76-test/br-02`). Weather's eight P95s are 16.67-16.72 ms (0.6.73-test: 16.70-16.84 ms); the mode paces at 60 fps, so only a cost past the frame budget would show. `br-01` failed gameplay on a null state after quitting, now guarded (`live`).

## Snow at gap lips ends in a face, October 28, 2026 (0.6.74-test)

The teeth along lead banks and crack lips (open since 0.6.70-test; [water and ice](#water-and-ice-floes-shore-ice-broken-rims-and-weather-on-the-water-october-27-2026-0670-test)) were the drawn snow falling from its 0.15-0.45 m to 0 at every lip point within one near cell (0.57 by 0.76 m since 0.6.59-test), and the bank shading (`dTilt`) lit each fan facet there as a 45-degree slope. World hashes, navigation, saves and `SnowAt` are unchanged; colliders change only by the small faces the cull now keeps (Holes, below).
- **Lip** (`PolarWorldStreamer.CutFracturedTriangle`). Lip points take the depth their grid triangle draws there (`PieceDepth`), which `SnowAt` already read, so people, items, footprints and footstep surfaces agree with the drawn snow up to the edge (a thinning ramp was rejected for moving all of them). Each boundary edge whose midpoint lies on the outline (`OnGapLip`) gets a face of two triangles down to copies of its ends at depth 0 (`LipFoot`), where the wall tops begin. The face shares the snow's edge vertices, so it stays closed under the tessellated relief. It has no area before the lift, so it joins the mesh after the numerical-face cull (`MeshScratch.lipFaces`), and collider cooking drops it. Near and distant meshes alike.
- **Shader** (`PolarData.hlsl`, `PolarGroundSurface`). A near-vertical face across which the drawn depth changes (rock cliffs carry none) is textured on its own plane instead of streaked from above, and the bank tilt is off there (its solve has no ground area).
- **Holes.** The numerical-face cull dropped every face under 0.005 m², which took whole small fan triangles at lips (a 2 cm lip edge 0.5 m from its piece's centre) and left dark slivers through the lifted snow. It now drops faces under 5 mm across (twice the area over the longest edge).
- **Evidence:** [gap-lip-snow](../logs/evidence/2026-10-28/gap-lip-snow/), 4 pairs from `PolarArtCapture` b4-ice-water: before `wi-fix3` (0.6.72-test's code), after `wi-cut2`. The group's other 16 shots change by 1.4% of pixels or less (water and slabs move).
- **Checks:** package `gl-01`: gameplay 382 (peak 6.54 GB) and physical 2706 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.74-test/gl-01`).

## Player body after the character animation work, October 26-27, 2026

The character animation plan's M2 and M4 ([plan](../plans/polar-character-animation.md), [gameplay](polar-gameplay.md#character-presentation-october-28-2026-character-animation-m6-release)) change what Series 1's player body (B7, below) shows; released in 0.6.73-test. Presentation only: the player's place, collider, eye position in the plane and input are gameplay's.
- **When it shows** (`PolarPlayerBodyView.Shown`, checked by the presentation contract's lifecycle table). The lower view and the shadow show while standing, walking, falling, swimming and climbing (B7 hid the body in the water and on the climb). Lying down, asleep, getting up and dead the body casts its shadow where it lies (`PolarRest`: the hut cot within reach, the player's own bag, or clear floor found by a capsule check) and the lower view is off.
- **Controller.** `PlayerWinter.controller` is still derived from `Winter.controller` by `PolarPlayerGaitAuthor` (the signed `GaitDirection` on Walk and Run) and so carries all three layers: the base with the water, rest and death states, Gesture (hits, mirrored by the attacker's side) and Talk (nothing plays on it for the player). Foot planting is off in the water, on the climb and while lying.
- **Water** (`PolarWaterMotion`). A fall of 1.2 m or more plays `WaterDrop` in the air, entering the water the pool fall from where the feet are under; then treading with the head bone 0.14 m above the surface, or swimming 0.02 m under it with the legs toward the travel; the climb's hang and stand run on `PolarSurveyPlayer.ClimbProgress` and ease onto the climb's path.
- **The eye.** Lying down, asleep, getting up or dead, the eye eases over 0.25 s to 0.06 m above the lying head's height (at least 0.12 m above the player's place) and back to 1.62 m over 0.1 s once up, applied after `PolarSurveyPlayer` places the camera. Height only, so on a cot it sees from beside the body. Swimming and climbing keep gameplay's eye.
- **First-person rig.** `Weapons.controller` has 37 states (20 added: stove, burner and food, water, doors, flaps and tent work, calls, the map, notes and writing). Props (flask, tin, ration and pencil) are rig children made before the foreground lighting takes over the rig's materials (layer 10, no shadows).
- **Not shown.** The body does not kneel for tent work, and the eye stays standing for it (since 0.6.77-test both kneel: [gameplay](polar-gameplay.md#first-person-arms-october-28-2026-0677-test)); reflections still show only what B7's note says.

## Water and ice: floes, shore ice, broken rims and weather on the water, October 27, 2026 (0.6.70-test)

Codex's handoff `PLAN (9).md` ([archived](../plans/polar-water-ice-codex-handoff.md); [adjusted plan](../plans/polar-water-ice.md)) with twelve concept images (archived at 1280 px in `docs/art/water-concepts/`, SHA-256 of the originals in
its `manifest.json`), adjusted to the code: no new components, tuning assets or worker-thread passes; constants live in their owners.
Render only: world hashes, colliders, navigation, ice rules, swimming, sounds and saves are unchanged.

- **Floes** (`PolarArtLeads`, concepts 01, 04, 06). A floe stands `Freeboard(r)` = clamp(.35 + .05 r, .45, 1.6) m above its water,
  where r is its drawn radius (the user chose the concepts' proportions over October 22's 2.5 x height). Each silhouette's cut face and
  wet band take one material, `Owned floe sides` (`_PolarIcePart` 3), shaded by height above the floe's own waterline, which the shader
  reads from the object origin (`PolarFloeSide` in `PolarData.hlsl`): blue-white ice lighter upward with vertical streaks and a few
  bubbles, an uneven dark wet band 2-8 cm high at the waterline, and lighter turquoise ice below it for the water's refraction.
- **Sea floes.** The 72 floes past the coast (`PolarWorldStreamer`) keep their spots, turns and footprints and are drawn by the owned
  silhouettes (`PolarArtLeads.SeaFloe`, radius = the legacy footprint's half-length x scale x .96, variant from the seed and index); the
  streamer hands them to `PolarLeads.Build`. The Built-in path keeps the legacy prefabs.
- **Floe motion** (`PolarLeads.Update`). Bob .08 m for small floes to .03 m at 20 m and more; tilt and turn so the edge moves at most
  6 and 8 cm (never past 1.2 and .8 degrees); full motion to 250 m, at rest by 400 m (was everything to 2 km).
- **River and lake shore ice** (`PolarArtLeads.RiverShoreIce`, concepts 02, 07, 08). Both banks of every open run (v18/v19, not
  tributaries, depth at least `WaterDepth`): from .3 m under the bank, where the carve leaves the floor half from the axis, .3-2.5 m out
  over the water, at most .35 x half; a quarter bare, a 6 cm crack about every 4 m, tapering out over a run's last 6 m, and a thin
  plate (6 cm, .4-1.2 m, 5-7 corners) loose off the edge about every 25 m. Points every 2 m (`RiverSpacing`; 1.5 m gave 483,000
  vertices); pieces of 64 m whose LODGroup stops drawing them at .16 of the screen (about 350 m); no shadows, no colliders, snow
  excluded. Seed 777's v19 world: 311,138 shore-ice vertices with the leads' 25,848, and 1,153 pieces.
- **Shore-ice and slab tops** (`PolarIcePiece`, concept 10). Vertex colour r marks frost: 1 at a ribbon's bank and a slab's rim, .35 at
  a ribbon's water edge and 0 at a slab's centre (`Buffer.Prism`'s hub). Frosted tops keep the old glaze; clear tops are grey-blue with
  sparse bubbles and glossier. A broken hole's face keeps its 5-9 cm bright band and darkens to wet near-black within 14 cm below it
  (was 62 cm), so from standing height the hole reads as a thin edge over dark water.
- **Frozen river ice** (`PolarRiverIce`). Broad dark-clear patches against frost, two sparse layers of white bubbles, and cell cracks at
  half strength; ground-snow dust unchanged.
- **Pond water** (`PolarArtWater.Create`): `_PolarWaterDeep` (.014, .026, .035), toward slate blue beside the HDRP inland water.
- **Water follows the weather** (`PolarArtWater.Weather`, once a frame from `PolarThinIceRuntime`). By the blended `storm`: the sea's
  swell 12 to 20 km/h (HDRP's amplitude table at a 500 m patch: about 0 to .2 m; 30 km/h would raise .8 m crests over small floes and a
  swimmer's eye), chaos .6 to .8, ripples 6 to 14 and foam .2 to .4; inland ripples 4 to 10 (gusts are left out since 0.6.72-test: each
  would rebuild the spectrum). Swell and
  ripples face the effective wind turned half round, since HDRP's orientation is where the wind comes from. Each write rebuilds HDRP's
  spectrum, so a value is written only when it moves 0.5 km/h or 1 degree.
- **Checks.** Sixteen before/after pairs (`docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-10-27/polar-water-ice`); the intact-ice control is
  unchanged. Seed 777: 1,221 floe visuals (the 72 sea floes added), 24 hidden, bank gap 2 m and floe gap 2.5 m as before, no colliders
  (a silhouette prefab with one is refused). An Editor probe moved HDRP's swell toward -x at orientation 0. Gameplay 377, physical 2706,
  performance 59 (every P95 16.7 ms, as rh-01) and weather 80 pass on `wi-01` (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.70-test/wi-01`).
- **Not done.** Surf and wet rock at the sea's shore (concept 12): HDRP foam generators and water decals are off and the ground shader has
  no coast distance. Lake ends have no shore ice round their rounded ends (the ribbons run along the banks and taper out).
- **Follow-up (0.6.72-test, build `wi-02`).**
  - **Pale triangles on lead water** (`b4-lead-floe-near`, there before 0.6.70-test). A gap's floor runs straight between its outline
    points (lead 6 of seed 777: ten points over 1.6 km) while each wall's foot followed the ground 70 m down, so where the bank rose above
    that straight line a slot opened between wall and floor. The eye saw through the water, out under the land, to the dry-land water
    exclusion (`PolarArtWater.Dry`, at the sea's datum + .5 m, here 56 m under the rim), and HDRP hid the lead water there. Walls now reach
    the gap's lowest floor (`BuildGapWalls`); no vertex is added.
  - **Teeth along lead banks** (same shot): not the walls' tops (wall points one near cell apart left them unchanged, so that was
    reverted); fixed in 0.6.74-test ([snow at gap lips](#snow-at-gap-lips-ends-in-a-face-october-28-2026-0674-test)).
  - Swell against the flags: right by HDRP's source (`SimulationCPU` wind = -`OrientationToDirection`); a blizzard's sea crest (~.22 m
    at 20 km/h) stays under the smallest freeboard less its bob (.37 m) and inland water has ripples only; pond water is the HDRP
    scattering hue at about a sixth of its brightness; breaking, swimming and climbing out are covered by gameplay and physical.

## Square near cells and crease-aware splits, October 26, 2026 (0.6.59-test)

The regular bumps along cliff tops, item 9 of the October 24 graphics audit, left open in 0.6.57-test. The audit blamed the fixed cell diagonal, but the near cells were slivers. A near chunk (8 lattice units) drew 128 by 128 cells of 1/16 unit. A unit runs 1.75 navigation units along the radius in the districts but r·2π/10752 round the ring, so a cell was 3-23 times longer than it was wide (0.41 by 1.84 m at seed 777's b3s-01). A sheer crest running round the ring therefore drew as a staircase of radial risers, a notch every few metres. This is the fault 0.6.31-test fixed for distant blocks (`Grid`).

The change is render and collision only. The world hash, navigation, saves, rules and distant blocks are unchanged. In worlds from v10 every near collider changes, including those of older saves; worlds before v10 keep 128 by 128 cells and the fixed split.

- **Grid.** `PolarStreamingPolicy.NearStretch(version, row)`: from world v10 a row of near chunks has 128/k columns k/16 apart and 128·k rows 1/(16k) apart.
  - k is 1, 2 or 4, chosen by Grid's rule (halve the columns while the radial cell is more than twice the ring cell) at the nominal radius of the row's centre.
  - k is 4 for z < 216 and 512 ≤ z < 656, and 2 elsewhere.
  - Every chunk in a row shares k, so chunks meet along the radius without stitching.
  - Cells stay at 16,384 a chunk; vertices go from 16,641 to 16,705 (k 2) or 16,929 (k 4). At b3s-01 a cell is 0.92 by 0.82 m.
- **Split.** `NearFlip`: a near cell splits along the diagonal joining its closer heights.
  - When |a−d| > |b−c| + 1 cm it is drawn as b,a,c and b,c,d: the same two triangles turned a quarter, with the same winding. Otherwise it stays a,c,d and a,d,b.
  - A crest crossing a cell keeps its corner cut off instead of folding a notch into the cell.
  - On a plane both splits are the same surface. Distant blocks keep the fixed split, which their morph and boundary code depend on.
- **Seams.** Where k changes (z 216, 512 and 656), the coarser row stitches its chunk edge to the finer row's columns (`NearEdgeStep`, `MeshScratch.nearSeam`). This reuses `BoundaryTriangle`'s centre fan, which near chunks now take on those edges only.
  - The distant cutout around the near window samples the near rows: 1/(16k) along its radial sides and `NearEdgeStep` round the ring.
  - `ValidateVisibleSeams` walks each chunk's own grid (keys in 1/64 lattice units). It returns 180 when a chunk on a band edge lacks a stitch vertex (a T-junction), and otherwise the largest normal angle at shared edge vertices, as before.
- **Mirrors.** Everything that reads the near facet under a point uses `NearCell` and `NearFlip`: `PolarWorldStreamer.SnowAt`, `PolarSurfaceCracks.Surface`, `PolarLandscapeScatter.NearMeshGround`, `PolarArtOutcrops.FacetAt` and `PolarArtCapture.RockClearance`. Their corner caches are keyed in 1/64 lattice units. `PolarSnowTracks`' 0.75 m cell bound still holds.
- **Effects.**
  - The crest at b3s-01, seen from above: deviation from a fitted line RMS 0.370 to 0.093 m, worst 0.654 to 0.146 m.
  - Square facets read the face's steepness, so more escarpment modules pass `PolarArtOutcrops`' test: at b3s-01, 9 to 26 accepted and 0 to 6 drawn.
  - The nearest walkable ground stays 0.41 m from a module for the camera and body and 0.38 m for the feet. The capsule radius is 0.35 m.
  - Crack lips and lead banks draw fewer, broader shards. Radial detail is finer and detail round the ring coarser.
- **Tools.** `PolarFiveAreasCapture` group `seams`: rows 656 and 512 near the camp, looking along and across the row from standing height, with the seam check logged at each stand.
- **Checks.**
  - Ten genuine pairs (`near-cells`): `PolarArtCapture` phases `nc-before` and `nc-after` over b3-distance and b4-ice-water; `PolarFiveAreasCapture` terrain, terrain2 and hollow; and the crest plan, drawn from a dump of the near triangles round b3s-01's face.
  - The `seams` group in `nc-after`: the seam check is 0 at rows 656 and 512, and no sky shows through the ground.
  - Package `nc-01` (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.59-test/nc-01`): gameplay 339 (peak 6.57 GB; ui-01 6.59) and performance 59 pass. Every performance P95 is at the 60 Hz cap, as in ga-01.
  - Fractured 29 passes. The three NPC shelter checks it had before went with NPC agency in 0.6.25.
  - Physical failed once in three runs. In the first run, none of 512 ground cells behind the maximum-stock hut (site 2, by the pole) would take item 1269, the 85th of 174.
  - Two reruns from the same start save passed 2,706 checks, and physical-resume 16 passed after the first rerun (`nc01-physical2`, `nc01-physical3`). ui-01 passed its one run (`ui01-physical`).
  - An Editor replay of the same loop with real frames placed all 173 items in 186 cells, so the cause was not found.
  - Loading profile, seed 777, new game, alternating with ui-01 twice each (`Logs/PolarLoadingProfile/2026-10-26/cmp-nc-*`): playable at 52.9 and 53.0 s (ui-01 54.2 and 59.1 s); distant refinement completes at 148.5 and 148.9 s (150.5 and 154.6 s). Refinement P95 is 8.33 ms in all four runs, the seam normal angle 0, and no T-junctions.
- **Not done.**
  - The shards along crack lips and lead banks, which were there before this change (fixed in 0.6.74-test).
  - Fixed in 0.6.60-test (build `pn-01`): `SnowAt` returned NaN on near cells by the pole, where corners round to one float (12 of 1,024 points behind site 2, 116 with the 1/16 cells). `PolarStreamingPolicy.ProjectedWeights` now gives a triangle with no area its first corner, so a drop view or person seated there no longer sits at NaN; the same 1,024 points and a 0.25 m sweep over 120 by 120 m round site 2 (231,361 points) give no NaN. That ground carries no snow, so nothing drawn changes.
  - Twice the rows (double the near vertices) is the next step if a staircase still shows.

## Graphics audit items 6-12, October 25, 2026 (0.6.57-test)

The rest of the October 24 graphics audit in one package ([decisions](../../design-decisions.md#darker-clear-nights-sun-in-snowfall-far-shadows-rock-hut-light-drifting-snow-and-water-october-25-2026-0657-test)); the user chose clear nights about 1.5 stops darker and a soft sun in medium snow. Render only: world hash, saves, rules and navigation are unchanged.
- **Clear nights (6).** `PolarArtCelestialSettings.asset`: clear-night indirect gain .342 to .2 and direct gain 1.083 to .38 (the field's range now reaches .1). `Night()` blends to the overcast values by `Cover(storm)` and to the twilight ones near 06:00 and 22:00, and `PolarHdrpSkyArt` raises the gains to `1-indoor`, so snow nights, the handover and interiors keep theirs. Clear-night open snow median 157 to 94 (`b5-land-night-clear`), the midnight horizon 161 to 97; ground contrast did not rise (std 8.9 to 8.3), so near moon shadows stay faint; distant ground takes the moon's far shadows (below).
- **Sun in medium snow (7).** `PolarArtCelestialPresentation.SunThroughSnow(storm)` is 1 to medium snow (.35) and 0 from snowstorm (.8); `SnowSun` multiplies it by `1-ClearVisibility`, so clear weather keeps 0.6.54's balance. By day, weighted by `SnowSun`: the deck thins towards `snowSunCloudDensity` (.4), the sky's indirect gain towards `snowSunIndirectGain` (.75; `DaySnowGain` may now go below 1), and the height fog lets the sun through. HDRP dims a directional light by the fog above the lit point (`EvaluateLight_Directional`), so the 10 km fog layer put the sun out in every snow grade; `PolarHdrpPresentation` now ends the even fog above the eye so that the sun crosses `PolarArtSettings.snowSunDepth` (.7) mean free paths straight up, more as `SnowSun` fades, with the fog along the ground unchanged. The sun's shadows follow `shadowDimmer = SunThroughSnow` by day (it was 1 in every weather). Medium snow median 176 to 184 with a visible shadow side; heavy snow slightly; snowstorm, blizzard and clear 14:00 unchanged.
- **Far shadows (8).** The cascades end at 500 m and distant blocks cast no shadows, so hills never shaded the plain beyond. `PolarFarShadows` (owned by the presenter) keeps the drawn heights (`PolarWorldController.RawHeight`) on a 727-point grid 31 m apart round the eye, lattice-solved every 22 points and interpolated, on worker threads when the eye moves 1 km or the world is made again (0.63 s on 20 cores); it sweeps them row by row away from the sun or moon into a 1024 px R8 map 16 km a side whenever the light turns 0.2 degrees (about every 0.5 s by day, about 4 ms on workers; one 1 MB upload). `PolarData.hlsl` `PolarFarShade` multiplies the ground's albedo past 425-500 m, where the cascades fade out: `lerp(1, A/(A+S), shadow)` with `A = (1-share)` x the shade's sky colour and `S = share x max(0, n.L)/max(.05, L.y)`, since a GBuffer pass cannot take out the direct light alone. The share is the light's part of open snow's light, measured in `b2-hold`: the sun 45% on a clear day at 32 degrees and 30% in medium snow, scaled with sin(elevation); the moon 85% on a clear night; fading through heavy snow, 0 from snowstorm on, on snowy nights and indoors. By day the shade takes the sky's colour (`PolarArtSettings.farShadeSky` (.7, 1, 1.6), measured under the hut); without it the shade read as a grey stain. Seen at 07:00 from 150 m up, 7% of the picture darkens (up to 67 levels); views from the ground show little ground past 500 m.
- **Rock (9).** Rock albedo .11, .115, .125 to .05, .052, .057 (`PolarGroundLook.Rock` and the `PolarSurface.shader` default together; the `PolarGround` twin rewritten). Cracks darken from the rock map's own height (`PolarRockCavity`: to .55 of the albedo from height .5 down to .1) on slope rock and kind-7 rocks. A distant pixel's partial rock cover leans to the rock (`rock = lerp(rock, sqrt(rock), saturate((fw-.3)/.7))`) instead of averaging into a pale grey stain. Cliff face at 28 m: darkest 5% 66 to 49.
- **Cliff-top bumps (9), not changed.** Every terrain cell, near and distant, splits along the same diagonal (`PolarWorldStreamer.ComputeMeshBody`), so a sheer face crossing the grid obliquely folds into a regular zigzag along its crest. Fixes: near cells square-ish at the outer ring, or crest-aware splitting and vertex heights; both change the walkable mesh and colliders and need the physical modes. Fixed in 0.6.59-test, where the main cause turned out to be the near cells' shape ([square near cells](#square-near-cells-and-crease-aware-splits-october-26-2026-0659-test)).
- **Hut light (10).** The stove lamp sits low in front of the firebox (since 0.6.99-test, in front of the kerosene heater's pane) (`PolarSiteView.StoveLightOffset` (0, -.55, -.85), was (-.5, -.1, 0) beside the shell, which burned its side white). While the stove burns (HDRP only), a shadowless warm point fill at (0, 2.6, 4), range 8, takes .7 of `PolarHdrpPresentation.StoveIntensity`, and a shadowless warm spot outside the window at (1.5, 1.9, -.65), aimed down and out, 110 degrees, range 9, takes .5 x the pane's glow (`PolarArtHutDressing.PaneGlow`, night only). Night room median 4 to 26, darkest 5% 0 to 4; the stove's side is iron again (2% of the stove picture above 230, the hot floor in front); a lit stove by day lifts its room slightly (median 44 to 49).
- **Blowing snow (11).** Ground drift and gust sheets draw owned wisps (`Resources/Polar/HDRP/Snow/DriftStreaks.png`, written by `Tools/polar_drift_streaks.py`) with no atlas, so the round cell mask no longer applies; chimney smoke keeps Smoke28, and its mipped copy is gone. Drift: size .12-.3 m, length 4 x size plus .14 x speed, 1,300 a second up to 1,800, alpha .14-.30, height .03-.33 m.
- **Water (12).** `PolarArtWater.Surface`: absorption 6 m, refraction colour (.10, .20, .24), scattering colour (.07, .15, .21) and ambient scattering 2. HDRP lights `.25 x ambientScattering` (summed with the wave terms and saturated) of the scattering colour like a diffuse surface, and does not clamp it to the inspector's 0-1. Lakes and bays from above: sRGB (2, 8, 20) to (7, 37, 60). Sky reflection 512 px (`PolarPipeline.asset`, was 256).
- **Tools.** `Tools/polar_tone_stats.py <before> <after> names...` (or `--concepts`) reports the darkest 5%, median, ground contrast and saturation against the concepts (day 54/160/41, night 31/59, interior 6/29).
- **Checks.** 31 genuine pairs (`graphics-6-12`: `PolarArtCapture` phases `ga-before` and `ga-final` over sky-time, b5-land, b5-snow, b6-habitat, b3-distance and b4-ice-water; `PolarFiveAreasCapture` hollow, lake19 and terrain; far shadows off and on in one `b2-hold` run, `ga-final-fs`). The first frames after a shader import drew the ground black once (`ga-fs5`, 7 s after entry); the same code drew them normally in two later runs. Package `ga-01`: gameplay 339, weather 80 (every grade's P95 under 20 ms, as sf-01), performance 59 (blizzard P95 16.7 ms) and ui 413 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.57-test/ga-01`); performance passed on its second run, the first having found the camera pitched 60 degrees up (mouse input, kept in `player/performance-first`). Loading, alternating with sf-01, two runs each: a new expedition playable in 53.5-53.7 s (sf-01 53.1-53.2 s), distant refinement complete in 152-154 s and its P95 12.5 ms as sf-01's (`Logs/PolarLoadingProfile/2026-10-25/cmp-ga-*`). Gameplay peaked at 6.45 GB (sf-01 6.29).
- **Not done.** The cliff-top bumps (above; fixed in 0.6.59-test); near clear-night contrast; far shadows on the distant range backdrop (kind 6) and from objects.

## Smaller, whiter stars and sea floes in the water, October 25, 2026 (0.6.56-test)

Item 5 of the October 24 graphics audit, and two faults seen in 0.6.46-test's sea capture (boulders in the sky over the far shore, a dashed white line across the sea).
- **Stars.** The star field was a 512 px a face RGBAHalf cubemap from `PolarArtSkyAuthor.Stars` (450 stars a face, almost all of one brightness, slightly warm), so each star drew about 4 px across as a golden dot. It is now `ClearNightStars.png`, a 12288x2048 strip of six 2048 px faces written by `Tools/polar_star_map.py` (seed 42025) and imported as a linear cubemap (generateCubemap Auto, no convolution, mips, trilinear, DXT1; the asset keeps its guid, so `PolarArtCelestialSettings.stars` and `Resources.Load("Polar/HDRP/Sky/ClearNightStars")` are unchanged in use). 5,200 one-texel stars placed uniformly over the sphere: brightness 1 to 1/16 over three magnitudes with faint stars the most numerous, 55% white, 32% pale blue, 13% pale yellow. A 2048 px texel is about 0.044 degrees, under one pixel at 1600x900 and about one at 2560x1440. `starEmissionMultiplier` is .2 (was .006, for texels with 16 times the solid angle and values near 1-13); in the clear 00:00 capture a star covers a median 4 px above the background (17.5 before) with about the same peak brightness, and at 22:00 the stars are fine points in the twilight. `PolarArtSkyAuthor` no longer writes stars; `PolarArtCelestialAuthor` loads the PNG.
- **Sea floes.** The 72 floes past the coast (`PolarWorldStreamer`, since October 3) had their base, the model's pivot, 1.5 m above the sea, so at scales 1-5 (17-90 m across, 5-55 m tall) they hung over the water. The base is now 1 m a unit of scale under the sea. Bay and lead floes (`PolarLeads`, `PolarArtLeads`) were already on their water.
- **The capture, not the game.** The boulders in the sky and the dashed line came from the capture tool: `PolarFiveAreasCapture`'s sea shot put the eye 25 m above the sea at lattice row 1000, which on seed 777's camp column is inside an 89 m coastal hill. From inside, the land's faces are culled, so the stones on it hung in the sky and a chunk edge on the far slope drew as dashes. The shot now stands 25 m above the ground or the sea, whichever is higher, and a new `sea-floe` shot looks at the sea floe nearest the camp from 3 m above the sea.
- **Checks.** Six genuine pairs (`stars-sea-floes`: `PolarArtCapture` phases `sf-before` and `sf-after` over sky-time and b5-land, the clear 00:00 sky also cropped; `PolarFiveAreasCapture` hollow, the sea and the sea floe; the multiplier tuned at .1 in `sf-tune-0.1`). Package `sf-01`: gameplay 339 and weather 80 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.56-test/sf-01`); gameplay peaked at 6.29 GB (sb-01 6.26). The star map is 100 KB as a PNG and about 17 MB of texture memory with mips (the old cubemap about the same).
- **Not done.** The sea floes keep the original prefabs' glossy look; the bay floes have owned silhouettes (`PolarArtLeads`). Stars do not twinkle, and the Milky Way and constellations are not modelled.

## People's kit: packs, scarves, straps, fur and beards, October 24, 2026 (0.6.55-test)

Package B of the period clothing ([decisions](../../design-decisions.md#peoples-kit-packs-scarves-straps-fur-and-beards-october-24-2026-0655-test); Package A: [period fabric maps](#peoples-clothing-period-fabric-maps-october-24-2026-0652-test)). `PolarCharacterArtBuilder` (October 9) made the kit from rounded boxes and Unity cylinders with a 0-1 uv square on every face, the straps as chains of ten stretched boxes, in flat colour, and painted the Winter body's beards on its 512 px face.
- **Author.** `PolarPeopleKitAuthor` (Tools > Polar Art > Clothing > Author kit (Package B)) edits the 21 `Npc` prefabs in place. Every part keeps its name, bone, place and size, and a rerun writes the same meshes (`Resources/PolarCharacters/Meshes/Kit-*.asset`, seeded by person; meshes it replaced that nothing uses are deleted). `PlayerBody.prefab` keeps the old kit: its body draws only shadows.
  - Canvas parts (rucksacks, their flaps and pockets, frame packs, satchels and flaps, the scarf tail) are rounded boxes of ten steps a face whose flat parts swell like stuffed canvas (6-25% of the smaller half side). Blanket rolls, sleeping bags and duffels are soft rolls with rounded ends. Their uvs tile at a fixed world scale: canvas 12 cm, knit 8 cm, wool 20 cm.
  - Each wool neck scarf becomes two folded wraps round the neck, loose at the front.
  - Each strap and sash chain becomes one rounded leather band through the same points (Catmull-Rom, a 12-sided flattened section, flat ends) on the same bone: u across it, 17 cm of leather a tile along it. Where the builder's cast had missed the shirt, the survivors' strap chains ended 25 cm in front of the chest: a chain's last point lies at most 3 cm in front of the one before it. (A first rule, at most 3 cm forward at any point below the shoulder, flattened every strap's front run into the coat; the comparison showed the straps gone from the chest.)
  - The parka hoods (Bell, Grant) get 150 fur cards round the ruff's tube, leaning out of the face opening (`Cap_Hood ruff fur`, owned `HoodRuffCards.mat`).
  - The nine bearded Winter-bodied people get hair cards (`Beard cards` on the head bone): 809-909 for a full beard (Adler, Dale, Grant, Lane, Olsen), 105-110 for a moustache (Carter, Jones, Reed, Vale). They root where `HEAD.png` paints the beard (the rule of `PolarCharacterArtBuilder.FaceSource`), placed on the face triangles from the skinned head (the scaled Winter model is skinned by hand: `BakeMesh` misplaced it), grow down and out from the skin, use the STP beard atlas's eight clumps and are tinted with the person's hair colour (`Person<id>BeardCards.mat`; dark hair lifted so it keeps its shading). Cards are wound so their front faces outward.
- **Maps.** `Tools/polar_kit_maps.py` writes owned sets (albedo, `_NRM`, `_MaskMap`) into `Resources/Polar/HabitatArt/Period/`: `KitCanvas` (plain-weave duck with fine mottling only, so the tile does not show), `KitKnit` (stockinette, columns along the scarf) and `KitWool` (felted twill), 512 px, tileable and neutral for the dye; `KitLeather` (128x512, pebble grain, two stitch rows and burnished edges, tileable along v); `KitGloves` (1024, leather in the Winter hand texture's layout with its creases); `KitFur` (512, strands with alpha, roots at the top). `PolarPeriodClothingAuthor` binds them by each recipe's source albedo (packs, pack trim and hoods canvas, scarves knit, blankets wool; tiled fabric keeps at most .12 weathering, whose noise would otherwise blotch it) and adds kind-0 recipes for `LeatherStraps` and `WinterLeatherGloves`: 138 recipes.
- **Survivors' underclothes.** Without the stray strap ends, the survivor body's skin showed where it pushes through the plaid shirt. `SurvivorSkinGloved.png` now also paints dark wool (sRGB .24, .22, .20 over the three survivors' mean skin tint) on every triangle of the body weighted below the Neck bone, except the hands, which keep their gloves (43.5% of the map), shaded by the body's own luminance; head and neck keep the skin, so the open collar shows an undershirt. The survivor model's own T-shirt mesh was tried as the undershirt first and clipped through the shirt.
- **Portraits.** The 21 portraits (also the keepsake photos) are re-rendered in the new kit (`PolarArtCapture` section `portraits`, `Tools/polar_portraits.py`).
- **Cost.** In the Editor, seven people filling the view at 4 m (`people-perf`), alternating: frame median 10.73 and 10.72 ms with the kit, 11.09 ms without it; no measurable cost. The kit adds about 4,800 beard cards and 300 fur cards over all 21 people, and 16 maps (about 8 MB of texture memory).
- **Checks.** Three assemblies compile; the period author passes (138 recipes, same-cut palette minimum dE 11.5, the original catalog's hash unchanged). Nine genuine pairs (`people-kit`: `PolarArtCapture` phases `kit-before` and `kit-after-h`, all 21 front and back at 2.7 m, Adler and Frost from behind, Bell and Ellis from the side, Dale's head at 1.3 m and the 21 portraits; `PolarFiveAreasCapture` talk, Adler's close-up). Package `kt-01`: gameplay 339, performance 59 and ui 413 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.55-test/kt-01`); the hut-to-hut walk P95 is 16.71 ms at the 60 Hz cap (16.69 ms in cl-01) and gameplay peaked at 6.58 GB (cl-01 6.52).
- **Not done.** Goggles stay primitives; the fur caps keep their fur map without cards; the painted beard stays under the cards; faces stay 512 px without skin scattering; new character models (the plan's later step).

## The sun leads clear days, October 24, 2026 (0.6.54-test)

The light and grade package (below) found the sun giving about a fifth of sunlit snow's light on a clear day. The user asked for clear days where the sun dominates: a thinner clear-day deck for a darker blue sky, with the exposure recalibrated so the snow stays white.
- **Deck.** `PolarArtCelestialPresentation.Apply` blends the volumetric clouds' density from the weather's (.45 clear to .92 blizzard) to `PolarArtCelestialSettings.clearDayCloudDensity` (.12) by `ClearVisibility(storm)` (1 in clear weather, 0 from medium snow on), then to the clear night's `clearCloudDensity` (.1) by `clear x (1 - 2 day)` as before.
- **Exposure.** `PolarHdrpPresentation` opens the day side's EV (`dayEV`) by `PolarArtSettings.clearSkyExposureLift` (0.8) x `ClearVisibility(storm)`. The handover's `boundaryEV` is unchanged, so the dawn and dusk blend ramps the lift in, and nights, the 06:00/22:00 handover and every snow grade keep their exposure. Interiors by day meter at most the outdoor EV, so they take the lift too.
- **Measured.** In the held `b2-hold` fixture at 14:00, linear (tonemapping and grade off), the sun's share of sunlit snow's light by clear-day density: .45 22%, .30 30%, .20 37%, .12 45%, .06 49%; the lift that keeps the snow's brightness: +0.31, +0.55, +0.79 and +0.93 EV (at .12, +0.85 at 07:00 and +0.78 at 20:00). The step from sunlit snow to shade grows from about a third of a stop to almost a stop.
- **Result.** Sunlit snow keeps its brightness (open land on a clear day: mean linear .529 against .532) and is a little cooler. The upper sky at 14:00 is bluer (blue minus red +6 to +33) and about a tenth darker, since the lift brightens the blue too. Shade beside the hut and the entrance drift reads blue. At 07:00 and 20:00 the snow keeps its brightness and turns bluer (blue minus red +1 to about +30). The station seen from 200 m is lighter, with no clipping. Interiors by day: the dark rooms 0.3-1.1 EV lighter (the station's 1.1, the hut's gable 0.85, window 0.6, room 0.3), views toward daylight equal to 0.26 EV darker, all cooler. Medium snow to blizzard, clear nights and the 06:00/22:00 frames are unchanged (mean differences of at most 0.7/255).
- **Levers found.** `PhysicallyBasedSky`'s air density has no effect (the EarthAdvanced model ignores it); aerosol density .003 added one point of sun share; the indirect diffuse multiplier at .7 gave 54% but washed out the sky; the sky's own exposure at -0.5 and -1 EV gave 51% and 56% with a darker sky (previewed, not taken: `PolarHdrpSkyArt` steps the sky's radiance, which also lights the clouds, interiors and the moon).
- **Cost.** No new pass; the clear-day deck is thinner. Not measured separately: the weather mode's four grades (medium snow to blizzard) are untouched by the change.
- **Checks.** 14 genuine pairs (`PolarArtCapture` phases `sb-before` and `sb-after` over b5-land, sky-time, b6-habitat and b3-distance on 0.6.51-test; tuning in `b2-hold`, phase `sb-tune`). Package `sb-01`: gameplay 339 and weather 80 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.54-test/sb-01`); gameplay peaked at 6.26 GB, as bf-02 did.

## People's clothing: period fabric maps, October 24, 2026 (0.6.52-test)

The user found people's clothing poor in the talk close-up and chose the 1920s-1930s expedition look on today's models ([decisions](../../design-decisions.md#people-in-1920s-1930s-expedition-clothing-october-24-2026-0652-test)). The period overlay of October 15 (review only until now, see "People: period clothing option" below) becomes the default and gets owned fabric maps.
- **Maps.** `Tools/polar_garment_maps.py` (with `Tools/polar_fbx_uv.py`, a minimal binary FBX reader) writes 14 owned sets into `Resources/Polar/HabitatArt/Period/`, each in its source texture's own uv layout: `<set>.png` (RGBA albedo), `<set>_NRM.png` and `<set>_MaskMap.png` (metallic, occlusion, detail mask, smoothness). Each source albedo is split by scale in log luminance. Painted dark stains (much darker than their surroundings, wider than seams, larger than buttons; splatter is closed into one blotch first) are filled from the cloth around them, and PANTS' track stripes and mark likewise from texels much lighter than theirs. The broad painted light keeps 12-30% of its contrast, part of its darker half moving to occlusion; folds, seams, buttons and quilting keep about 85% and drive the normal map; fine grain comes from the source or from an owned STP fabric (ClothSack canvas, a seeded felt noise, the holster leather). Dyed cloth averages 0.7, which the dye multiplies (lift 1). Texels outside the uv islands, rasterised from the FBX one triangle at a time (mirrored halves share uv space), are push-pull filled, so mip levels do not bleed the background into seams. The same sources give the same bytes.
- **Sets.** Coat_1 (the Puffer cut) as quilted cotton, zip kept and the sleeve label removed; Coat_2 (Overcoat) wool melton; Coat_3 (FurCoat) even fur; Coat_4 (the long coat) waxed canvas with a natural sheepskin collar and facing (alpha 0, 13% of its texels, solid shapes from its light painted regions); PANTS wool trousers; the survivors' Shirt as heavy wool with its own normal and occlusion (the check stays the shader's); Cap_1 fur without the badge; Cap_2 fur; Cap_3 and Cap_4 one-colour knit; Shoes_1-4 relief and masks over their own albedo (Shoes_3's camouflage gaiters become canvas).
- **Shader and catalog.** HabitatSurface kind 4 is the garment dye (kind 1) with the albedo's alpha as its mask: alpha 1 is dyed, alpha 0 keeps its own colour, and frost pales only the dyed cloth. `PolarArtMaterialCompatibility.Valid` accepts kinds 0-2 and 4. `PolarPeriodClothingAuthor` binds the sets by each recipe's source albedo (`Fabrics`; 52 garments become kind 4), adds Shoes_2 and Shoes_4, and gives the survivors' eyes a non-metal recipe (their STP material converted with metallic 1): 136 recipes. The first-person sleeve follows the quilted map (0.169, 0.255, 0.336). `PantsPeriod.png` and its stripe fill are retired; FurPeriod stays for the hood ruff. Mask maps import at 1024 at most.
- **Default.** `PolarArtSettings.periodClothing` is true (the asset holds no value, so the class default applies); `-polarClothing current` shows the Winter pack clothing for review, and the build check no longer refuses period clothing.
- **Beards.** `Person6/13/19Beard.mat` (owned HDRP/Lit) are alpha-clipped at 0.5 and double-sided, so the survivors' beards draw as hair instead of solid blocks.
- **Portraits.** `PolarArtCapture` section `portraits` renders the 21 people in the game's own presentation at the 14:00 clear fixture, framed by the October 9 rule (1.7 m in front of the head, a 30 degree lens), arms down and outdoors (the October 9 ones were Built-in renders in the bind pose on dark teal). `Tools/polar_portraits.py` crops each frame's centre 4:5 to 384x480 into `Resources/PolarCharacters/Portraits`, the roster portraits and the keepsake photos.
- **Cost.** `PolarArtCapture` section `people-perf` (seven people filling the view at 4 m, 1920x1080, uncapped, in the Editor), alternating: frame median 10.65 and 11.66 ms in the old clothing, 12.14 and 9.80 ms in the new; no difference beyond run-to-run noise. The Editor's GPU frame timer read 0.09 ms there and is not usable. The 39 maps take about 65 MB of texture memory.
- **Checks.** Three assemblies compile. The period author passes its checks (136 recipes, same-cut palette minimum dE 11.5, the original catalog's hash unchanged). Nine genuine pairs (`period-clothing`: `PolarArtCapture` phases `clothes-before` and `clothes-after-c`, all 21 front and back at 2.7 m, the 30 m lineup, Adler and Frost, Adler's and Shaw's heads at 1.3 m and the 21 portraits; `PolarFiveAreasCapture` talk, Adler's close-up). Package `cl-01`: gameplay 339, performance 59 and ui 395 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.52-test/cl-01`); the hut-to-hut walk P95 is 16.69 ms (16.68 ms in earlier packages) and gameplay peaked at 6.52 GB (ks-02 6.18, lg-01 6.43).
- **Not done (a later package).** Packs, scarves, straps, hoods and goggles keep their primitive shapes in flat colour; the Winter-bodied people's beards stay painted on their 512 px faces; gloves are plain leather colour; faces have no skin scattering. (Packs, scarves, straps, hoods, beards and gloves: [0.6.55-test](#peoples-kit-packs-scarves-straps-fur-and-beards-october-24-2026-0655-test).)

## Light and grade, October 24, 2026 (0.6.50-test)

The user chose four quick wins from a graphics review. Against the 31 concepts, 182 captures of October 21-23 were flat and milky: the darkest 5% of pixels at 119/255 against 54, ground contrast (standard deviation) 18 against 42, fine detail a third, mid-tones neutral (blue minus red +3 against +18), saturation 5.2 against 15.6, the brightest 1% equal (209 against 206).
- **Sun shadows.** HDRP ignores `QualitySettings.shadowDistance` (600 m in `PolarAtmosphere`, a Built-in setting): the sun's shadows ended at the default volume profile's 150 m (`HDShadowSettings`), drawn at HDRP's default 512 px a cascade, since nothing set the light's resolution. The owned profile now carries `HDShadowSettings`: 500 m, splits .05/.15/.3 (25, 75, 150 and 500 m), the last 15% fading out (`PolarArtSettings.sunShadowDistance`, `sunShadowSplits`, `sunShadowFade`), and the sun draws 2048 px a cascade (`sunShadowResolution`, the pipeline's cap). At every distance the cascade is at least as fine as before, 2.5-4 times finer from 7.5 to 150 m, and huts, the station, boulders and outcrops 150-500 m away cast shadows. Distant terrain blocks still cast none (only near chunks do, `PolarWorldStreamer`).
- **Cloud shadows.** HDRP's volumetric cloud shadows (512 px), opacity `cloudShadowOpacity` (1) x `PolarArtCelestialPresentation.ClearVisibility(storm)`: full in clear weather, none from medium snow on, where the light is already overcast. They reach the moon on clear nights, whose deck is thin.
- **Clouds.** 8 light steps (was 4, `PolarArtSettings.asset`), micro erosion, and clear-weather erosion .7 (was .4, in `PolarHdrpPresentation` and `PolarArtCelestialPresentation`, which sets it last): clear days show shaded, crisp-edged clouds with blue sky between them instead of a soft grey haze. The flat distant cloud layer was left alone: it draws at 8% opacity in clear weather and is fogged out in snow, so a sharper map there changed nothing visible (tried in tuning).
- **Day grade.** Weighted by the day look and outdoors (`look x (1 - indoor)`): contrast +12, saturation +8 (on top of the night term), split toning with cool shade (.42, .48, .58) and slightly warm light (.53, .51, .48), and a vignette (.18, smoothness .45) at all times. Nights and interiors keep their grade; the brightest snow stays neutral white. Dusk takes part of the grade and turns blue with the window warm against it.
- **Found, not changed: the sun lights little of the snow.** In the held clear-day fixture at 14:00, the snow lit by the sun alone renders at 88/255 and by everything else at 180, with all at 198: the sun supplies about 21% of the snow's light (screen-space GI off changes nothing; the sky's probe, with its sunlit cloud deck, is as bright as the snow). Shadows and cloud shadows therefore darken snow by about a fifth at most. Dimming the probe by 60% with 0.9 EV more exposure deepened the hut's shadow but washed out the sky; a different sun and sky balance is an art decision (taken in 0.6.54-test: [the sun leads clear days](#the-sun-leads-clear-days-october-24-2026-0654-test)).
- **Cost.** Weather mode against 0.6.49-test (dg-01), alternating from one save, two runs each: outdoor GPU 4.71 and 4.56 ms against 5.30 and 5.12 ms; indoors 7.13 and 7.15 ms against 6.99 and 5.92 ms, up to about 1 ms more inside the hut (the two dg-01 runs differ by 1.07 ms there). The weather mode has no clear weather, so the cloud shadows' own pass (512 px) is not in it. Gameplay peaked at 6.43 GB (dg-01 6.11 GB).
- **Checks.** 15 genuine pairs (`PolarArtCapture` phases `lg-before` and `lg-after`, 80 frames each: b5-land, sky-time, b6-habitat, b3-distance; tuning in the held `b2-hold` fixture, phase `lg-tune`). Package `lg-01`: gameplay 339 and weather 80 pass.

## Route flags wave in the wind, October 23, 2026 (0.6.48-test)

The user found the route flags too small and too solid, and asked for flags that wave in the wind.
- **Flag.** `PolarWorldStreamer.RouteFlag`: a 3.2 m pole (the built-in cylinder, 6 cm across) and a red cloth 1.1 by 0.7 m from the pole's top, a 12 by 6 grid with both faces (`PolarRouteFlag`), placed upright; no collider, as before.
- **Motion.** While the cloth is in view within 250 m of the camera, `PolarRouteFlag.Shape` lays it out each frame from the live wind (`EffectiveWindHeading`, the weather's `wind` and its gusts: strength 0.2 + 0.8 wind/0.7 + 0.3 gust, 0.28 in clear weather, 1 in a blizzard). It leaves the pole 45 degrees below the horizontal in a calm and 4 degrees in a blizzard, bends down toward its free end along an arc, and carries sideways folds running to the free end (up to 0.27 m, 1.2-3.1 a second) with a smaller ripple up and down. It runs on game time, so it stops while the game is paused; elsewhere a cloth keeps its last shape. 144 vertices a flag, and at one flag per 600 m of walk only a few are shaped at a time.
- **Checks.** Four genuine pairs (`flags` capture group: the flag nearest a new seed-777 camp from 7 m and from 40 m, in clear weather and in a snowstorm) and a 60-frame clip from upwind in the snowstorm (`flag-wave`).

## Dry hollows under the sea's datum, October 23, 2026 (0.6.46-test)

Found while checking world v19: the infinite HDRP ocean (and the opaque plane before 0.6.41) drew every inland hollow below sea level as open sea, swell and all, although it is dry land (`PolarWorldController.WaterLevel`); people walked under its surface. Every district of three sampled seeds has such ground, to -148 m; seed 777 has a hollow 17 m deep 1.3 km from the camp.
- **Fix.** HDRP's water exclusion (`supportWaterExclusion` on in `PolarPipeline.asset`): a mesh drawn with HDRP's own `Hidden/HDRP/WaterExclusion` shader marks the stencil wherever it lies in front of the opaque scene, and no water surface is drawn there. `PolarArtWater.Dry` builds one such mesh at load over the land inland of the coast's shelf (lattice rows below `Height`-70, where `WaterLevel` stops calling ground below sea level the sea), 0.5 m above the sea and so under every river and lake bed (-6 m at the lowest), with holes over coastal bays and leads, whose water lies over deep floors. Cells of 16 lattice columns by 4 rows (16 rows in the inner ring), both faces so a hollow seen from inside shows the sky; about 97,000 vertices, released with the streamer's meshes. Render only, every world version.
- **Cost.** The exclusion pass takes about 0.006 ms of GPU time (`waterperf` capture group); the water passes are unchanged.
- **Checks.** Five genuine pairs (`hollow` capture group, a new seed-777 expedition): the hollow from 60 m above its rim and from 2 m above the sea's level shows its snow; the nearest lead, coastal bay and the sea off the nearest coast keep their water. HDRP draws gap floors and lead sheets facing up; a capture camera that ended inside a hill looked through the terrain's underside and saw the sea (before) or the sky (after), so the group keeps its cameras 25 m above the ground.

## Open water ends where the land holds it, October 23, 2026 (0.6.45-test)

Found while checking world v19's new lakes: flat water sheets hanging in the air past their ends.
- **Cause.** `PolarLeads.RiverWater` pushes each open-water run's ends out along the axis by its half-width + 2 m to cover a basin's rounded end. Where the land there lies lower (past where a river turns to ice downstream, or past a lake's end over lower land) the sheet hung over it: on seed 777's world v18, 66 of 224 run ends, 15 of them lake ends, lake 4's by 22 m.
- **Fix.** A run's end is pushed out only where the land at the pushed edge (its middle and both corners) stands at or above the water (`Held`); elsewhere the sheet ends at its last wet point. Render only, every world version; world v19 also places lakes only where their ends hold ([world](polar-world.md#world-v19-a-lake-in-every-district-october-23-2026-0645-test)).
- **Checks.** Two genuine pairs (`overhang` capture group, seed 777's world v18 lake 4, from beyond its end and beside it): the sheet past the end is gone, and the carved end shows as an icy hollow past the water.

## Snow at open water's edge, October 23, 2026 (0.6.44-test)

The user's leftover: a fine zig-zag along v18 river and lake shores.
- **Cause.** The drawn snow (0.15-0.45 m) ran up to the river floor's ice colour (`PolarRivers.Ice`, also laid under open water), which took it away within 0.75 m, so along every shore the snow ended in a lip stepping down over one terrain triangle.
- **Fix.** `PolarRivers.ShoreDistance` gives the metres to the nearest open water's edge (v18 medium rivers and lakes at least `WaterDepth` deep), and the streamer's snow depth thins to that edge over 3 m (`OpenShore`), as it already did at ponds (`Shore`). Render only; frozen rivers (v17) are unchanged.
- **Not changed.** A lake's banks keep slight corners every 10 m where its width is interpolated along the centreline; bringing the water strip in from 2 m to 0.5 m past the edge was tried and made no difference, so it stays 2 m.
- **Checks.** Two genuine pairs (`v18` capture group: river and lake from above); world hash unchanged (6C0B30E32B1B4EAF).

## Snow edges at bare crests, October 23, 2026 (0.6.43-test)

The 0.6.40-test snow-edge ramp still stepped along the terrain triangles at wind-scoured crests (the user's p7 leftover).
- **Cause.** Bare crest cover (`PolarLandscape.GroundCover`) multiplies in a slope ramp (0.05-0.17) read from `PolarHills.Height`'s gradient. The ridge octave is ridged noise (`1-|v|`), whose slope flips sign at its crease: on seed 777 the slope fell from 0.317 to 0.056 and the cover from 1.000 to 0.008 within 10 cm along the crest line (`PolarWorldCheck --diagnose-cover`), so cover jumped between neighbouring vertices and the drawn snow dropped 0.15-0.45 m over one triangle, a toothed shelf along the crest.
- **Fix.** `Height` takes an optional `crease` width; within that many metres of a ridge crease the ridge's slope term fades through zero instead of flipping. Only `GroundCover` passes one (`CreaseMetres` 3), so heights, normals, navigation and world hashes are unchanged. The cover now falls from 1 to 0 over about 3.5 m at that crest, and the drawn depth ramps over about 1.2 m along an irregular painted edge.
- **Checks.** Twenty v18 seeds pass; bare land 3.8-6.5% (was 3.9-6.5%, limit 7.5%). Three genuine pairs (`snowedge` capture group, pinned to the camp fixture's crest).

## Frame timing at 240 Hz, October 23, 2026 (0.6.41-test wt-04, fixed in 0.6.42-test ck-01)

The laptop runs its panel at 240 Hz on AC and 120 Hz on battery. The loading profile's clock probe (`-polarClockProbe <seconds>`, `PolarLoadingProbe`) plays a new expedition unpaused and compares real time with Unity's unscaled and scaled time, the world clock and every frame's real length (`Logs/PolarClock/2026-10-23`).
- **Borderless fullscreen** (2560x1440, vsync on, the default the user plays with): game and world clock equal real time (90.003 s in 90.005 s); 67 fps on average, p95 22.5 ms, longest 103 ms, 48 frames over 50 ms, all during distant refinement after entry.
- **Windowed** (1600x900): unscaled time stays right (89.76 s) but `Time.deltaTime`, and with it the world clock, sums to 82.9 s: Unity rounds each frame down to whole refreshes when vsync is on and the frame is composed (a Unity bug on Windows 11, fixed in Unity 6000.5). The Player modes' background windows under-reported more (0.325 s in 0.5 s) and stall for 0.6-5 s, so test waits count game time (the weather mode's door, wt-03).
- **Fix (0.6.42-test).** In a built Player in a window with vsync, `PolarSettingsHost` sets `Time.captureDeltaTime` each frame to the real time the last frame took (at most `Time.maximumDeltaTime`), and to 0 (off) otherwise, so game time, physics and animation advance with real time while vsync still paces the frames; settings are untouched. Windowed 1600x900 at 240 Hz now keeps real time (90.004 s in 90.000 s, 120 fps on average, longest frame 50 ms); fullscreen is unchanged (90.003 s in 90.004 s). Remove it with Unity 6000.5.
- **Weather and ui Player modes (October 27, 0.6.65-test `sx-02`).** Both now pace their frames as the acceptance modes do (`QualitySettings.vSyncCount` 0, `Application.targetFrameRate` 60, set at the start of `PolarWeatherRestValidation.Run` and `PolarUiValidation.Run`), so they no longer depend on the display.
