At 240 Hz under vsync their background windows never idle the CPU, the main thread swung from 6 to 16 ms a frame with the laptop's other load, and Unity's unscaled clock, which `captureDeltaTime` does not touch, stayed at one refresh a frame for whole 6 s blocks: weather's four-grade P95 and ui's motion checks (they wait in real time) failed in about half the runs of 0.6.64-test and 0.6.65-test alike. With the pacing pinned, at 240 Hz weather passes 80 twice (every P95 16.67-16.75 ms) and ui 427 twice (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.65-test/sx-02-*`). Turning off Windows' power throttling for the Player made no difference (`exp-*`). Play itself is unchanged: in a window at 240 Hz below 240 fps the interface's motion and refresh still run on that unscaled clock.

## HDRP water, October 22, 2026 (0.6.41-test)

The user asked for water that no longer reads as a solid material (chosen: the HDRP Water System).
- **Sea.** Where HDRP draws the world, one infinite ocean `WaterSurface` at sea level (`PolarArtWater.Ocean`, created by the streamer) replaces the opaque Southern Ocean plane: a low swell (distant wind 12, chaos .6, ripples 6, foam .2), dark polar colours, absorption over 2.5 m; caustics and underwater rendering are off, since a swimmer's eye stays above the water. The Built-in path keeps the plane.
- **Leads, rivers and lakes.** One custom-geometry surface (`PolarArtWater.Inland`, pool type, ripples 4, no tessellation) draws every lead sheet and one mesh of v18 river and lake strips (`PolarLeads.RiverWater`: across every run at least `WaterDepth` deep, at the floor's level and 2 m into the banks, a run's ends pushed out along the axis) at their own heights; their MeshRenderers are switched off. Water under broken pond ice keeps the owned material.
- **Settings.** `PolarPipeline.asset` turns water on (simulation 128; exclusion and water decals off). The global default volume profile already enables Water Rendering and the camera frame settings already include Water, so no volume or frame-setting change is needed.
- **Look.** From the shore, at the user's stuck spot and over a bay the sea now moves and reflects the sky instead of reading as a flat grey sheet; rivers and lakes are dark open water between snow banks.
- **Cost (profiled October 22-23).** In the Editor, uncapped, HDRP's own water passes take 0.3-0.8 ms with water in view (simulation and update 0.13, GBuffer 0.1-0.3, lighting 0.07-0.28 ms; the `waterperf` capture group). Ocean tessellation off, a 64 simulation, 60-pixel water triangles and no foam made no measurable difference; screen-space reflections off saved about 0.6 ms at the shore but change every reflection, so nothing was trimmed. In the Player, the same world with and without HDRP water (wt-03 against a diagnostic build without it, alternating, 240 Hz) showed no measurable difference outdoors (3.5-4.8 ms either way); indoor differences followed frame pacing. The first figure, +2.3 ms outdoors in medium snow against fx-03, compared different worlds on battery at 120 Hz, where this laptop's GPU lowers its clock whenever a frame has time to spare, so it did not measure the water. Compare GPU only within one power state and frame pacing.

## Playtest fixes: rocks, floes, footprints, the snow edge, October 22, 2026 (0.6.40-test)

- **Smooth grey blobs.** `IceRock1-5` (the same polar pack ice meshes as TundraBoulder1-5, with plain HDRP/Lit `BareRock.mat`) were never converted in Stage 4. `PolarPackRockAuthor.Write` now gives them their boulder's pack rock as a Look child, and the streamer applies the scatter's owned kind-7 rock to rock islands, the 6 m rocks on closed ground and the distant silhouettes (`PolarLandscapeScatter.Rock`, now public; `PolarWorldController.Scatter`). The 6 m rocks sink 0.75 m instead of 0.3 m.
- **Floes.** Their owned silhouettes stand `Thickness` (2.5) times as high above the water and cull at 0.001 of the screen instead of 0.004. Seen low and from 240 m in a snowfall, a floe still fades like anything small.
- **Footprints.** `PolarFootPlanting` presses one print a footfall (when a sole first comes under 4 cm, re-armed above 8 cm) along the foot's IK heading; `PolarSnowTracks.Boot` takes the side, carried in the sign of the half width; the `Stamp` kernel draws a narrow heel widening to a forefoot that leans inward, mirrored per foot, with walls at 10 (was 4) and a 0.12 m margin. Before, every frame a sole was low stamped a capsule along the body's heading, which smeared both feet into one trench.
- **Snow at bare ground.** `PolarSnowDepth.Mask` thins the depth from cover .02 to none at .28 (the painted edge), outside the rock and ice factor; the twin's `PolarSnowCover` no longer multiplies by a gravel-textured bare step. The knee-high wall becomes a ramp. Where cover jumps at a break of slope (measured: 0 to .92 within half a metre at a wind-scoured crest) the ramp spans one terrain triangle.

## The face's snow on outcrops; crest shadows tried, October 22, 2026 (0.6.39-test)

The user asked for the two visual items left after 0.6.38-test: longer crest shadows and a closer match between the cliff outcrops' surface and the cliff face round them.
- **Outcrop surface (shipped).** Kind-7 rock gains the ground slope rock's streak rule (snow where the bent face looks up more than the model's own face), weighted by a new `_PolarRockFace` (0 for every other kind-7 material; clamped); `PolarArtOutcrops` sets 2 (`WallStreaks`) and moves `WallTone` from 1.3 to 1.2, chosen from five weight and tone pairs on `b3s-06`. The outcrop now carries the face's small flecks of snow (bright pixels 0.9% to 3.4%, against 0.9% and 7.9% on the face either side) at median 85 and mean 89.5 (82-91 and 86-99 on the face).
- **Crest shadows (tried, not shipped).** Crests have no shadows of their own: the twin's shadow pass draws the plain vertex lift, and the contact shadows (0.2 m ray, 10 samples) change nothing on snow at any ray length up to 1 m, or switched off, by day or at a low sun in clear weather. Tessellating the shadow pass only in the sun's cascades narrower than 80 m (a directional cascade's view-projection scales x by 2/width; the cascades reach 150 m, splits .05/.15/.3, 512 px) drew the crests into the shadow map, but the snow views changed in at most 0.8% of pixels with equal means, and the Player weather mode cost about 2 ms more outdoors (alternating with ot-02, two pairs: 6.06-6.83 against 4.55-5.60 ms; indoor equal), although an Editor A/B had shown nothing. A temporary probe showed why the gain is small: lifting all near ground 5 m in the shadow pass only, so it shades itself fully, darkens the snow by only 4-9%, as the sky, not the sun, lights snow in this game. Visible crest shadows would need a different balance of sun and sky light, which is an art decision, not a shader change.
- **Package** `cs-02`: gameplay 339 and weather 80 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.39-test/cs-02`); weather-mode GPU against ot-02, alternating, two pairs: outdoor 4.58 and 4.89 ms against 4.44 and 5.26 ms, indoor equal. `cs-01` (gameplay 339, performance 59 with P95 16.70 ms, weather 80) carried the crest shadows. **Evidence:** [outcrop-surface](../logs/evidence/2026-10-22/outcrop-surface/), 3 genuine pairs (before = the 0.6.38-test capture `ot-after`; the other b3-ground and b3-distance frames are unchanged).

## Cliff outcrops in the face's tone, October 22, 2026 (0.6.38-test)

The user asked for the 0.6.37-test outcrops, which read about 10-20% darker than the cliff face round them, to match it.
- **Cause.** On the outcrop frame (`b3s-06`) the snow share was like the face's (bright pixels 0.9% against 0.9-7.9%); the rock itself was darker (median 74 against 82-91). The pack's own maps account for about a third of that (74 to 79 with them off); the rest is kind-7 rock reading darker than the ground's slope rock.
- **Change.** Kind-7 `_Color` now tints the rock and not its snow (every kind-7 material has it white, so nothing else changes), and `PolarArtOutcrops` sets its material's to 1.3 (`WallTone`), chosen from 1.3, 1.6 and 1.9 on the same frame. The outcrop now measures median 89 (mean 91) against 82-91 (86-99) on the face either side; boulders and every other `PolarArtCapture` b3-ground and b3-distance frame are unchanged.
- **Package** `ot-02`: gameplay 339 passes (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.38-test/ot-02`); a material tone reaches no other mode. `ot-01` (also gameplay 339) was built after the Editor, closing after the captures, had saved a Hut2 flame material with its texture cleared (`Derived/Source-d3ae…-Flame.mat`); the file was restored from git and `ot-02` rebuilt. **Evidence:** [outcrop-tone](../logs/evidence/2026-10-22/outcrop-tone/), 3 genuine pairs.

## Thick snow, Stage 4: pack rocks in the world, October 22, 2026 (0.6.37-test)

Stage 4 of the [thick-snow plan](../plans/polar-thick-snow.md): PBR Rocks models for the boulders and the escarpment outcrops; render only.
- **Owned copies** (`PolarPackRockAuthor`, Polar > Thick snow > Write pack rocks; `PolarTundraArtBuilder.Build` runs it after rebuilding the boulders). Nine low-poly pack models (Rock_03, 05-09, 11-13; 550-1180 vertices) are copied, readable, to `Resources/Polar/HDRP/Environment/SourceModels/<source GUID>-<name>.fbx`, each with a `<copy>-maps` PolarSurface material carrying the model's own normal and occlusion maps: 1024 copies of the pack's 4K TIFFs (`Tools/polar_pack_rock_maps.py`; normals averaged and renormalised; eight sets, about 21 MB of PNG).
- **Shader.** Kind-7 rock reads a model's own maps on UV0 when `_PolarRockLichen.w` is on (it was unused): the normal map bends the face before the triplanar `Cliffs_01` rock, the crack snow and the ledge snow follow it, and the occlusion darkens the albedo to .72 in cracks and the ambient term. Other kind-7 rock is unchanged. `PolarArtRock.WithMaps` copies a maps material's textures onto a caller's own rock material; the scatter keeps one copy per normal map, the escarpments one per form. The tessellated ground twin was regenerated (`PolarGroundTwinAuthor`).
- **Boulders.** Each of the six boulder prefabs keeps its root, scale and collision mesh (`FootprintFactor`, explorer clearance and the boulder pass are unchanged) and switches its original renderers off. A "Look" child carries the pack rock whose width and height to length are nearest the original's (Rock_13, 03, 12, 09, 08 and, for the flat block, Rock_11: the slab Rock_10 read as a drum once its foot was buried), turned so its long side lies along the original's and scaled on each axis to the original's box (the widest stretch is Rock_08's height, 31%), so what is seen and what is touched share one box. The boulders read as craggy, faceted rocks with snow on their ledges instead of smooth rounded stones.
- **Escarpments.** Four tall pack rocks (Rock_03, 05, 06, 07) replace the CoastCliff shells in `PolarArtOutcrops.Sources`. Every earlier source failed the closed-mesh audit (open edges above the bottom 5%: Rock3 49, Rock5 359, the shells 160-196), so the outcrops had been inert; the pack rocks are closed and kept, so modules are built again (99 candidates, 11 accepted round the capture's test face, 2 drawn from 25 m) and `Blocks` keeps the player out of them, as designed in B3. A drawn module reads as a darker outcrop pressed into the cliff (about 10-20% darker than the face round it, with less crack snow on its sheer sides).
- **Captures.** `PolarArtCapture` b3-distance adds b3s-06 (the nearest module from 25 m on its lower side; skipped when there is none, as before this change); `PolarFiveAreasCapture` adds a capture-only `rocks` group (the six boulders at 2.2 m in a row 7 m ahead, sunk as the scatter sinks them, near, from 18 m and at a low sun).
- **Cost.** Package `rk-01`: gameplay 339, physical 831, performance 59 (P95 16.68 ms over 14580 frames at the 60 Hz cap) and weather 80 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.37-test/rk-01`). Weather mode against st-03, alternating, two runs each: outdoor GPU means 4.68 and 4.51 ms (st-03 4.69 and 4.43 ms), indoor equal (`cmpw-*`); the day's absolute numbers sit about 1 ms above yesterday's runs of the same st-03 build, so only the alternating pairs compare. Loading against st-03, alternating, one run each (st-03 in brackets): playable 50.7 s new game (52.2 s) and 17.6 s Continue (17.2 s); distant refinement complete 144.0 s (149.1 s) and 117.1 s (111.0 s); P95 while refining 8.34-8.35 ms (`Logs/PolarLoadingProfile/2026-10-22/cmp-st03-c`, `cmp-rk01-a`).
- **Evidence:** [thick-snow-s4](../logs/evidence/2026-10-22/thick-snow-s4/): 14 genuine pairs (`PolarArtCapture` b3-ground and b3-distance, `PolarFiveAreasCapture` terrain and terrain2, and the rocks lineup, whose before was taken with the committed boulder prefabs restored after the shader edit, which leaves rock without pack maps unchanged).

## Thick snow, Stage 3: deep snow, relief and footprints near the viewer, October 22, 2026 (0.6.36-test)

Stage 3 of the [thick-snow plan](../plans/polar-thick-snow.md), from a design chosen out of three by judges (spec in the session's scratchpad); render only, as Stage 2.
- **Depth** (`PolarSnowDepth`). Open snow is 0.15-0.45 m (about 0.3 m): `Mask` (Stage 2's terms, now including the distant cliff band in negative R) times an exposure from lee slopes on the world's sastrugi axis, swells about 40 m along it and 11 m across, wind-packed snow and rocky tiles. It thins over 3 m to pond outlines (`PolarWorldStreamer.Shore`), keeps the hut, hub and tent clears and is 0 on gap outlines. Every terrain vertex carries it, near and distant: shared vertices (the window cut, distant block edges) use position-only inputs (`EdgeNormal` on block edges); a distant block's other vertices use its own colour and grid normal. The distant morph adds the coarse mesh's depth to its start position, so a refinement no longer jumps. Bounds grow by 0.8 m.
- **Tessellated twin** (`PolarGround.shader`). Generated from `PolarSurface.shader` by `PolarGroundTwinAuthor` (Polar > Thick snow > Write PolarGround twin; the HDRP build refuses a stale twin): HDRP 17.2's hull and domain in the GBuffer, DepthOnly and Forward passes; the shadow pass is not tessellated (tessellating every cascade cost about 1 ms more than the budget; contact shadows still catch the crests). Near chunks whose bounds come within the relief reach plus 3 m take the twin (back beyond plus 8 m; `UpdateRelief`); the reach is 18 m, 12 m in a blizzard, never past the near window's edge less 2 m, rising at most 4 m/s (`PolarArtSettings.snowReliefFar`, `snowReliefFarStorm`; 20 px segments). The per-vertex factor and the displacement weight are 0 from the reach on, so an edge a plain chunk shares is drawn whole and undisplaced.
- **Domain** (`PolarMeshModification.hlsl`). The surface is the open depth (the vertices' depth before the tents' clearing, which the domain applies exactly, so tent pits have smooth 2 m banks) under the painted rock and bare edges (`PolarSnowCover`, trilinear reads at the segment's level), with sastrugi crests of 12-29 cm from the pixels' own ridge maths and grain within 8 m; then footprints within 6-10 m. `precise` keeps the depth prepass and the GBuffer in agreement. The displacement reaches the pixel stage (texCoord2.y) so the rock map is read at the height the domain used.
- **T-junctions.** Gap and pond cuts split near-chunk triangles whose neighbours stay whole, which a nonlinear displacement opens into cracks. Gap cutting now leaves whole any mesh piece a gap's convex piece does not touch (`Apart`, completing the separating-axis test KeepsWhole only half did), and the domain keeps the plain linear lift, with no prints, within 1.6 radial cells of gap outlines and over pond cut boxes (`PolarSnowTracks` puts them first in its flatten list). The hole probe (`tracks` group: the camera clears to magenta with fog and clouds off) finds 0 holes at the camp and near the inner ring.
- **Footprints** (`PolarSnowTracks`, `PolarSnowTracks.compute`). A toroidal 1024² R32 field of 4 cm texels round the camera holds the highest the drawn snow may stand; boots within 18 m on streamed terrain stamp their soles (`PolarFootPlanting`), falling snow raises it again (the deepest print in 420 s up to medium snow, 60 s in a blizzard, game time), refilled at most 10 times a second; never saved. Prints are geometry within 6-10 m and shading to 20 m. Setting Footprints (Picture tab): Off, Player, Everyone (default). The relief also flattens round items put down (wider for rifles and harpoons), people lying (who rest on the snow, `PolarCharacterPresentation`) and narrow cracks, 64 entries nearest first.
- **Pixels.** Within the reach the crest slope is the geometry's own (off where the snow is cleared); drift swells tilt the snow; banks of the drawn depth (hut discs, tent pits, pond banks) are lit as slopes; prints are a little greyer, glossier and without glints.
- **Effect.** Open snow reads about 0.3 m deep: people stand in it to the shins, prints trail behind boots near the viewer and fill in a blizzard within a minute, sastrugi crests stand up with lee shading, the camp tent sits in a smooth pit, items and the dead lie on the snow, pond edges are low banks. Beyond the reach the depth stays as drawn, without relief. The look-down view at the player's feet was black once in the first Editor run, while the new tessellation variants compiled; the same pose renders normally afterwards.
- **Cost.** GPU, Editor, relief and footprints on minus off within one run (`tracks-perf`, two runs; median): +0.73-0.95 ms along the trail, +0.27-0.57 ms looking across the wind, medium snow and blizzard (budget 0.8; the spec's full reach, 12 px segments and a tessellated shadow pass had cost +1.5 to +3.8 ms, so fallback F1 and a plain shadow pass were taken). Uncapped `b3-distance-perf`: mean P95 9.23 to 9.47 ms over 18 rows (+0.24). Package `st-03` (st-01 with the window edge measured without height samples; st-01's physical mode had crashed on a sample past the world's last row): gameplay 339, performance 59 (P95 16.67-16.71 ms at the 60 Hz cap), weather 80, weather-resume 10 and ui 382 pass on st-01, physical 831 on st-03 (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.36-test`). Against cb-01, alternating, two runs each: weather-mode outdoor GPU means +0.02 to +0.58 ms by grade (3.32-3.99 ms against 3.12-3.55 ms); loading playable 50.2-50.5 s new game (49.2-50.6 s) and 15.9-16.2 s Continue (15.6-15.8 s), distant refinement complete 142.1-142.5 s and 108.4-108.8 s (135.9-137.4 s and 102.8-102.9 s, about 5% longer: the depth on every distant vertex).
- **Evidence:** [thick-snow-s3](../logs/evidence/2026-10-21/thick-snow-s3/), 42 genuine pairs (`PolarFiveAreasCapture` terrain, terrain2, motion and the new capture-only `tracks` group; `PolarArtCapture` b3-ground, b4-pond-five, b5-land, b6-habitat and people-contacts).

## Distant cliff band edges: October 21, 2026 (0.6.35-test)

After 0.6.33-test the sides of the distant cliff band showed feathered dark teeth 200-600 m away. The band is rock painted from vertex colour over sheer faces on 128 and 256 blocks. On the cliff 230-400 m from the camp it showed rows of horizontal spikes and two long fingers; on the relief streak its sides were toothed. Diagnosed with temporary shader views of the camp near view and the streak (band value, slope coverage, combined coverage, rock with and without the map height, contours of the band value and of n.y):
- **Not the normals or the slope term.** Slope coverage from the interpolated normals added only small patches inside the band.
- **Sawtooth: the smoothstep fade.** Sampled once per vertex and interpolated over 6-12 m triangles, the fade bent at every triangle edge. Its contours zig-zagged with one tooth per row of the mesh.
- **Fingers: the rocky-tile term.** The band was vertex R .86-1, which the rocky-tile coverage also reads as fully rocky. Its noise patches stood out round the band's faded outer vertices, up to 8 m beyond the visible edge, and dotted rock into bare ground beside weak bands.
- **Feathering: the edge spread over the whole fade.** The band's coverage rose over all three spacings of its fade. The rock edge (sharpness 2 at a distance, plus the map height x 1.6) let the rock map's facets, seen at a slant, poke out across all of it as thin horizontal spikes.

Fix: three lines and their comments, in `PolarWorldStreamer.CliffBand` and `SurfaceColor` and in `PolarGroundSurface` (`Resources/Polar/HDRP/PolarData.hlsl`).
- **Straight fade.** CliffBand fades its strength linearly over the same three spacings. The triangles interpolate a straight fade exactly, so the band value's contours run parallel to the cliff.
- **Negative R.** SurfaceColor writes the strength (.05-1) as vertex R 0 to -1. Between a band vertex and its neighbour (.04) the value never passes the rocky-tile range. v15 knoll cores keep .86-.98 and their own mapping.
- **Steeper rise.** The shader's band coverage rises over a quarter of the strength range, centred at .35: `(wander*.15-R-.35)*4+.5`. The edge is crisp, and the map height only makes it ragged. The slow noise moves the edge about ±1.4 m at a 6 m spacing; it moved it ±3.9 m when it also had to hide the mesh's rows. The rock edge rule itself is unchanged, so slope rock, rocky tiles, knolls and far rock patches look as before.
- **Weak bands.** These are where a cliff gives way to a ramp, at sheerness under about .5. They are a trade-off of one value per vertex, because a weak band's core and a strong band's side carry the same value. Six variants were captured on every camp and relief view:
  - Centred at .43, a steeper rise broke the camp range view's ridge band into fragments.
  - At .35 the ridge band stays continuous and the strong bands are about 1.5 m wider each side at a 6 m spacing.
  - The faintest lines (sheerness .2-.35, by a bare patch near the camp) lose their scattered streaks and rocks, which came from the fingers' term and the old fringe.
- **Tried and dropped.** A constant edge sharpness with half the map height beyond 40 m also crisped the band. It removed faint slope rock near the camp too and made rock patches 2-6 km away blocky.
- **Cost.** The CPU work per distant vertex is the same (one smoothstep fewer) and the shader adds a max. Package `cb-01` was measured against sd-01, alternating (cb, sd, sd, cb) with a seed 777 new game and the fixed v17 Continue (sd-01 in brackets):
  - refinement complete: 147.0-148.6 s new game (145.8-147.1 s) and 110.2-112.4 s Continue (111.3-113.5 s);
  - playable: 53.4-54.4 s (53.4-54.3 s) and 17.4-18.3 s (17.9-18.0 s);
  - P95 while refining 8.34-8.35 ms in all eight runs.

  A first round in the opposite order is not counted: it ran while another session compiled, and world generation, which this change does not touch, was 4% slower in its middle runs.
- **Evidence:** [cliff-band-edges](../logs/evidence/2026-10-21/cliff-band-edges/), 12 genuine pairs from `PolarFiveAreasCapture` terrain and terrain2. Before: the thick-snow session's captures of 2dc657b3. After: the same views with the fix. The diagnostic views are kept under `Logs/ProjectUpdateCapture/five-areas/{diag-band,diag-cand1,cand2,rise,var}`.

## Thick snow, Stage 2: a drawn snow layer, October 21, 2026 (0.6.34-test)

Stage 2 of the [thick-snow plan](../plans/polar-thick-snow.md): near ground is drawn up to 0.10 m above its collider, so boots, tents, crates, boulders and huts stand in the snow instead of on a painted sheet. Render only: colliders, navigation, world hashes and saves are unchanged.
- **Depth** (`PolarSnowDepth.At`). A function of world position only: the snow `PolarGroundSurface` paints (cover with its noise, the slope term, rocky tiles, bare knoll crests and river ice, at the shader's q), full depth only well inside painted snow (smoothstep .55-.95 of the snow weight) and 80-120% by a 50 m exposure noise. It is 0 on rock, bare ground and river ice, in a 9.5 m disc round every hut (2 m fade; the door approach lies inside), in the hub sectors (faded in over 16 lattice units, as the plaza's microrelief), on pond outlines and, until 0.6.74-test, on gap outlines (the wall tops; since then the snow keeps its depth there and ends in a face).
- **Bake** (`PolarWorldStreamer`). The streamer stores the depth in each terrain vertex's tangent w as -1 - depth; HDRP reads only its sign, so the vertex layout and memory are unchanged. Near chunks carry it on every vertex. Distant blocks carry it only on the near window's cut, with the colour and normal the near chunks have there, so the two meshes meet without a step; elsewhere a distant vertex has 0 (0.1 m is under a pixel beyond the window). Pond cuts interpolate the depth and set 0 where a point snaps to the ice; gap pieces take their triangle's interpolated depth (Subtract also splits along the lines of a gap's convex triangles far from the gap) and 0 only on the outline. `OnLocalCutBoundary` now also accepts the window's column at x = Width, as `LocalCutEdge` stitches it (a distant block there had taken its own normal). Bounds grow by 0.12 m.
- **Shader** (`PolarMeshModification.hlsl`, every pass). Kind-0 ground with ground maps rises by the baked depth (`ATTRIBUTES_NEED_TANGENT` in all passes of `PolarSurface`); pitched and pitching tents clear it under their floor plus 0.4 m with a 1 m fade, through a global array of up to 24 entries that `PolarSnowShelter` fills every frame (`_PolarSnowClear`). The habitat shaders, which share the include, are untouched (`POLAR_GROUND_SNOW`).
- **What rests on it.** Items put down on open ground are drawn on the snow (`PolarMvpController.Seat` adds `PolarWorldStreamer.SnowAt`, which interpolates the depth over the near mesh's 1/16 lattice triangle with the hut, hub and tent clears and the pond shore); saved positions stay on the ground. Grounded items and narrow cracks are seated again when the tent clears change (`PolarSnowShelter.ClearRevision`). Narrow crack overlays lie in the snow; drift particles run 0.10 m above the ground. Feet stay on the collider, so people stand 0.10 m deep.
- **Not lifted** (accepted): scatter, outcrop and escarpment proxies, hut dressing, bodies (a lying body sinks up to 0.10 m, which reads as lying in snow) and harpoons stuck tip first (they keep their saved pose, so the snow covers 0.10 m more of the shaft; a harpoon that glanced off lies on the snow); items close to a gap's lip. A near chunk released when the window moves becomes distant ground with no depth, a 0.1 m drop over a whole chunk: beyond 100 m in most of the map, but 20-55 m away around the inner ring (district rows below about z 720), where a lattice unit across the ring is only 1.3-2.3 m.
- **Effect.** Boots sink into the snow up to the ankle welt; tent stakes, crates, boulders and the cliff foot are banked by it; lying people rest partly in it; put-down items and harpoons lie on it. Seams: none on the window cut, the water lips or the gap walls in the level-of-detail walk (600 frames) and the camp, relief, pond, hut and contact views. From 30 m on the change is under a pixel, as planned; the thick look of open snow needs Stage 3's depth and relief.
- **Cost.** One vertex-shader branch on the ground (tent loop only where the depth is above 0) and one depth per near vertex while meshes are built. Uncapped Editor P95 over the 18 `b3-distance-perf` rows was 12.78 ms before and 11.33 ms after in the same Editor session (a first before run, with the machine busier, gave 17.00): the change is inside the run-to-run noise. Package `sd-01` against cn-01, alternating on the same machine: weather-mode outdoor GPU means 4.23-4.83 ms (4.30-5.06 ms); loading playable 51.4-53.4 s new game (52.5-53.3 s) and 16.2-17.7 s Continue (17.2-17.8 s), refinement complete 142.3-146.0 s (145.2-151.7 s) and 108.3-110.4 s (112.4-113.0 s). Player modes gameplay 339, physical 831, performance 59 (P95 16.67-16.71 ms at the 60 Hz cap), weather 80, weather-resume 10 and ui 382 pass (`Logs/PolarV7/0.6.34-test/sd-01`).
- **Evidence:** [thick-snow-s2](../logs/evidence/2026-10-21/thick-snow-s2/), 29 genuine pairs (`PolarFiveAreasCapture` terrain, terrain2, world, harpoon and the motion2 level-of-detail walk on the camp save; `PolarArtCapture` b3-ground, b4-pond-five, b5-land, b6-habitat and people-contacts on a new seed-777 expedition).

## Distant normals from the grid: October 21, 2026 (0.6.33-test)

After 0.6.31-test faint ribs remained on the upper part of the relief streak (block (11,7), 300-590 m from the vantage). Distant normals are now computed from each block's own grid with a smoother kernel, which also cuts their height samples.
- **Grid normals** (`PolarWorldStreamer.Surround`, `GridNormal`). A distant block samples its grid's positions and two rings around it, about one height sample per vertex instead of five. Each grid vertex's normal crosses central differences of the grid smoothed 1-2-1 both ways: each difference weighs the vertices 1-2-0-2-1 along it and 1-2-1 across it. A face narrower than the spacing (the streak: about 8 m high and 2.5 m across, under cells of 6.7 by 6.1 m) then lights the same wherever it falls between vertices. Kernels compared in a top-down shading of block (11,7) under the scene's sun (`Logs/CliffSobel/eval-*.png`): point and box differences draw a chain of beads along the face, the 3x3 Sobel kernel (one ring) a fainter chain, a 1-4-0-4-1 difference faint lumps, the 1-2-0-2-1 difference none.
- **Off the grid** (`MeshNormal`, `OffGrid`). Stitches along block edges, fan centres and points on gap outlines take the normal the grid's triangles interpolate there (each cell split along its diagonal, as the mesh is built), with no height samples. The morph rebuilds the coarse mesh's normals the same way from its retained grid (`AddMorph`, `MorphBoundarySurface`), so a refinement starts from the normals on screen.
- **Edges.** Blocks at the same spacing compute identical normals at shared vertices (0 degrees over 513 vertices on each edge measured, including across the angular seam between blocks 83 and 0). Blocks at different spacings, such as a 256 beside a 128, still differ there, as in 0.6.31, since each uses its own spacing (mean 0.15-0.78, at most 11.6 degrees on a sheer face). On the near window's edge every distant vertex takes the near chunks' 0.15-unit normal (`OnLocalCutBoundary`), so the two meshes meet without a lighting seam; 0.6.31 used the block's spacing there.
- **Effect.** Lighting the ground's own normals alone (grey albedo and the interpolated vertex normal, through a temporary shader switch for the capture), the ribs on the streak are gone and the scallops on the cliff 230-400 m from the camp much reduced. A sheer face narrower than a vertex spacing now shades softly over about two spacings; the rock band's colour still marks it. In the normal view the change is small: Stage 1's rock map (snow in its cracks) draws most of the band's texture, and its diagonal strokes are the same with every kernel.
- **Cost.** Height samples per distant grid vertex 5 → about 1.04 (two rings), none for stitches and fans. Distant refinement after entry in the Editor, same save and views: relief group settled 200.1 → 106.6 s, camp group 172.8 → 90.4 s. Built package `cn-01` against sn-01, alternating on the same 120 Hz display (sn-01, cn-01, cn-01, sn-01; seed 777 new game and the fixed v17 Continue): refinement complete 140.0-143.2 s new game (225.1-245.7 s) and 109.4-109.7 s Continue (197.1-217.1 s); playable 51.6-53.0 s (53.9-57.5 s) and 16.9-17.7 s (20.0-20.8 s); P95 while refining 8.34-8.35 ms in all eight runs; worst frame 329-471 ms new game and 392-438 ms Continue (575 ms and one 17.6 s stall, and 333-367 ms; 0.6.31's comparison saw 333-650 ms).
- **Removed.** `PolarWorldController.Normal(x, z, ex, ez)` (only the 0.15-unit form remains) and `MeshScratch.normalX/normalZ`.
- **Remaining.** The sides of the cliff band on distant cliffs 200-400 m away still show feathered teeth in the normal view; they come from the band's vertex colour and the rock map, not the normals.
- **Evidence:** [cliff-sobel-normals](../logs/evidence/2026-10-21/cliff-sobel-normals/) (the record's id predates the change from the Sobel kernel), 13 genuine pairs from `PolarFiveAreasCapture` terrain and terrain2: eight normal views and five views of the normals lit alone. The Sobel attempt's captures are kept under `Logs/ProjectUpdateCapture/five-areas/sobel-after`.

## Thick snow at every distance, Stage 1: October 21, 2026 (0.6.32-test)

The user: the ground snow reads "thin, like a paper on the ground", and slope rock shows an unnatural repeating frieze; they supplied PBR Rocks - Nature Pack 3.0 (ANGRY MESH) ([plan](../plans/polar-thick-snow.md)). All ground snow is pixel shading on the terrain mesh, so rock and bare patches were colour changes at the same height. The evidence frames are dominated by views from 30 m to 2 km, where a raised layer would be below a pixel, so Stage 1 works in shading and the rock map; raised snow and footprints come in later stages.
- **Rock map.** Owned 2K copies of `Cliffs_01` (cool grey fractured rock) replace `T_beach_cliff_01` (tan sand with large rectangular strata) on `Terrain0-7.mat` (`_Scale` .14 to .18, a 5.6 m repeat; the October 20 fade to a repeat 2.8 times larger from 40 m stays). `PolarArtRock` passes the maps to every kind-7 rock (boulders, stones, cliff proxies, escarpments). The albedo carries the map's height in alpha; `PolarGroundLook` rock luma .248 to .186 (full-resolution linear mean of the owned copy). Files: `Resources/Polar/HDRP/Environment/Textures/8f76c4d65bd8840438e3b824dc423d28-BC.tga` and `3a47944bfc8fadc4f8f156ad1fbcc298-N.tga`; the vendor parts used are under `Assets/ANGRY MESH/` (untracked, like the other vendor folders).
- **Edges** (`PolarGroundSurface`, `Resources/Polar/HDRP/PolarData.hlsl`). The rock/snow edge follows the map's height instead of its brightness (`PolarRockEdgeHeight` 1.6), so snow buries the low parts first and the raised facets stand out of it; snow fills cracks where a face looks up more than .55 (`PolarRockCrevice`; at .4 it drew white diagonal streaks on steep faces). The bare/snow edge follows the owned gravel height (`_PolarGround2H`, the ScouredGravel sample's `_HeightMap`). A texture-free copy of the snow mask (cover and slope terms only) gives each pixel its distance from the edge in pixels: rock and bare ground darken in a band under the edge (at least 1.5 px, .35 m near; AO .60) and the snow rounds off towards it (.18). The band sits at mask level .3, not .5: at .5 it drew a thin line across smooth snow where slope rock barely starts.
- **Less satin.** Snow smoothness .30/.36 to .27/.33, ripple and sastrugi lee slopes .06/.18 to .08/.24, grain normal .30 to .34, sastrugi hollows .18 to .26 and lee tint stronger (`PolarGroundLook` and the shader defaults together).
- **Rock caps** (`PolarRockSurface`). Snow in cracks as on the ground, a darker band under each cap's edge, and faces of kind-7 rock that look up swell by up to 4 cm (less on small stones) in `PolarMeshModification.hlsl` (render only). Cap coverage is unchanged: the boulder was already mostly white under its cap.
- **Shadows** (`PolarHdrpPresentation`). Micro shadows (the ground's occlusion shades sunlight) and contact shadows (length .2 m, to 60 m) with the sun's contact-shadow flag on.
- **Effect.** Rock bands and steep faces read as fractured rock with snow in its cracks instead of a printed frieze; edges of rock, gravel and boulders are irregular. Open snow is nearly unchanged (blizzard and looking-down views below the comparison threshold): a thick layer there needs geometry (Stages 2-3). Remaining: steep faces 150-250 m away show a fine diagonal hatch from the texture's angular facets, much milder than the frieze; frozen ponds keep their flat edge (Stage 2's raised layer gives them a bank).
- **Cost.** Uncapped Editor P95 over the 18 `b3-distance-perf` rows: mean 9.86 to 10.13 ms (+0.27, median +0.23; worst row +0.91 ms, dist-06 home view). The ground shader adds a few ALU per pixel and one texture read near bare edges; contact shadows are most of the cost.
- **Evidence:** [thick-snow-s1](../logs/evidence/2026-10-21/thick-snow-s1/), 19 genuine pairs (`PolarFiveAreasCapture` terrain and terrain2 on the camp save; `PolarArtCapture` b3-ground and b5-land on a new seed-777 expedition). Tuning passes are kept under `Logs/ProjectUpdateCapture/{five-areas,polar-art}/snow-s1-tune*`.

## Distant cliff stripes: October 21, 2026 (0.6.31-test)

After 0.6.30-test the user asked to confirm the faint stripes inside distant cliff rock bands and to fix them unless they had improved; they were unchanged from er-01. Diagnosed from the relief vantage (a streak 300-590 m away on block (11,7), resolution 256) with temporary shader views:
- **Not the cause:** the rock texture (same stripes at a 2.8 times larger repeat), shadow maps (sun shadows off), screen-space ambient occlusion, global illumination and reflections (each off), smoothness and occlusion from coverage (fixed), and the terrain itself (true heights every 0.05 lattice unit both ways are smooth apart from one step 4-5 m wide).
- **Cause:** the distant mesh's vertex normals. `PolarWorldController.Normal` differenced the height 0.15 lattice units either side of each vertex, so on a face narrower than the mesh spacing each vertex saw the face or missed it depending on where the face fell between rows, and the lit band alternated into diagonal ribs.
- **Normals** (`PolarWorldStreamer.MeshNormal`, `Normal(x, z, ex, ez)`): distant vertices difference a whole vertex spacing either side on each axis (at least 0.15), and so do the morph's coarse grid and border fans; near chunks keep 0.15. The same four height samples per vertex, so no added cost. Half a spacing was tried first and left the ribs.
- **Square cells** (`Grid`): a distant block trades columns for rows (columns at least 16, rows at most 512) until its cells are about square, keeping its vertex count; its resolution, and so which blocks refine and when, is unchanged. The cliff band applies to the same blocks as before (by resolution) and measures each axis with its own spacing. Sheer faces are sampled about twice as finely along the radius, so their rock bands are narrower and crisper.
- **Remaining:** fainter ribs on the upper part of the streak. Removing them fully needs a smoother kernel than a box difference; computed from the mesh's own grid heights it would also need fewer height samples.
- **Evidence:** [cliff-band-normals](../logs/evidence/2026-10-21/cliff-band-normals/); the diagnostic views are kept under `Logs/ProjectUpdateCapture/five-areas/{squarecells,fixnormals*,widenormals}/diagnose`.

## Rock repeat at distance: October 20, 2026 (0.6.30-test)

The user asked for terrain detail without a longer wait to play ([plan](../plans/polar-five-areas-0630.md)). Jagged, hatched cliffs were diagnosed one factor at a time from fixed Editor views of the seed-777 camp save (a 79-degree slope 230 m from camp, a 366 m peak 1.6 km from a vantage, a far silhouette), at entry, 30 s later and settled:
- **Image aliasing:** TAA off and TAA high left the hatch unchanged.
- **Geometry and LOD:** every distant block at its highest resolution ("uncapped": 0/144/488/40 blocks at 32/64/128/256 instead of 336/212/112/12) left it unchanged.
- **Sampling:** distant blocks with fewer columns around the ring so their cells were square (`PolarWorldStreamer`, same radial levels) left the band pattern as it was, made a cliff band running along the radius blurrier and wider, and lengthened background refinement by 13% (195.5 → 220.4 s, 300 → 662 blocks queued after entry). Reverted.
- **Texture (the cause):** the slope-rock map (`T_beach_cliff_01_BC`, `_Scale` 0.14) repeats every 7.1 m and its large strata blocks are distinct. Along a long cliff seen from afar the repeat drew a frieze of identical marks; on a steep slope seen edge-on the three triplanar projections overlaid it into a see-through lattice. A 20 m repeat (`_Scale` 0.05) removed both, but up close the strata became large zig-zag steps.
- **Fix** (`Resources/Polar/HDRP/PolarData.hlsl`, `PolarGroundSurface`): from 40 m the rock map gives way to a repeat 2.8 times larger (`k*.36`), alone beyond 120 m; both are read only between (`PolarRockMap`, `PolarRockPert`, dynamic branches). Near rock, rock objects (kind 7), heights, meshes, collision, loading and saves are unchanged.
- **Remaining:** inside the distant cliff band (vertex red above .86) a sheer face still shows the mesh's rows as stripes, and the band's edge fades over three vertex spacings by design (October 16, 2026).
- **Evidence:** [terrain-detail](../logs/evidence/2026-10-20/terrain-detail/); the per-axis and 20 m-repeat attempts are kept under `Logs/ProjectUpdateCapture/five-areas/after-r1` and `after-r2-scale005`.

## River ice: October 17-18, 2026 (0.6.24-test)

World v17's rivers ([polar world](polar-world.md#world-v17-frozen-rivers-october-17-18-2026-0624-test)) are cut into the terrain, so near meshes (1/16 lattice unit, about 1.5 m along the radius) carry their floors, banks and ends exactly; no separate river meshes.
- **Vertex code.** `PolarWorldStreamer.SurfaceColor` sets vertex blue to .2 - .08 x the river-ice weight (`PolarRivers.Ice`): on a floor only where the land was cut down to it; on coarse meshes a medium river's band widens to 0.75 vertex spacings, while a tributary narrower than that fades. Recipe tiles give .2-.8, gaps under .05 and morphing skips under .1, so the code is free.
- **Shader.** `PolarRiverIce` (`Resources/Polar/HDRP/PolarData.hlsl`, kind-0 ground path): grey-blue glaze with frosted patches, faint crack lines (cells about 7 m) and soft drifted-snow patches, smoothness up to .72, on ground facing up (steep banks keep the slope rock). A first version's anisotropic streak noise drew diagonal spikes and was replaced.
- **Snow.** The drift-particle mask (`PolarSnowLayers`) treats river ice like intact pond ice. Scatter, local props and escarpment rock keep off the rivers.
- **Windows** are ordinary ponds (plates, cracks, water) on the river floor.
- **Evidence** (`docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-10-17/rivers-v17`): six genuine v16/v17 pairs from fixed cameras over the seed-777 home district (no river within 3.5 km of the birth hut, so they show the walks and huts unchanged) and an after-only sheet of a river floor at eye level, a window from above and a river from 600 m.

## Loading in 2 minutes, Continue in 30 seconds: October 17, 2026 (0.6.19-test)

Plan: [tent help, oil and loading](../plans/polar-loading-0619.md). The user waited about 7 min for a new expedition and over 5 min for Continue, and asked for 2 min and 30 s.

Measured on this laptop (the user's: i7-13700H, RTX 4070 Laptop) at the desktop's 2560x1440, with the profiler now keeping the player's display (`Logs/PolarLoadingProfile/2026-10-17`):

| Load | tn-02 (0.6.18, old 1600x900 profile) | 0.6.19-test (ld-07) |
|---|---:|---:|
| New expedition, seed 777 | 139 s | **61.5 s** (pass 1 18.5, pass 2 24.3, distant terrain 10.5, near window 1.8, cache 5.1) |
| Continue of the user's save | 253 s | **25.8 s** (world 7.4, distant terrain 11.3, near window 4.6) |
| First Continue of a save written before 0.6.19 | | 49.3 s, once (full check 26.1, cache rewrite 4.2) |

**No camera renders while loading.**
- `PolarHdrpPresentation.LateUpdate` turns the player's camera off while `world.IsGenerating` and on again when generation ends, and keeps the menu camera off meanwhile.
- The loading page is a screen overlay whose opaque fill covers the screen. Before this, both HDRP cameras drew the half-built world behind it at full resolution with no frame cap.
- `PolarMvpController.Loading` caps the frame rate at 60 while loading and restores the player's limit afterwards.

**Distant detail after entry.** `LoadingResolution` builds blocks within 6 km at 128 (lower where a block is capped) and all others at 32. `MissingInitialDetail` uses the same rule.
- Before, the 6 and 16 km rings were built at 256 and 128 before play: 143 blocks on v16 seed 777, 80 s of the 139.
- In play, the existing background refinement (one block at a time, nearest first, 0.35 s morph) brings every block to its usual detail. That took 196 s (Continue) to 211 s (new game) after entry.
- The rings' existing hysteresis keeps the outer tenth of each ring one step coarser until the player comes nearer.

**Gap clipping** (`CutFracturedTriangle`). The user's save stands inside a gap's bounds. There, every triangle was clipped against every convex piece of the gap with list-allocating `PolarThinIce.Subtract` calls, then drawn as a fan of new samples even when nothing cut it. Near chunks took 41 s each instead of 0.6 s, and distant blocks up to 121 s.
- `PolarThinIce.KeepsWhole` replicates `Subtract`'s edge order and tolerances to find, without allocating, the pieces it would return whole. `Subtract` is called only where it would cut.
- A triangle that no gap cuts and that has no edge on a gap outline is drawn as anywhere else (`CutTriangle`).
- `DenseGapBank` reads only the outline segments that can reach the chunk (`MeshBuffer.bank`, filtered with the tolerances it uses).
- Result for the user's save: near window 61 s to 4.6 s, distant terrain 155 s to 11.3 s.

**Frames in play.**
- In a 1600x900 window the longest frame over 200 s of refinement and the minute after was the first one (0.7 s), with p95 8.3 ms.
- Full-screen runs at 2560x1440 while the user used the machine had frames of 0.5-10 s. The game's scripts and physics took a few milliseconds of them; Unity presents a frame at the start of the next one, so the time was spent waiting to present.
- The last full-screen run showed 0.54 s at most over 75 s, before its window was closed. An undisturbed full-screen run is still to be done.

**Profiler** (`PolarLoadingProbe`).
- It keeps the player's display and frame limit.
- `-polarWaitDistant` waits up to 600 s, logs every frame over 0.5 s split into scripts, the time after them and physics, and watches one more minute after refinement settles.
- `continue.upgrade_cache`, `continue.validate` and `compact.prepare` are new rows.
- Player.log gets one line per load ("Polar loading: Continue playable after 25.8 s").

## Playtest fixes: October 16, 2026 (0.6.17-test)

Plan: [playtest fixes for 0.6.17](../plans/polar-playtest-0617.md). No visual capture was taken: the user chose lean checks and playtests.
- **Rock-band teeth.** Cause: the distant cliff band (`CliffBand`) fell from full to nothing within half a vertex spacing, so each vertex of a 5-30 m distant mesh was rock or snow and the triangles between drew the edge as a row of teeth. The band now fades over three vertex spacings centred on its old edge (the visible width is unchanged; the cheap reject moves from 60 to 80 m). `PolarData.hlsl` moves the band's edge by a slow noise (about 17 m) and softens the rock edge where a pixel covers more ground (sharpness 5 near, 2 far). Distant normals are unchanged: normals sampled at each block's own spacing would differ at the borders of blocks of different resolution and show seams.
- **v16 distant resolution.** `SpacingMaximum`'s 5 m limit scales with v16's map (4.08 m), so its blocks keep v15's resolutions and vertex counts.
- **Moss.** `TundraMoss.mat` is olive-brown (0.55, 0.50, 0.34) instead of the bright green boost (1.15, 1.25, 0.80) made for the old flat Built-in ambient; the Editor builder matches. `PolarLandscapeScatter` keeps a moss member only where the ground shader draws bare ground (its noisy cover test at q, as the pavements do), and sinks it 30% of its height. Other plants are unchanged.
- **The 22:00 flash.** Light and exposure fell about 9 stops in the last second of dusk, because they blended energies, and what follows a frame or more behind (screen-space GI history, the snow) flashed. Now the sun's lux blends in stops (`exp(lerp(ln night, ln day, day))`), and the exposure keeps the image's brightness (lux x luminance / 2^EV) on a straight line in stops between its day and night values. Dusk and dawn change at most about 0.7 stops a second over the hour. The interior's ambient EV blends the same way.
- **Dusk interiors.** `InteriorExposure` caps the ambient EV at the outdoor EV (before the daytime white-out lift), so a hut is never exposed darker than outside; full day and full night are unchanged. Between about 21:15 and 22:00 the interior had been 1-2.3 stops darker than outdoors while getting less light.
- **Stove light.** The stove lamp (400 lm, 31.83 cd) is scaled every frame by 2^min(EV - 0, 9). Its on-screen brightness is then the same at night, at dusk and in overcast daylight, about two stops below the old night interior. In clear daylight it reads up to three stops dimmer. The interior meter uses the lamp's own 31.83 cd, not the scaled value.

## Loading time: distant blocks and the window (October 16, 2026, 0.6.15-test)

Plan: [loading time](../plans/polar-loading-time.md) (decision 1A: every block reaches the same resolution before play, with the same meshes). Generation changes are in [polar world](polar-world.md#loading-time-october-16-2026-0615-test).
- **Built once, in parallel.** `BuildProxies` builds each of the 672 blocks once, at its initial resolution: 128 or 256 inside the 6/16 km rings, 32 elsewhere (64 targets still refine in the background after play). Until now every block was built at 32 and the 111 detailed ones (seed 777) again, one at a time. Workers compute blocks nearest first (`PolarLoadingParallel.Pipeline`, at most four per worker ahead of the upload); the main thread uploads them in the same order, so `far` holds them in the same order as before.
- **Scratch per computation.** `ComputeMesh` rents a scratch set from a pool for each computation (the single shared set kept it on one worker); `Pack` copies every result out of it. After loading two sets are kept.
- **Window.** While the world loads, `UpdateWindow` computes the missing near chunks in parallel up front and uploads them in its usual ring order. In play each chunk is still computed when the loop reaches it, on one worker.
- **One cut.** `BuildProxies` sets the spawn window's hole before building, so the blocks the window would recut (`UnderWindow`: under it, or the first and last columns near the angular seam) are built with it once. The other blocks are built without a hole (`computeWithoutCut`), as the old order built them before the window existed: a block that only touches the window's edge would otherwise stitch that edge more finely. `UpdateWindow` recuts distant blocks only when the hole moves (it recut the same blocks at an unchanged window too). Continue builds the terrain round the saved position (`PolarWorldController.Restore(…, at)`), not round the first hut and then again; there a block that touched both windows can keep a different edge stitch than the old two-step order gave it.
- **Loading-screen budget.** Until the world is ready, main-thread terrain work may use 12 ms of a frame instead of 2 ms, and distant pond ice is built within that budget instead of one pond per frame (about 600 frames). Play keeps the 2 ms budget and the single background worker.
- **Collection at the end.** `PolarMvpController.StartWorld` collects garbage once before the rules bind (`load.collect`, about 13 ms). Without it the first minutes after a parallel load had stops of 0.4-2 s (the weather mode's timing windows: p99 45-75 ms, one window's P95 25 ms); with it every window's P95 and p99 are 4.2 ms. In the first such run each window still had one long frame (0.4-1.8 s); in the confirming run the longest frames were 13-88 ms (0.6.14-test: at most 42 ms).
- **Diagnostics.** `-polarSerialLoading` runs every loading item on one worker in order; `-polarMeshDigest` hashes every mesh the load builds (vertices and indices) and reports the digest when the world becomes ready (`meshDigest` in the profile report).

**Measured** (seed 777; details in [polar world](polar-world.md#loading-time-october-16-2026-0615-test)): the distant build takes 63.6 s instead of 268.4 s (pond ice 0.8 s instead of 9.9 s), the window 1.7 s instead of 22.2 s, and every block settles 190 s after a new game starts instead of 694 s. The mesh digest is the same for the parallel build and the serial order.

**Tests.** The activation check "Nearby mountains retain detail and extreme distance uses coarse meshes" now asks for every block's initial 128/256 detail round the player (`MissingInitialDetail`), since a continued expedition builds its detail where the player stands. Run the HDRP suite with `--nonactivated-window` (as for 0.6.14-test): in hidden test windows the weather driver never renders, so HDRP is not running when the held rig's materials are cloned.

**Not changed, noted.** A distant block that only touches the window's edge keeps a coarse edge against the fine window, as before; building it with the hole would stitch that edge (and close a possible T-junction), but it would change meshes, so it is left for a decision.

## Habitat props, thin ice and cracks, daytime white-out (October 15, 2026)

Presentation only, HDRP only: no collider, navigation, save, rule or text change, so no twenty-seed sweep. It targets the non-landscape concept rows: huts 04, 13, 14, 16, 18; interiors 05, 15, 17; ice and water 20-23; blizzard 27. Evidence: `polar-art-habitat-ice`, 28 pairs. Before: `hb-before` (172 shots), plus `hb-before-b6` for the two re-framed fixtures. After: `hb-after` (172) and `hb-after-b6` (34, after the one-mast fix). Package `0.6.14-test` (`0.6.13-test` is left to the world-composition branch).

### Huts and Pole Station (`PolarArtHutDressing.Props.cs`, a partial of the B6 dressing)
- Interiors of intact and damaged huts, under their own LOD cut (`interiorLod` .1, about 80 m):
  - a five-board shelf unit in the back-left corner and high wall shelves where the wall stands;
  - an upper bunk over the cot, with mattress, pillow and blankets;
  - a bench and a stool at the table;
  - a chart on the table and one framed over it, two pictures by the door, mugs;
  - a hanging lantern and a table lantern, which glow while the stove burns;
  - a rug, firewood by the stove, and a kettle and pot on the stove.
  - Since 0.6.109-test the pack's huts bring their own shelves, bunk, bench and table; the code-built ones are retired and the props stand on the pack's furniture.
  Shelves hold crockery, books and open empty tins, never closed tins, fuel or sacks, so nothing reads as stock. Ruined huts get none.
- Hut2 props come from `PolarArtHabitatPropsAuthor` (`Assets/Editor`). It writes 18 render-only prefabs to `Resources/Polar/HabitatArt/Props`, with the existing HDRP copies of the Hut2 materials and no colliders, scripts or lights. The existing `Polar/Derived` furnishings (lantern, teapot, book) are reused. Rug, blanket, ticking and chart maps are procedural textures generated at runtime.
- Exteriors:
  - Chimney smoke from the flue cap while the stove burns, drifting with the displayed wind. It uses the owned particle shader on a mipped Smoke28 copy, at most 48 particles, and is primed when a frozen capture or a streamed hut first shows it. It is fainter at night.
  - Snow fillets against all four walls (about 1 m on the lee wall, at most 1.4 m) inside the 0.33 m band that the 0.35 m body radius never enters. Walkable snow keeps the 0.15 m cap; the door corridor stays clear.
  - A drift banked against a snow-blocked door: up to 2.5 m at the wall, sloping 2.6 m out, and shrinking with the clearing.
  - Boards nailed over a damaged door, removed one at a time as it is cleared. Both replace only the obstruction's renderer; its collider and interaction target are unchanged.
  - Split firewood and a snow cap against the lee wall; skis and a shovel by the door.
  - Loose and leaning boards round damaged and ruined huts, half in the snow and kept clear of resting drops.
- Pole Station: a 22 m triangular lattice radio mast 9 m behind the station, with guy wires, an aerial to the gable, an instrument screen and seven route marker poles, on the hub site with the lowest id only (one mast per station), drawn to about 5 km like the cupola. The other station huts keep smoke and weathering. Since 0.6.109-test the mast and screen are the pack's `StationMast` and `StationInstrumentBox`, at Pole Station only.
- Capture notes add `PropRenderers`, `SmokeOn`, `LanternsLit` and `RebuildMilliseconds` (`hb-after-b6`, Editor).
  - A dressing rebuild costs 2.4 ms on an intact hut, 4.7 ms on a damaged one and 8.8 ms on a ruined one.
  - The first hut built pays 183 ms once, for prop assets and procedural maps. That hut is the station, built during world load.

### Thin ice, cracks and floes
- Narrow cracks (0.25-0.8 m, no collision) were never drawn: their strip wound downwards and the crack material culls back faces. They now face up and have nine vertices across: a bright lip, a blue ice wall darkening downwards, and a black slot.
- Thin-ice caps: shore snow keeps to the outer band (threshold .64, was .46), so a small pond's weak plates read as grey glaze, and the white stress fractures show (concept 21).
- Broken plates (`PolarArtIcePondView`):
  - a jagged rim strip 8-34 cm wide (was 4-18 cm);
  - 3-5 slabs tilting into the water (was 2-3);
  - 6-14 floating shards and brash pieces (was 3-6);
  - the cut face reads as thick blue-grey ice that darkens towards the water, not a black wall.
  The hole still follows the real plate polygon and the water stays at the gameplay level (0.65 m down).
- Lead floes may grow to 1.3 x the original half-length, inside the same bank and floe gaps. They stay drawn to a third of the old distance (cull .004, was .012).

- At 14:00 the blizzard sat one stop darker than every other grade, relative to its light. `PolarArtSettings.WhiteoutLift(storm)` (1.6 EV above storm .8, smoothstep) now lowers the outdoor EV, weighted by day weight.
- Night, the 06:00/22:00 handover and interiors are unchanged, because interiors meter from `DayExposure`. This is one line in `PolarHdrpPresentation`; its build-check hash is updated.
- Mean frame value of `b5-day-blizzard`: 100 before, 177 after (`b5-day-blizzard-downwind` 114 to 190). The snowstorm grade and the night blizzard change by under 1.

Two sit behind it on a capture-only bench, one on the stool, and one on the front bench with their back to the camera; packs are off, and there are bowls, plates and a pot. The spikes then stopped completely, the last two grades ran at a constant 4.16 ms frame, and the rerun on the same build passed with the base build's spike counts. ## Series 5: world composition (October 15, 2026)

User decision: new terrain relief (world v15, see [polar world](polar-world.md#world-v15-october-15-2026-0613-test)) and the distant range on. Rendering side:
- **Knoll rock.** `PolarWorldStreamer.SurfaceColor` writes `PolarKnolls.Rock` into vertex red for v15 worlds. A knoll's top and plucked lee side get a small solid core (.86-.98, the cliff band's range, so the ground shader paints it fully) round the outcrop, and broken rocky patches (.04-.80) round that. The edge is ragged by a warped 12 m noise plus a 4.5 m noise; distant meshes (vertices over 6 m apart) keep only the 12 m term. The ground shader is unchanged: its rock carries ledge snow, so the tops read as snow-capped dark rock. Earlier worlds keep their colours.
- **Outcrops and boulders.** Each v15 knoll top carries a 3-5 m outcrop boulder set 22% deep (collision and explorer steering as any boulder), and boulders are about 2.7 times as common as in v14. Boulders are the concepts' snow-capped dark rocks; a knoll's rock patch alone read as a flat stain (`wc-after-a`).
- **Stone snow caps.** `PolarArtRock.StoneRock` ledge snow starts at n.y .48 and is full at .74 (was .60/.84), so boulders and ground stones carry thicker caps. Escarpments keep `WallRock`.
- **Low rock slabs.** `PolarArtSettings.lowRockPavements` is on: render-only slabs 0.15 m proud on rocky patches (no collider), now also on knoll rock.
- **Distant range.** `PolarArtSettings.distantRange` is on: the render-only range 5.5-15 km past the coast (`PolarArtBackdrop`), drawn only while visibility exceeds 4 km (clear air), so it costs nothing in snow.
- **Capture.** `PolarArtCapture` section `b5-land`: clear day, clear night and the four snow grades by day and night, at eye height 110-200 m from the home hut, looking back at it across the rockiest knoll top near it. The concept sheet now pairs concepts 01, 19 and 24-31 with these shots; they framed a hut wall before.

## Frame-hitch pass (October 15, 2026)

Profiled the 50-90 ms hitches of `bench-s3-01b` and the weather test, and the dawn/dusk cost, with Development Players. Presentation-side causes are fixed in code (compile-checked); the release test round is pending (see Status).

### Tools
- `PolarHdrpBenchmark -polarHdrpProfile` (Development Player only; `Tools/polar_hdrp_native_run.py --profile [--static-only]`; build with `PolarHdrpBuild -polarHdrpDevelopment`): day, 14:00, 06:30 and night static samples, the first 30 s of the real 60 s blend from clear to blizzard, and 40 s of an explorers' route. One Unity Profiler log per 12 s (`<run>/<sample>-<n>.raw`). `-polarHdrpProfileAllocations` adds managed call stacks to GC.Alloc samples (inflates timings).
- Every benchmark sample now also records `hitches` (frames over 33.3 ms), the thread split of FrameTiming rows over 30 ms, and the presenter's renderer sweep size, frames and conversions.
- `PolarHitchProfileAnalysis.Run -polarHitchProfiles <run>` (batch, Editor closed) writes `hitch-analysis.txt` (frame-time distribution, markers by excess time in hitch frames per thread, allocation paths, optional `-polarHitchFocus <marker>`) and `typical-<sample>.json` (per-marker means of ordinary frames, for comparing samples).

### Findings (captures prof-01 to prof-06 under `Logs/PolarArt/Native`; prof-02 to prof-06 overlapped other sessions' seed sweeps and Unity captures and are used for relative comparisons only)
- Hitch frames are main-thread spikes: about 60 ms main thread while the render thread (3 ms) and GPU (10 ms) stay normal.
- Autosave (gameplay, about 60% of hitch frames, never in ordinary frames): `PolarMvpController` saves whenever `State.revision` changes, at most every 0.5 s. One save of the 865 KB benchmark state costs about 50 ms of main thread (validate 6, JsonUtility 10, SHA-256 10, envelope JSON 7, write and flush 3, `File.Replace` 15; Editor timing). The 1 s NPC step is in about half of the hitch frames.
- Presenter renderer refresh, every 2 s: 9-29 ms in one LateUpdate. Most of it was `RefreshHuts`, which rebuilt all 219 hut frames from terrain height (three `VertexPosition` evaluations per site); the rest was a material array and shader-name strings per slot of about 11,000 renderers.
- Dawn and dusk (and weather blends): the render thread spends about 6 ms per frame outside the camera's passes (`RenderLoop` self time), and the main thread waits for present. Bisection: freezing the game clock removes it; holding only the presenter's hour removes it; holding only `PolarHdrpSkyArt`'s sky exposure and saturation removes it; volumetric clouds, shadows, volumetric fog and sky reflection make no difference. Cause (HDRP 17.2, engine side): `PhysicallyBasedSky.GetPrecomputationHashCode()` starts from `SkySettings.GetHashCode()`, which includes `exposure`, so every exposure change releases, re-allocates and recomputes all atmosphere tables (multiple scattering, sky view, ground irradiance, three in-scattered radiance tables). The presenter changes the sky exposure every frame while the radiance moves (twilight, weather blends), which is also the blizzard-onset render-thread rise.
- HDRP 17.2 also re-bakes the cloud layer whenever its baking hash changes, and the hash includes the layer rotation. `CloudLayerRenderer`'s texture cache stores the baked texture under height x height but looks it up under width x height, so each re-bake allocates a new texture. The presenter's slow rotation changed every frame.

### Fixes (presentation only)
- `PolarHdrpPresentation`: hut frames are cached by sector and hut until the world or its datum changes; the renderer refresh is one snapshot per period prepared in slices of about 1 ms per frame, without per-renderer arrays, with a classification cache per material and shader; converted materials are destroyed only after two sweeps without a user. Profiler markers `Polar.Presentation.Sweep/Climate/SkyArt/Celestial`.
- `PolarHdrpSkyArt`: the radiance (sky exposure) follows its target in steps of at least 1/64 EV while the target moves and lands exactly when it holds still; the cloud layer turns in .05 degree steps (about one pixel at 1080p). Steady states (14:00 clear, night, each weather grade) are unchanged.

### Background autosave (the user's decision, October 15, 2026)
The user chose to move the slow part of each autosave to a background thread; the 5 s coalescing option was not approved.
- `PolarSaveService.WriteInBackground`: validation and the JSON snapshot stay on the main thread; hashing, the envelope, the flush and `File.Replace` run on one worker. Same file, same bytes, same cadence (change saves at most every 0.5 s, the 60 s autosave).
- One publication at a time. `Write`, `Read`, `TakeBackgroundFailure` and every `Slot` change wait for a pending one first, so files land in order, a read sees the latest write, and a write never lands in another slot.
- `PolarMvpController`: only the periodic autosave publishes in the background; `SaveNow`, leaving, quitting and the expedition's end stay synchronous and first wait for (and report) a pending publication. A failed background publication counts as a failed save, so the save is owed again and the existing retry notice applies.
- Editor probe on a copy of the benchmark save: the background file is byte-identical to a synchronous write (865,073 bytes); two publications land in order; a slot change waits; main-thread cost per autosave falls from 44.9 ms to 12.2 ms (medians).
- `Tools/polar_art_verify.py` hashes original game sources, so it reports `PolarSaveService.cs` and `PolarMvpController.cs` as changed. That is this approved change; the verifier's allowance was left untouched for the art owner to update.

### Test round (release build `hitch-rel-01`, label still `0.6.12-test`; not a package)
- 1080p benchmark `bench-hitch-rel-01`: **passes all its gates for the first time**: soak complete in all three regions, peak private memory 5.64 GB, late growth 60 MB at 3.9 MB/min, real saves unchanged. Against `bench-s3-01b`:

| Sample | p99 before | p99 after | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| day-1 to day-4 (static) | 47-51 ms | 18-20 ms | hitches per 24 s from about 24 to 2-5 |
| clear 06:30 | 48 ms | 15.6 ms | p95 24.4 to 10.9 ms; main p95 17.4 to 10.2; render p95 19.0 to 3.5 |
| clear 21:30 | 52 ms | 16.2 ms | p95 17.7 to 12.5 ms |
| route-0 / route-2 / route-4 (uncapped walks) | 46 / 49 / 63 ms | 36 / 21 / 16 ms | |

- Player suite run `hitch-rel-01`: gameplay (356 checks), physical (833), physical-resume (16), continue (34), performance (59), buildings (400) and ui (359) pass; fractured, handbook and handbook-revision were not run (untouched by this change). Performance: hut-to-hut P95 16.76 ms over 17,805 frames (16.81); night sleep P95 18.11 to 16.80 ms (accelerated rest moved the sky exposure every frame). Peak private memory 6.19 GB.
- Weather: in the weather timing samples the blizzard p99 falls from 46-58 ms to 17-21 ms and its render thread from 3.9-5.0 ms to 1.2 ms. The weather mode itself failed its door check ("Animated open leaf clears actual doorway") in three of four runs of builds with the background autosave, and passed in the s3-01 control, in the build with only the presentation fixes and in one background-autosave run. Diagnosis: the leaf turns on `Time.unscaledDeltaTime` (0.35 s to open) and the check waits 0.5 s of real time. At 240 Hz with VSync on, Unity sometimes reports one refresh (4.17 ms) per frame while frames take about 6.2 ms, so the reported time sums to 0.34 s and the leaf stops at 86.7 degrees (88 needed). No background save runs before that check (the controller is paused), so the link to the change is unexplained. The check now writes a NOTE with the frame count, the reported seconds and VSync. One weather run (`hitch-dbg-02`) also had a slow outside heavy-snow sample (P95 25 ms, main thread 11.75 ms mean against about 6 ms in the other grades and builds) with no save error or other load on the machine.
- Confirmation (`hitch-rel-03` = `hitch-rel-01` plus that NOTE, same start save): weather 80/80 and weather-resume 10/10 pass; the door wait reported 0.500 s; every grade's mean is 7.2-8.9 ms with the blizzard p99 at 17-25 ms (s3-01: 46-58 ms).

### Open
- The door check's 0.15 s margin on Unity's reported frame time, and whether Unity under-reports frame time at 240 Hz with VSync more generally (game time would run slow while it does). Not changed here.
- The 1 s NPC step is in about half of the remaining hitch frames (gameplay).
- `Tools/polar_art_verify.py` flags `PolarSaveService.cs` and `PolarMvpController.cs` (the approved autosave change) until the art owner records it.

No production art change. Package `0.6.12-test` is build `Builds/HdrpMilestones/s3-01`, which passed the full suite (`suite-s3-01`). `PolarPackage.TestFolder` now points at it.

### 31-concept comparison

Run it from the final capture `s4-final` (214 shots: people, ice and water, ground, distance, snow, huts); 30 of 31 concepts are matched. Concept 17, the shared meal, has no fixture.

At dawn and dusk (06:30, 21:30) the main thread p95 rises to about 17 ms, against 10 ms at other hours. Both need a profiler pass.

## Series 3: huts, first-person equipment and people (October 15, 2026)

Installed from the staged Series 3 union (26 files, hashes checked against its manifest; b03f7a4), then the authoring tools. No collider, navigation, save, rule, map-scale or text change. Evidence: `polar-art-series3-huts` (14 pairs), `b7-first-person-equipment` (13) and `polar-art-series3-people` (12, four of them period review pairs), all from the combined 209-shot captures `s3-before` and `s3-after-a` plus `s3-period-a` (64 shots) and `s3-period-live` (79).

### Huts, Pole Station and interiors (B6)
- Seven owned habitat maps from the in-project Hut2 textures (`PolarArtB6Textures`; licence row in `docs/art/third-party-assets.md`, receipt `docs/art/hdrp-receipts/b6-habitat.json`): weathered grey exterior boards, raw-wood interior boards, a board-gap mask, frost, a cold window and a stove flipbook. Boards use a luminance pack with staggered butt joints, so walls show no repeating cross-fade band.
- `PolarArtB6HutMaterials` edits the six hut materials in place (kind 8 boards and snow, darker galvanized steel, dark glass, felt) and adds five B6 Lit materials (cast iron, icicle, stove fire, cold window, window firelight). `PolarArtB6HutUv` writes board UVs on all 30 hut meshes: 1,923 blocks and 11,538 faces, of which 3,818 face inward. A per-face interior flag is baked into UV0, so ceilings, gable faces and rafters shade as interior. Both retired in 0.6.109-test with the old hut meshes.
- `PolarArtHutDressing` (runtime, HDRP only, no colliders or lights) adds the cast-iron stove shell and round flue (the kerosene heater since 0.6.99-test), the outside frost pane, a cold inside window that glows by hour and storm, sooty stove glass when the fire is out, lee-side icicles and shelter snow in the object frame. The red cladding is gone; huts read as weathered timber with a dark frosted window that glows warm when the stove is lit (concepts 04, 05, 14, 16, 18).
- `PolarHutAssetValidation.Run` (batch, Editor closed): 2,556 checks pass.
- Any fresh Editor import runs HDRP's material validation on `Timber`, `Weathered-oxide-cladding` and `Wind-packed-snow`. It syncs `_MainTex` to the B6 board map and adds `_NORMALMAP`, which `PolarArtB6HutMaterials` had avoided. The validated form is committed so the tree stays clean; it renders the same (at most 0.45% of pixels differ between `s3-after-a` and `s4-final` hut shots). `PolarArtB6HutUv` and `PolarArtB6HutMaterials` are retired in 0.6.109-test, when the huts became the habitat pack's.

### First-person equipment (B7)
- Owned walnut, steel, ash and iron tool maps (`Tools/polar_art_tool_textures.py`, derivative crops of in-project vendor textures; hashes in `docs/art/b7-equipment/textures.json`).
- `PolarArtFirstPersonEquipmentAuthor` patches `FirstPersonRig.prefab` and its 17 clips in place (same GUIDs, names, lengths and controller) with tool-relative grips: the hand works the bolt during Bolt and Reload, and both hands stay on the harpoon shaft through Charge, Stab and Throw. Verify: 321 checks and the book arm parity check pass (`docs/art/b7-equipment/receipt.json`).
- The held handbook is drawn in the foreground pass like the weapon rig (`PolarHdrpBookMaterials`): its arms get the rig's sleeve and glove split and in-shader exposure, and the cover stays glove leather.

### People: period clothing option (review only)

It finds frame packs and rucksacks up to 13.6 cm inside the coat, against an allowed push of 4 cm, so `PolarPeopleContactAuthor` refuses before writing. No Npc prefab changes; `PolarCharacterPresentation` keeps today's carry behaviour while the contact fields are empty.
