## Series 2: land dressing, ice and water, wind and snow layers (October 14, 2026)

One tested group on top of Series 1: capture tooling (f22a853, 0837e01), snow layers (f845ef4), land and ice (8f64866), water and lead floors (a6b4cad), cold-water check (b00dce6). No collider, navigation, save, rule, map-scale or text change, so no twenty-seed sweep. Evidence: `polar-art-series2` (47 pairs from `s2-before-a`/`s2-after-b` and `s2-before-b5`/`s2-after-b5`).

### Wind and snow layers (B5)
- `PolarSnowLayers` replaces the vendor snow route under HDRP with four owned particle systems: falling flakes, blowing streaks, low ground drift (masked off ice) and gust sheets in snowstorm and blizzard. Counts scale with the weather grade (about 2,400 particles in medium snow to 9,400 in a blizzard) and respond to wind, gusts and lee shelter.
- Indoor culling uses the hut shelter volumes. Priming is teleport-safe.
- `PolarParticle.shader` follows the HDRP transparent fog contract; round flakes and soft stretched cards clip only empty fragments, which removes the dark square flake borders. The thin-ice splash moves to the transparent queue.
- The weather validation mode adds the snow-layer checks (67 to 80). `Tools/polar_b5_snow_metrics.py` measures contrast and coverage against the concepts. Tuning lives in `PolarArtSettings.snowLayers`.

### Land dressing and distance (B3)
- One charcoal rock recipe (`PolarSurface` kind 7, with lichen spots) for boulders, ground stones and all 17 cliff proxies, which used tan beach-cliff materials.
- A band-limited cliff mask in the distant mesh paints continuous rock bands instead of dashed dark parallelograms; escarpment rock modules sit over sheer faces within 400 m of the near mesh, render-only.
- The horizon seam: sky fog and volumetric ambient now follow the restored indirect light, so the ground no longer steps against the sky. A clear-air aerial-perspective path is added; the optional distant range (`distantRange`) is off by default.

### Ice, water and fissures (B4)
- Thin-ice plates render a separate fan with frost, bubbles and stress fractures driven by the actual plate stress; broken, settled and reloaded states show plates, cut faces and shards.
- Leads and ponds: dark moving water (`PolarSurface` kind 4) with wet edges and shore ice; owned floe silhouettes inside the original footprints (no stepping-stone route); layered fissure walls with snowy lips on dry cracks; water-gap walls keep rock with a wet band and rime.
- Near open water keeps less sky reflection (specular occlusion .30 near, .45 far) so it reads dark beside the bank; distant water stays at smoothness .80 or above, so its rough lobe does not reach the physically based sky's dark ground.
- Gap floors under a lead or bay (vertex `a` = 1000) shade as deep water. In an old v13 world a 3.1 km lead's uncovered 70 m-deep floor otherwise drew as a black slab across the far half of the lead.
- `PolarColdWaterCheck` (v13 fixture): 26/28 before Series 2 (lead and swimmer water read like snow); 28/28 after.

### Performance and tests
- Builds `s2-01` (all of Series 2) and `s2-03` (snow-layer cost fix). Suite `suite-s2-01`: 10 of 12 modes pass on `s2-01`; the hut-to-hut walk P95 is 16.80 ms over 16,960 frames (Series 1: 16.77 ms). Its weather mode failed the four-grade gate in the blizzard (P95 20.83 ms, gate 20 ms), so weather-resume did not run. `suite-s2-03-weather` reran gameplay, weather and weather-resume on `s2-03`, and all three pass. Blizzard P95 is 16.67 ms outside and inside the hut; every other grade is 8.3-12.5 ms. Peak private memory is 6.26 GB.
- The weather test now writes a `NOTE timing` line per measurement: mean, p99, max, frames over 16.7 ms, and main-thread, render-thread and GPU means (FrameTimingManager).
- Diagnostic run `suite-s2-03-diag` (switches removed afterwards): a blizzard frame costs about 7 ms with the snow rig cleared. Culling adds about 1-2 ms on the main thread at a hut. In the first half minute of a blizzard, the render thread rises from about 1.2 ms to about 4.5 ms.
- The fix leaves the culling result unchanged: the exact shelter test now runs only for particles within 5.6 m of a hut's volume centre, which encloses every volume of that hut. Blizzard flake and streak densities are .35 and .016, about 2,800 flakes and 1,000 streaks.
- Open (Series 4 performance pass): in a blizzard about 3% of frames are 50-90 ms hitches (p99 54-58 ms; other grades p99 12.5 ms), so the gate margin is one refresh interval.

### Capture tooling
`PolarArtCapture` adds `b3-distance`, `b3-distance-perf` (alone), `b4-ice-water` and `b5-snow`, and records any degenerate-tile collision warning (an inherited Built-in-era terrain message) as a known note for every tile.

## Series 1: player body, time of day and ground snow (October 13, 2026)

Claude continued Codex's HDRP art work from the relay. Series 1 is one tested group: capture tooling (4378d5f), light (3e0070e, a72255c), ground snow (6eb43cd), player body and guards (82b0398, c142783). Evidence: `polar-art-series1` (59 before/after cases from the 192-shot sets `s1-before-d` and `s1-after-a`).

### Time of day
- Night shows a dark sky with moonlit snow. The visible physically based sky is multiplied by 0.08 on clear nights (0.15 overcast, 0.5 blizzard, 0.25 twilight, exactly 1 by day); an IndirectLightingController restores ambient, and the moon key reaches direct light only through `lightDimmer`.
- Moon and sun keep at least 8 degrees elevation; disk and stars are compensated for the sky multiplier. Moon disk 1.6 degrees, radiance 0.1. Clear nights thin the cloud deck to density 0.1, weighted by night so clear days keep their clouds.
- Day exposure follows sun elevation (low-sun slope 0.75), so 07:00 and 20:00 are no longer underexposed brown. The 06:00/22:00 handover matches exposure-normalised light on both sides. Night grade: saturation -10 and a cool white balance.
- Measured direct-light share before the change (`s1-before-share`): 0.517 at midnight, 0.507 at noon, so `nightDirectGain` 1.083 and `nightIndirectDiffuseEquivalent` 0.342.
- Interiors: inside an intact hut footprint the exposure blends geometrically to an interior meter (dimmed ambient plus running stoves). `interiorNightOffset` 3.1 keeps a cold hut at night slightly brighter than outdoors (about -8 EV); `stoveExposureBias` -2.5 gives a readable stove-lit room. The stove light is 400 lm, colour (1, .70, .42), 0.5 m in front of the firebox; at the old stove point the stove body shadowed most of the room.

### Ground snow (B3 v2)
- Cause of the brown near snow: with GlobalSnow off under HDRP, the shader still painted rock from the tile channel (42-80% on whole flat districts) with a warm beach-cliff map.
- Fresh and packed snow colours come from measured map luminance (.78/.81/.86 and .72/.75/.80, cap .95), with macro tone, anti-tiling warp, metre ripples, sastrugi along a deflected katabatic axis, a grain normal and view-faded sparkle cells.
- Charcoal rock appears only on steep slopes and rocky tiles, with snow on ledges. An Editor-only check keeps `PolarGroundLook` (C#) equal to the shader defaults.
- Cost: the ground GBuffer pass grew from 755 to 1810 instructions; watch the performance mode.

### Player body (B7)
- A player-only copy of the Winter controller (`PlayerWinter.controller`) adds a signed `GaitDirection` speed multiplier: backward steps reverse Walk/Run. Sideways steps turn the legs up to 60 degrees while spine and chest keep the shoulders square to the first-person arms.
- Walk/Run switch by measured speed (2.6/2.3 m/s), so Walk no longer plays at triple speed. The neck is held at the reviewed Idle fit during the Walk/Run lean, which closes the running collar ring (measured neck z -0.319 m in every pose), with a 0.3 m/s cap so the feet do not slide.
- Foot IK also lands on boulders (layer 18); during the air grace it stays on only within 0.2 m of support.
- First-person sleeve and glove recipes are derived from the player's actual coat and gloves, and the held foreground (drawn after post-processing) now gets HDRP's ACES, so the flat bright-blue sleeve matches the coat.
- The body stays hidden while swimming, climbing, sleeping and dead, by design (superseded October 26-27: [player body after the character animation work](#player-body-after-the-character-animation-work-october-26-27-2026)). Reflections show only the lower body because no planar reflection probe exists; deferred.

### Guards and tools
- `PolarArtNextPlayerBuildChecks` hashes normalise CRLF to LF (raw hashes are still accepted), so a `core.autocrlf` checkout cannot break a reviewed hash. The parent adoption receipt now lives in tracked `docs/art/hdrp-receipts/`.
- `Tools/polar_art_verify.py` accepts only the exact Body44 attach line in `PolarMvpController.cs` and the foot IK mask in `PolarFootPlanting.cs`.
- `PolarArtCapture` gained `Begin`/`Start` for open-Editor runs, input freeze after every `SetPanel`, all Game-view gizmo flags off, per-section results in `complete.txt`, and the sections `player-sun-shadow`, `player-gait`, `player-slope`, `sky-time`, `b2-extra`, `b2-direct-share`, `b2-hold`, `neutral-stock` and `b3-ground`. **Polar > B2 tuning** drives a held fixture.

### Open after Series 1
- Daylight is flat: at the shadow fixture direct sun gives about 4% of the snow luminance under the default cloud deck, so shadows barely show.
- Tundra patches end in hard rectangular edges with a visible dotted tiling.
- Gloves on the held handbook are not tonemapped (`MvpHandbookBookView` draws its own camera without post-processing).
- Night window glass reads brown; huts are still red-painted boxes (B6).

## Sky group implementation, awaiting native visual review

The isolated Source now contains the paired B2 Presenter043b and strict build helper2567, adopted through CurrentStock05 after32 genuine current-production before images. The current after capture is phase hdrp-sky-group-after-39, owned Editor63500 / parent38888. Its visual outcome is pending. Original Capture43 is temporarily replaced by the identical optional grouped capture adapter; restore through CurrentStock05/after_capture_operations.py after actual native exit. Earlier operation paragraphs below are historical checkpoints.

PolarHdrpSkyArt configures the existing physical sky and volumetric-cloud profile, with a256-square linear ARGBHalf live control map. Owned ClearCoverage/OvercastCoverage assets blend from the authoritative storm value, with a change threshold rather than a per-frame texture allocation. The cloud profile LUT remains linear numeric data. A distant upper-hemisphere CloudLayer supplies restrained thin cloud structure at7000m with512 resolution and no independent distortion clock. Its rotation and the volume coverage offset read the existing simulation clock. Five native volumetric cloud wind contributions are zeroed so pause and accelerated rest do not introduce another weather clock. It creates no weather or save system.

Owned ClearNightStars and ProjectMoonSurface derive from local project formulas, with no new external license or paid asset. PolarArtCelestialSettings holds art-only sun/moon disk and star parameters. PolarArtCelestialPresentation uses the actual hour/day/storm weights, masks celestial visibility during snowfall, and provides continuous light rotation with the exact prior rotation fallback when disabled. Disk shading uses HDRP's emission/source contract and does not multiply the physical Lux to normalize the apparent moon. No aurora is introduced. Existing physical light energy, controlled exposure and nightExposure -7.5 asset remain unchanged by this initial integration; their wider night calibration is not accepted by the earlier scoped stock result.

The source and generated resources are owned, separately hashed and GUID-preserving. Runtime blend material/RenderTexture are released by the sky owner's disposal. Existing Renderer material retention and first-person ownership remain present. The paired builder pin changes only its expected Presenter hash; every original foreground and stock proof predicate remains. ## Sky, fog, snow and light: October 10, 2026 (0.6.9-test)

Plan: [playtest fixes](../plans/polar-playtest-069.md), missions P2 and P3. The user's pictures from 0.6.8-test showed a pale hill in night fog, sand-coloured ground in a snowstorm and a clear black sky in a storm.
- **Fog takes the light of the hour** (`PolarAtmosphere.Light`): day fog as before; night fog dark blue-grey, (0.035, 0.045, 0.07) on a clear night to (0.05, 0.058, 0.07) in snow, blended on the sun's dusk and dawn curve. The fog post effect (`PolarWeatherFog`) is unchanged.
- **Sky** (`PolarSky.shader`, rewritten): the horizon takes the fog colour, so distant ground fades into the sky with no edge. Snowy nights are an overcast deck, and from heavy snow up the sky is the fog colour throughout; a storm never shows a clear black sky. Clear nights (2% of nights) have stars and the moon, ported from the island skybox; the directional light becomes faint moonlight (0.07).
- **Visibility close to real snowfall** (`PolarWeatherProfiles` fog): at 5% contrast about 700 m in medium snow, 300 m in heavy snow, 120 m in a snowstorm and 40 m in a blizzard (was 216, 108, 43 and 20 m). Firelight carries as far (791, 341, 137 and 46 m). Sight, recognition and call ranges are unchanged.
- **Ambient follows the sky.** `RenderSettings.ambientMode` is Custom; the ambient probe is fitted every 0.25 s from the sky colour overhead, the fog round the horizon and snow lit by both below. Global Snow's full-screen overlay never saw probe changes (its ambient stayed at the scene's start-up value, which lit night snow a flat grey), so the same three colours also go to `_PolarAmbientSky/Equator/Ground`, read by its overlay (local vendor edit in `SnowOverlay.cginc`; original in `Logs/VendorBackups/GlobalSnow-20261010`).
- **Reflections follow the sky.** A 128 px realtime probe of the sky alone (culling mask 0, one face per frame) is re-rendered every 3 s of real time and at once when the fog colour moves noticeably; ice, water and snow reflect it. The overlay now writes occlusion 1 instead of 0, so snow receives it.
- **Storm light** is mostly sky light: the sun dims from 0.9 to 0.06 as storm rises, shadows soften (0.8 to 0.3).
- **Ambient occlusion.** Post-processing v2 MSVO on the world camera (`PolarPostProcessResources`, intensity 0.6), for contact shading at huts, boulders, cliffs and crates.
- **Snow surface.** Global Snow takes the real sun; its sparkle depends on the view (a facet glints only when it mirrors the sun to the eye); smoothness 0.2, normals 0.3, occlusion 0.4; a softer, ragged edge on steep banks (slope sharpness 0.35, noise 0.12). Rocky patches still show stair-stepped edges where the terrain mesh's per-vertex cover meets snow (remaining). Falling snow is tinted by the light (`_PolarSnowLight`), so flakes no longer glow at night.
- **Terrain shader** (`PolarTerrain.shader`): rock only where slopes are too steep to hold snow and on rocky tiles (the height band that turned every summit to rock is gone); triplanar snow and rock on steep faces; the CoastDunes cliff normal on rock; at most a tenth of dirt in snow (was 12-48%); neutral tundra tint; smoothness from the snow texture. Procedural boulders have no texture coordinates, so they get no normal map.
- **Snow cover** (`PolarLandscape.GroundCover`, every world version): bare ground only on wind-scoured crests (hills above their surroundings and sloping), slopes too steep for snow and small rocky patches; about 5-6% of the land (was 20-31%).
- **Measured** (`playtest-069` captures, v11 fixture): night snow about 30/255 under a sky of about 15; heavy-snow midday snow 185-210; medium-snow afternoon 180; snowstorm ground 160, white rather than sand. The fog was verified on snow with a test density.



Plan: [finishing v7](../plans/polar-experience-v7-finish.md), mission M3. The v7 blend faded fog density linearly over 20 s; sight is about 1/density, so it collapsed in the first 3–4 s. Snow particles were also scaled twice mid-blend, and a blizzard was one constant state with one 8 s wind loop.

- **Blend length** (`PolarWeatherTransition`): 20 s plus 10 s per weather grade changed, at most 60 s. Grades run clear 0, medium snow 1, heavy snow 2, snowstorm 3, blizzard 4; a change that starts mid-blend counts from the current mixture. The length is saved as `duration`; a save written before this change has none and blends over 20 s, as before.
- **Fog** blends on a log scale, so sight closes in steadily. Medium snow to blizzard (50 s): sight 89 m at 3 s, 82 m at 10 s, 65 m at 20 s, 43 m at 30 s, 20 m at 45 s, 18 m at the end; the old blend was at 29 m by 15 s.
- **Gusts** (`Gust`): three slow waves (14, 25 and 53 s) seeded from the expedition seed and driven by the expedition clock, shaped into gusts that build over a few seconds, so a restored save has the same gust at the same second. Medium snow has none, heavy snow some, a snowstorm and a blizzard the full range. A full gust multiplies fog by 1.45, sight by 0.7 and wind by 1.35. Cold, recognition, calls and firelight never gust; firelight reads `SteadyFog`.
- **Presentation** (`PolarAtmosphere`): snow is scaled once (emission by opacity, alpha fading in over the first 12%); fall speed follows storm strength to the power 1.6, so medium snow drifts gently; gusts raise emission and speed and swing the heading a little. Wind sound is three layered loops (16 s gusts, 11 s howl, 7 s driven snow) whose levels follow storm strength and gust.
- **Checks:** `WeatherTransitionChecks` covers the log-scale midpoint, steady visibility steps, grade durations, the 20 s old-save blend, and that gusts vary, stay in range, never touch cold or calls, and repeat for the same seed and second. Gameplay passes 2,513 shared rules plus 20,607 weather/rest and 46 keepsake checks; `CloudNpcCheck`'s blizzard call case now reads the current sight.
- **Evidence** (`docs/engineering/logs/evidence/2026-10-09/v11-weather`): twelve frames at standing height outside the seed-777 home hut, medium snow to blizzard at 0–60 s plus three settled-blizzard samples 7, 13 and 22 s later (gust 1.00, 0.00 and 0.12). The first baseline put the camera 6 m under the ground: the capture kept a camera offset left by the crack close-ups. The weather section now resets the eye to 1.62 m. The baseline was taken again from the committed weather code (`f156c9d` `PolarWeatherTransition` and `PolarAtmosphere`, with compile-only stubs for newer callers) on the same v11 world, so the pair differs only in weather.

## Mixed landscape and shared weather phase: October 7, 2026

World v10 uses a deterministic outer-latitude ground-cover mask with patch variation: snow thins into dark earth, gravel and muted moss. The terrain shader reads this mask from vertex alpha; the same field feeds a generated 2048-square Global Snow coverage texture so snow rendering and exposed ground agree. Older worlds keep zero soil-mask alpha. Region-edge continuity, local collision and distant LOD policies retain their existing contracts.

`PolarLandscapeScatter` places sparse low grasses, shrubs and bare stones within 300 m of the viewer on a seeded 14 m physical grid. It excludes hut footprints, void points, pond margins (the pond radius plus 4 m) and steep slopes; it does not impose a separate crack-bank clearance. Existing Coast Dunes grass/shrub and Ice Pebbles meshes are reused through project-owned derived assets; no vendor source is changed. Instanced draw batches use existing highest-detail meshes and shared material copies, rebuilding asynchronously when the local focus changes. The fictional mixed polar setting intentionally permits this lower-latitude vegetation; it is not a botanical simulation of continental Antarctica. These plants and stones are landscape dressing and do not add resource or collision mechanics.

`PolarWeatherTransition` persists one normalized weather mixture and shortest-arc wind heading. A target change blends with smoothstep over 20 simulation seconds, preserving the current mixture if retargeted midway. At ordinary speed that is 20 real seconds; shared 10x sleep advances it in 2 real seconds, and pause freezes it. Cold, visibility, recognition, calls, firearm accuracy and firelight read the same effective profile as fog, sky, snow particles and wind audio. `ShelterAtHeading` samples actual blended wind angles for cold protection; the old integer-direction API remains a compatible wrapper. Particle layers are retained and weighted by the shared phase. Survey presentation without gameplay state retains its own 20-second fallback.

The focused pure-state checks cover shared midpoints, pause, retarget continuity, save/restore, completion, normalized particle weights and wind wrap-around. The genuine pre-change capture remains `Logs/ProjectUpdateCapture/experience-v7/before`. The original paired weather adapter inherited the route-overview camera at approximately 250.392 m in both phases: those images demonstrate fog/sky changes, not near-player snowfall. They are preserved unchanged. The paused adapter explicitly advances the production climate at the recorded simulation times; it does not advance the gameplay clock or fabricate a baseline.

The separate `after/grounding-weather-v2` sequence uses the actual player eye at local (0, 1.62, 0), world Y=2.1600. Eight inspected weather frames cover settled MediumSnow (1), MediumSnow to Blizzard (2) at 0/0.25/1/3/10/20 simulation seconds, and settled Snowstorm (4). Projected nearby particles increase from 206 at the start to 298 at the Blizzard endpoint; the Snowstorm endpoint has 261. Fog changes from 0.008 through 0.0465 at 10 s to 0.085 at 20 s, while the shared cold factor changes from 1.4 through 1.95 to 2.5. These are actual retained vendor particle layers, explicitly simulated for the paused evidence fixture. The final raw filename `ground-weather-blizzard.png` is mislabeled: its unchanged fixture records state 4 (Snowstorm), fog 0.04 and cold 2. The accompanying `weather-state-clarification.txt` records the correction, and future adapter runs use `ground-weather-snowstorm.png`. No pixels or recorded states were relabeled as a different weather condition. Candidate-05 passes 67 packaged weather checks. Its strengthened fresh-process continuation fixture stops at textual JSON equality: the saved wind elapsed value changes by one binary64 ULP (1.776e-15 s), while the other phase fields and clock are unchanged. The later paused-climate, fog and resumed-advance assertions were not reached in candidate-05. Candidate-06 contains the corrected numerical-comparison fixture and passes weather 67/67 plus fresh-process resume 10/10, including paused climate, rendered fog, resumed advancement and dawn completion. The final twelve-mode acceptance totals 3,120 passing checks.

Visual review exposed dark strips where new rolling terrain crossed the older 150 m LOD skirts. v10 now triangulates a shared half-cell perimeter on far blocks and a shared 1/16-cell perimeter at the near-window cutout, while retaining the original 32/64/128/256 interior resolutions. Shared boundaries remain fixed during LOD morphs; a transition-local cache evaluates the actual coarse boundary fan surface, keeping the regular-grid interpolation path for all ordinary cells. v10 no longer renders those skirts. The older version path is unchanged. Pure streaming checks cover complete shared sample sets in both windings at every interior resolution (1,314 checks total). The repeated actual Unity home and route views no longer show the long black skirt strips or white/black boundary seams. This is scoped visual evidence, not an all-viewpoint proof or a frame-time result.

Newly streamed water, pond walls and pool floors register explicit GlobalSnow exclusion. A layer-mask setting alone did not add newly created objects to the deferred overlay's initial exclusion catalogue, and the first broken-ice capture showed snow painted over exposed water. `PolarExperienceCapture.WorldReview` repeated the home/route/nearest-pond fixtures and added two real outer-latitude ecology views. All seven frames in `Logs/ProjectUpdateCapture/experience-v7/after/world-v2` were inspected. Exposed water now occupies the full broken plate cells; intact snow-covered ice retains its existing appearance. The pond fixture has radius 7.763 m, 56 plates and 11 broken plates. The outer location has ground cover 1 and 342 actual seeded scatter instances; exposed soil/moss is visible, while individual plant/stone art is too small in these wide views to certify. Supplemental close-ups select existing production draw matrices without injecting specimens or changing materials. Unpaired ecology locations do not replace genuine before/after evidence.

Close-ups of the six actual seeded scenery types exposed a grounding defect in shallow pebble clusters. At the first stone specimen, the existing near-render triangle was 4.67 cm above the analytic ground sample and 1.45 cm above the entire drawn cluster. `PolarLandscapeScatter` now grounds instances on the existing 1/16-cell projected triangles and uses their plane normal. Rock sinking is 6% of the scaled mesh height, capped at 6 mm; plants retain their root sink. Candidate selection, density, type distribution and scale are unchanged. The repeated six specimens in `after/grounding-weather-v2` retain their earlier indices and logged camera poses. All six images were inspected, the total remains 342 instances, and the two stone AABBs now extend 6.00 cm and 10.66 cm above native terrain-collider support. These comparisons verify the grounding repair at the selected specimens; they do not establish botanical realism or every surface configuration. The supplemental Editor private-byte sample is 4,374,822,912 bytes, an endpoint diagnostic rather than a Player peak.

The Editor diagnostic counted 2,561,255 terrain vertices and 193,949,884 vertex/index-buffer bytes at home (193.95 MB), and 2,567,659 vertices / 194,460,224 bytes at the outer location (194.46 MB). Retained far-grid data were 15,433,344 and 15,508,608 bytes respectively. Four/eight distant blocks were still pending at those samples, so these are neither settled-state nor peak measurements. The original managed private-memory API returned zero on this Unity runtime: that metric is unavailable, not a zero-memory result. Later adapters use `PolarProcessMemory.PrivateBytes()` instead. Candidate-04 passes its 23 legacy packaged performance checks, with maximum capped P95 16.798 ms and an independently monitored 4.140 GB process peak. Most of those images face hut interiors; they do not establish the new outdoor scenery/traversal scope. The newer outdoor measurements below extend that legacy scope; final [package acceptance](../logs/evidence/2026-10-07/polar-experience-v7/acceptance.md) also covers all other Player modes.

The packaged acceptance adapter adds an outdoor ecology traversal to the existing indoor/station weather benchmarks. It selects a real supported 100 m segment near the inspected seeded outer patch, checks the loaded near-mesh collider and normal standing eye, and walks out and back using ordinary player input. Blizzard (2) and Snowstorm (4) each settle for 20 simulation seconds before at least 720 moving frames are sampled. Each leg retains P95 <= 20 ms, and the independent native process monitor retains the 5 GB memory gate. The report records feet/eye positions, support height, projected seeded instances and snow particles, distance and frame counts, plus five original outdoor images. Route selection never injects scenery or modifies terrain, timed movement never teleports, and the existing benchmark cases remain unchanged.

**Latest scoped result: candidate-06, October 8.** Performance passes 52/52 with exit 0 in 556.8 seconds. Blizzard walks 99.6766 m over 1,622 moving frames at P95 16.6716 ms; Snowstorm returns 99.5183 m over 1,586 moving frames at P95 16.7016 ms, totalling 199.1949 m. Every moving frame passes actual terrain-support and normal-eye checks; recorded endpoint eyes are 1.6500-1.6515 m above native support with a 1.62 m local offset. The outward route rises 5.1523 m. Maximum P95 across all ten reported cases is 16.7603 ms (night sleep). External monitored peak private memory is 4,147,851,264 bytes (4.148 GB), distinct from the 3,932,164,096-byte in-process endpoint diagnostic. These serial samples use 1600x900 at a 60 FPS cap on an RTX 4070 Laptop GPU/Core i7-13700H; they are not broader hardware or whole-world guarantees.

All five original candidate-06 outdoor PNGs were individually inspected: medium-snow route and Blizzard/Snowstorm start/finish. They show actual textured mixed ground, a gentle rise, sparse grasses/shrubs/stones and blowing snow, with no observed blank/black/magenta frame, obvious ground clipping or visible terrain seam. Dense Blizzard fog obscures distant scenery. Nearby instance counts are 342/342/383/383/344, projected scenery counts 14/14/3/15/9 and projected snow counts 166/249/222/412/370; projection alone does not prove visibility through fog. The [readable inspection report](../../../Logs/ProjectUpdateCapture/experience-v7/inspection/candidate06-outdoor-performance-review.md) and [exact JSON receipt](../../../Logs/ProjectUpdateCapture/experience-v7/inspection/candidate06-outdoor-performance-review.json) retain paths, image hashes, camera/support telemetry and limitations. The frozen source-manifest fingerprint is d7d63a664357f6ca7efbca4628b9efb42467e8d9d83fe9ea0eabaf7ca8611caa, and the independently hashed game assembly matches the build summary: B628260822EF2A11EE259DEE3ECB8C0087A80B59FC9D247666D00BEA6805E3F8. The source tree was not rehashed during serial performance measurements; a full post-test source verification subsequently passed.

## Six-minute hut spacing: October 7, 2026

New expeditions are about twice as large ([world](polar-world.md#six-minute-hut-spacing-october-7-2026)). Streaming rules are unchanged: the 6/16/28 km resolution rings and the 672 distant blocks stay, and blocks simply cover twice the metres. Pole Station is about 120 km from home in seed 777, so the gameplay test's station visit loads a different mix of block resolutions; package memory is re-measured on 0.5.19-test. The Southern Ocean plane now reaches 1,280 lattice units from the world centre at the new spacing, past the coast and floes (about 130-170 km out); older worlds keep their 160 km square.

## Obsolete far detail released first: October 6, 2026

After a long move, the background now releases distant detail that is no longer needed before building new detail (user request, the gameplay Pole Station memory peak). Evidence: [terrain-overdue-release](../logs/evidence/2026-10-06/terrain-overdue-release/).
- **Before.** `PolarWorldStreamer.Update` replaced one block at a time, always the closest one whose resolution should change. After a relocation, such as the gameplay test's return from Pole Station, every block near the new position came before any of the hundreds of obsolete blocks at the old one, so both sets stayed in memory together for about 100 s. Each obsolete block was also coarsened with the 0.35 s morph, which first rebuilds the block at its old, finer resolution.
- **Now.** A block more than 1.5 times beyond its resolution's distance threshold (`PolarStreamingPolicy.Overdue`; walking leaves blocks within the 1.1 hysteresis band) is coarsened first, farthest first. It goes through `proxies.background_release`, which skips the morph. Everything else keeps the closest-first order and its morph.

Verification (`Assets/Editor/PolarTerrainRoundTripCapture.cs`, a new adapter that drives the production background selection over a Pole Station round trip):
- **Terrain peaks.** Settling at the pole, terrain buffers peak at 371 MB (was 436 MB). Settling back home, they peak at 387 MB (was 454 MB), and the return settles in 23 s instead of 105 s. Editor private memory peaks at 4.71 GB (was 5.00 GB).
- **Settled state.** Unchanged: 367 MB at the pole and 196 MB at home, with the same block counts.
- **Views.** Taken at the same game time, the settled pole and home views are pixel-identical before and after. A first pair taken without pinning the game clock differs only in lighting, because the two runs spent different times settling.
- **Limitation.** Not captured: for a moment after a long jump, far obsolete blocks change without the morph.
- **Packages.**
  - On 0.5.16-test the gameplay test peaked at 3.81 GB, but mostly because it moved on from Pole Station sooner. Its relocation helper waits for the streamer to go idle, and released blocks leave idle gaps sooner, so the pole's detail was never built.
  - The test now waits, paused, until terrain settles at Pole Station, the run's worst case. On 0.5.17-test the gameplay peak is 3.96 GB, against 4.11 GB for the same scenario on 0.5.15-test. All 1,827 offline checks pass with P95s of 16.67–16.78 ms.

## Distant detail capped by block width: October 6, 2026

To regain memory headroom (user request), each distant block's resolution is now capped by its real width (`PolarWorldStreamer.SpacingMaximum`, folded into `MaximumDistantResolution` with the `PolarCentralPlane` bound). The resolution stops where the block's ring-wise vertex spacing would fall below 5 m. Evidence: [terrain-inner-detail-cap](../logs/evidence/2026-10-06/terrain-inner-detail-cap/).

- **Why.** Distant blocks are 128 lattice cells square. On the inner disc around the pole they are narrow wedges, about 165–660 m across at their outer edge. At resolution 256 their vertices sat 0.6–2.6 m apart around the ring, against about 6 m at the outer ring's best. Standing at Pole Station put about a hundred of them at 256.
- **Effect.** Inner-disc rows are capped at 32, 64, 64 and 128 from the pole outward. Outer-ring blocks, over 1.5 km across, keep 256. Radial spacing stays at most about 69 m, close to the outer ring's 60 m.
- **Where the peak came from.** The built-game gameplay test's "5 km away and back" scene picks the first site more than 5 km from home, which is Pole Station. Its memory stages show terrain buffers rising from about 190–280 MB to 877 MB there (blocks at 256/128/64: 84/168/252). Those blocks are then coarsened one at a time after returning home, which is the transient behind the 4.83 GB gameplay peak.

Verification (`Assets/Editor/PolarCentralMemoryCapture.cs`, the October 3 recipe, with an output-folder argument and terrain figures added):
- **Memory.** Settled at the pole, terrain buffers fall from 950 MB to 387 MB; blocks at 256/128/64/32 go from 102/150/252/168 to 0/137/267/268. At the birth hut nothing changes: 196 MB and 9/10/30/623.
- **Pole views.** The three Pole Station views differ by 446–457 pixels of 1,440,000, along the mountain silhouettes; the shoulder and pass keep their shape. The four plaza-block transition shots differ by 430 pixels.
- **Outer views.** The six near and outer-ring LOD views are pixel-identical.
- **Package 0.5.13-test.** All 1,827 offline built-game checks pass, with capped frame-time P95s of 16.68–16.76 ms. The gameplay memory peak falls from 4.83 GB to 4.39 GB, and terrain buffers after the Pole Station trip from 877 MB to 387 MB. The performance mode, which also visits Pole Station, peaks at 3.98 GB (was 4.57 GB).

## Vendor Snow VFX and indoor clipping: October 4, 2026

The active emitter is now the Hovl Built-in NoGround medium/heavy/snowstorm/blizzard preset selected by the visual catalogue. Vendor originals are preserved; runtime material instances retain their textures, tint, blending/fading and particle layers through the project-owned shelter shader. `PolarStandardParticles.cginc` derives from the installed Unity6000.2.15f1 standard particle include, adding a world-position interpolator initialized in the unlit vertex function. Fragment clipping removes only authored indoor volumes. The elevated snowfall source is adapted to12m in the local moving weather window; wind direction and natural lee modulate vendor velocity/emission. Sky/fog/wind audio remain owned by `PolarAtmosphere`, which crossfades over1second without duplicate procedural snowfall. Stream changes refresh the nearby shelter volumes.

The local shelter vectors also encode the pitched roof underside, shared by particle rejection and fragment clipping. The four preset roots stay active outside while the player is indoors. Instances are pooled per weather type, and particle buffers are retained rather than allocated each frame. The world/terrain generation and cache version remain v9; this update does not alter terrain geometry or streaming/LOD policy.

## v9 fractured geometry

New surface existence comes from world gap polygons, independently of terrain LOD. `PolarWorldStreamer` subtracts the same convex decomposition from near and distant top triangles in lattice coordinates, then projects the surviving geometry. Bank edges insert the fixed two-cell samples used by explicit walls, so coarse tops cannot bridge or straighten a narrow contour that fine geometry exposes. Internal gaps have seventy-metre walls and explicit bottoms; coastal gaps meet sea level. Pit walls have their own geometry and are not subjected to the old smooth mountain profile test. Skirts skip gap edges. Thin-ice cuts remain a separate physical polygon operation.

After clipping, the worker removes numerical faces under 5 mm across (below 0.005 square metres in three-dimensional area until 0.6.74-test, which also dropped small whole triangles at gap lips). At the world's large float coordinates, nearly collinear polygon differences can otherwise round to centimetre-wide fragments inside a gap. The reproduced hole-bank fixture had nine invalid top centroids among 362,501 upward top faces; cleanup retains 362,415 and zero invalid centroids. No gap, ridge, height recipe or navigation bit changes. Explicit walls/bottoms are identified separately from terrain top faces during actual Player validation.

`TryGroundAt` distinguishes missing surface instead of returning the pit floor as walking support. `CanMoveTerrain` checks the complete projected capsule segment against gap banks, then samples terrain slope and navigation permissions; it is shared by Player and NPC movement, including unloaded NPCs. The bank check handles sub-sample gaps and circular clearance around tips. A hut footprint cannot override missing surface. Normal movement never enters pit floors. Climbing, fall damage, caves and collapse are outside this implementation.

Terrain shelter uses authoritative world heights and physical upwind distances rather than loaded mesh colliders. Seeded wind headings drive storm particle velocity. Terrain lee attenuates storm flake/drift emission and wind volume; the English environmental HUD exposes the state. Building roof feedback remains independently computed. Final paired images and actual Player ridge/pass/gap/lee views were inspected. All twenty geometry/route reports and the serial performance gates pass. Fresh startup is 140.573s versus 166.635s for the hardware/quality-matched 0.3.2 sample (0.844 ratio). Highest sampled active P95 is 16.727ms; highest independently observed Windows lifetime Player private commit is 4.731GB. The 25% / 20ms / 5GB gates pass. These are the documented serial fixture samples, not a universal hardware or cold-disk guarantee. See [acceptance](../logs/evidence/2026-10-01/polar-fractured-world/acceptance.md).

The polar prototype uses Built-in Gamma rendering, Global Snow relief mapping, physical radial terrain and project-owned hut variants. D3D11 is the accepted Windows backend; a D3D12 retrial on November 7, 2026 failed again (see the section of that date). Near terrain, buildings and terrain collision retain their existing local-window budgets. Thin ice uses physical polygon cuts, unique water IDs and state-preserving near/distant views. Water stays excluded from snow.

## Progressive distant terrain, October 1, 2026

The approved 0.3.2 change first covers all 672 distant blocks with resolution32, sorted nearest first. A single worker computes batches of8; main-thread mesh upload yields after a 2ms budget. Distance to nine-sample projected block bounds selects resolution256/128/64 within6/16/28km, otherwise32. All128/256 target blocks and the near collision window are prepared before playable state. Other targets refine one block at a time in the background, keeping the old mesh visible until the replacement is ready.

Fine-window movement takes priority and recuts only affected far holes, retaining their existing resolution. Angular first/last chunks wrap; invalid initial cut coordinates no longer manufacture old-wrap work. One worker avoids contention in the shared decoded-height LRU (128 blocks then; every block of a world, 672, since 0.6.132-test). Replacement and Unity object disposal stay on the main thread; regeneration waits for the old task and discards obsolete background upload. Initial replacements defer snow refresh until final preparation; background replacements refresh coverage after upload.

Fine resolution, shared height/normal/color sampling, thin-ice subtraction, crack bottoms and skirts remain the same. At that historical milestone, geometric morphing was not implemented and distance transitions could still change distant approximation. Six frozen Mono512 fixture comparisons (four clear directions, blizzard and planner) have been inspected; visible spawn terrain is stable. This is limited fixture coverage, not every viewpoint or seed.

Actual loading, background completion, active-gameplay performance and release status are tracked by the [world system](polar-world.md), [active plan](../plans/polar-progressive-loading.md) and [evidence](../logs/evidence/2026-10-01/polar-progressive-loading/loading-analysis.md). Twenty map screenshots use the real generator and diagnostic survey canvas, without streaming 3D terrain for each map-only view.

Synchronous Editor generation completes nested distant-ice and initial-detail routines before declaring Ready. Radial and thin-ice diagnostics check the configured candidate count instead of assuming 512. Historical explicitly configured 512 fixtures remain supported.

For hidden Windows acceptance, launch windowed at 1366x768 and let the probe explicitly resize to 1600x900. On this machine, launching directly at the target size produced black ordinary world captures; batch mode did not fix it. The different initial size restored real world rendering. Player screenshots use absolute paths because Unity resolves relative screenshot names inside the Player data directory. Do not count black frames as visual or GPU acceptance.

## Continuous terrain streaming, October 3, 2026

`PolarStreamingPolicy` keeps the initial 6/16/28km rings and adds ten-percent distance hysteresis to later upgrades and downgrades. A block retains its current resolution inside the hysteresis band. Both directions use the finer topology for a 0.35-second height/normal transition; at most two background transitions remain active. Coarse heights are evaluated on the actual projected triangle plane, with fixed horizontal coordinates. Interpolating projected horizontal positions can separate adjacent curved block boundaries. Gap-bank cells, explicit pit walls/floors, pond edges and local collision geometry remain fixed. Historical v8 void cells receive the same feature lock without creating a v9 shape or changing their height recipe.

Worker-produced buffers include interleaved positions, normals, tangents and colors in the original 56-byte vertex layout. Only transient morph display meshes add height/normal deltas in an 80-byte layout. Both directions hold an inactive base-layout endpoint and retire the morph display when settled, so ordinary static terrain does not retain unused channels. Mesh bounds include both endpoints. Main-thread vertex/index uploads use 2,048/8,192-element packets; a replacement remains inactive until its property block and visible ownership can switch together. Distant meshes release their Unity CPU copy after upload, retaining only the coarse sample grids required for a later transition. Fine meshes remain readable for physics and seam diagnostics. The old visible mesh stays present throughout computation/upload. All native terrain meshes, including a partially uploaded mesh, are registered for destruction.

The serialized worker reuses scratch vertex/normal/color/tangent/index/lattice lists. Packed results and coarse position grids own independent arrays, so a later computation cannot overwrite an in-flight upload. Gap and pond clipping allocate polygon lists only after an overlap is found; coarse grids copy directly, while fine collision chunks omit unused morph grids. Coarse normals are deterministically recreated on the worker only for an active transition. Distant ice/water release their CPU mesh copies and rebuild from authoritative pond records; local collision remains readable. Upload and retirement release large result/grid references explicitly. An earlier seed-12345 run passed its function/frame checks but exceeded the memory gate at 5.252GB; all failed package/run attempts are preserved, and the complete final acceptance is rerun on the optimized package.

Polygon clipping emits repeated suffix vertices. Worker compaction merges only bit-identical position/normal/tangent/color tuples, including signed-zero distinctions. It preserves the leading sample grid and keeps suffix vertices separate from that prefix because morphing derives their coordinates differently. Every face remains in its original order, with remapped indices. A frozen seven-case audit compares SHA256 over the ordered triangle attributes, including all morph deltas, before/after compaction; static, morph, wrap, gap and hole cases match exactly. Some clipped vertex counts fall by 46%. Repeating the unchanged six-camera adapter after static-layout and compaction changes yields byte-identical images. Terrain sampling, clipping arithmetic, face attributes, bounds, shape and navigation remain unchanged.

The installed Global Snow forward replacement and zenithal depth shaders share `_PolarLodBlend` and the same vertex displacement. `_PolarLodActive` defaults to zero in replacement shaders, so other objects keep their original vertex behavior. The terrain material and transition property block opt in. This narrow vendor integration must be retained when upgrading Global Snow. A real midpoint capture exposed a false blue gap when snow rendered the final vertex height while terrain rendered the intermediate height; the shared snow/depth deformation fixes that reproduced artifact. Six unchanged-adapter, fixed-recipe comparisons verify clear/storm near views, refinement/coarsening midpoints and settled endpoints. Near frames are identical; settled endpoint channel differences do not exceed one unit.

`PolarStreamingBudget` accounts for measured main-thread work across a frame, including uploads, object units, collision binding/enabling, retirement, proxy visibility and snow-refresh requests. Coroutines yield after the 2ms scheduling budget is consumed. Indivisible Unity calls can overshoot it; diagnostics report maximum individual operations and aggregate work frames, with a bounded 2,048-frame history. This is not a hard real-time guarantee. Native terrain collision baking runs on the single worker; collider binding/activation remains on the main thread. Required collision neighbors are prepared before old windows retire. Movement cancels optional distant computation and takes priority; regeneration discards obsolete results and destruction waits for a running bake before freeing its mesh. Snow-refresh requests coalesce. Pond plates are built in stages and keep broken state across near/distant retirement.

`PolarStreamingValidation` is opt-in via `-polarStreaming`. It uses a non-Development D3D11 Player, a production scene, isolated fixture/save output and normal capsule movement. Both baseline and candidate fixtures dispose the NPC director before measured walking to isolate deterministic terrain streaming; production renderer/buildings and the 21-person/324-site state remain. Full NPC-active performance belongs to the separately coordinated v5 acceptance. Clear/storm walking frames include window handover and are sampled separately from placement/screenshots. Physical ray support and loaded collision are checked each walking frame; a transient movement-state enum during gravity settlement is not treated as loss of physical support. Extended coverage exercises both LOD directions, angular wrap, a true surface hole and broken-ice retirement/reload. The original twenty archived gallery recipes are replayed without screening. The separate `0.5.1-streaming` package preserves the accepted 0.4.0 release and does not certify the parallel v5 gameplay/NPC milestone.

Historical v8/Mono512 uses a separate terrain/motor diagnostic package with identical production streaming inputs. Its opt-in adapter samples original traversal flags and slopes along the chosen route and excludes thin ice from normal ground walks. Gameplay Update and saving are disabled because the current v5 validator does not accept a v2 expedition. The first generic legacy fixture selected a non-walkable cell and attempted incompatible saving; its failure is preserved. This correction changes diagnostic setup, not terrain, navigation flags or old-save rules. Exact candidate and legacy adapters and independent source/package seals are retained in the evidence.

All twenty original v9 recipes pass 17,341 actual Player assertions, with another 1,078 on historical v8/Mono512. Highest moving P95 is 16.801ms, maximum moving frame 61.473ms and Windows lifetime private commit 4.264GB. Matched seed-777 cached renderer/collision preparation is 26.382s versus 27.677s (0.953 ratio). The 25% / 20ms / 5GB gates pass on the recorded hardware; preparation excludes archive replay and fresh WFC generation. The [completion plan](../plans/polar-streaming-finish.md) is complete for this terrain-streaming scope. [Serial results and limitations](../logs/evidence/2026-10-03/polar-streaming-finish/acceptance.md). The [evidence record](../logs/evidence/2026-10-03/polar-streaming-finish/manifest.json) contains the genuine paired visuals; the source/package seal and serial results are kept alongside it. Startup comparisons for this milestone measure frozen cached-recipe renderer/collision preparation, excluding archive replay and fresh WFC generation. Do not reinterpret them as fresh-generation timing or exploration-scale calibration.

## Central planar memory amendment for NPC acceptance (completed)

The full NPC-active six-region Player run exposes a 5.911GB lifetime private-commit failure absent from the separately sealed terrain-only idle setup. Pause does not disable production rendering; a fixture waiting for `IsUpdating` can allow the old central focus to settle hundreds of distant blocks before relocation. The independent two-region diagnostic records 252 resolution256 blocks, 1.733GB of terrain buffers and 5.604GB private commit at the next local window. Forced diagnostic collection does not reduce it. Normal new-focus settlement reduces terrain buffers to 185MB and private commit to 3.832GB. The original failed run and diagnostic remain preserved; collection and idle LOD gating are not acceptance remedies.

`PolarCentralPlane` limits only complete hub blocks in distant radial rows0/1 to resolution64. They end at lattice z256, before the hub's constant40m plaza starts its shoulder at z320. Any same-sector pond (including shore blending), overlapping gap, invalid height or non-flat sampled gradient retains the original maximum256. The renderer caches this bound per world and applies it to initial detail targets, hysteresis targets, pending counts and normal background selection. Distance can still choose32. Row2 includes a shoulder and is excluded. Other mountains, feature geometry, projected sampling, skirts, color, snow/depth morphs, the near128 mesh and all collision/navigation remain unchanged.

Thirteen actual fixed-recipe before captures precede this production edit: central clear/storm/edge, central refinement/coarsening midpoints/endpoints, normal outer clear/storm and mountain refinement/coarsening midpoints/endpoints. The unchanged adapter produced all thirteen after captures with a byte-identical fixture. Every generated comparison was inspected: the planar surface, mountains, snow shading and joins remain stable, without observed new cracks or missing terrain. Small rasterization differences remain recorded in the metrics. [Paired evidence](../logs/evidence/2026-10-03/polar-central-floor-memory/manifest.json).

Original twenty-archive geometry proof uses the actual Unity Mono reader and passes 2,190,821 assertions, with 168 eligible blocks per recipe. Fixed projected coarse-cell samples include the pole and radial/angular edges; maximum sampled horizontal float error is 0.01953125m and plane-height error is zero. This is sampled numerical evidence, not a claim that every vertex was tested. The separate .NET replay identity failure remains diagnostic. The exact ten production/check source files and result SHA are bound in the NPC evidence collector. Complete NPC-active regression and the actual normal-speed cloud hour pass on preserved measured source `6baced4b9aad3a0c4137c899acd6055043155080a02ecd353314b2574f9ccac4`; highest native lifetime private commit is 4.616GB and highest regression P95 is 16.715ms. The final player-only journal/load amendment retains byte-identical rendering and NPC files and reruns gameplay/continuation separately. [NPC measurements and exact reuse binding](../logs/evidence/2026-10-03/polar-npc-gdd-v5/acceptance.md). The terrain-only sealed package and its historical acceptance remain unchanged.
